# Changelog

This project follows [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/). First public release will be tagged `0.1.0`.

All notable changes are documented here. Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

---

## [Unreleased]

### Security

- **Reviewer gate — workspace-boundary check now covers failure-verdict evidence paths.** `_check_behavioral_verification` / `_check_regression_verification` validated evidence-path safety only on a `pass` verdict (they early-return on `fail`/`cannot_verify`), but the failure-verdict synthesisers (`_synthesize_behavioral_blocking_issues`, `_synthesize_regression_blocking_issue`) copied `file_or_screenshot_or_log` verbatim into `blocking_issues[].affected_file` — so a traversal-shaped evidence path on a failure verdict reached `failure_context.json` unchecked. The synthesisers now run the same `os.path.realpath` + `os.path.commonpath` boundary check and **blank** an escaping `affected_file` (the blocking issue's `description` is preserved; a safe-but-absent path is kept, since a failed phase may produce no artifact). Defense-in-depth: the reviewer is a trusted in-session agent and the value never reaches a filesystem syscall, so this closes a consistency gap, not an active exploit. The previously-inlined boundary check was extracted into a shared `utils.path_escapes_workspace()`, and the three `reviewer_gate.py` contract validators were converged onto it (no behavior change, pinned by existing escape/symlink/existence tests). Tests: `autodev/tests/test_gate_path_safety.py` (helper contract), plus non-pass traversal cases in `test_reviewer_gate_synthesize_blocking_issues.py` and `test_reviewer_gate_regression_prior_phase.py`.

### Added

- **Provider-agnostic escalation comms + inbound operator-reply path.** The escalation agent now notifies — and can be answered — over whatever channel the operator bound in OpenClaw, both outbound and inbound. No hardcoded `signal` literal; an operator away from the dashboard can recover an escalation by replying on the channel.
  - **Part A — outbound is provider-agnostic.** New `Orchestrator._resolve_notification_channel()` resolves the raw-notification channel in order: explicit `notification_channel` config key → the OpenClaw binding whose `agentId=="escalation"` (its `match.channel`) → `None`. The two raw-notification fallbacks (the reset-cap notices and the escalation-webhook-failed failsafe) now share one `_post_raw_notification()` (channel-resolved, `timeout=15`, returns a delivery bool); `send_signal_notification` → `send_raw_notification`. An *unresolvable* channel now halts `HALTED_SILENT` (with an `escalation_failed` breadcrumb) instead of POSTing to a guessed "signal". Channel-named prose genericized in `webhook_client.py`. Tests: `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_webhook_request_error.py` (resolver tiers + no-POST-on-None), `test_escalation_cap.py` (renamed).
  - **Part B1 — correlation token.** Each escalation episode mints `{entry_id}.{nonce}` (`_prepare_escalation_reply_token`), persists it to the escalated project's `phase_state.json`, and threads it into the agent webhook message (`_build_escalation_webhook_message(reply_token=…)`) so the agent echoes it and the operator starts their reply with it. Generated while the queue entry is still ACTIVE and the symlink is local, so it binds to the project that escalated. Tests: `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_escalation_reply_token.py`.
  - **Part B2 — `POST /api/escalation/inbound`** (`ui/server.py`): the only endpoint exempt from the UI-token middleware; it authenticates with the **hooks** Bearer secret (`secrets.compare_digest`, fail-closed → 503/401). Maps an operator reply → command verb (RETRY/PROCEED/STOP/RESET_*; SKIP/NUCLEAR_RESET only on an explicit request; unrecognized/ambiguous → clarify, never a defaulted command), resolves it to the **escalated** project by correlation token (single-parked fallback; ≥2 candidates → 409; stale → 404; re-checks answerability at write time), and writes through the SAME `_write_escalation_files` / `_write_pending_escalation_files` the dashboard uses — now stamped `source:"inbound"` (defaulted param; existing callers unchanged). **Project-boundedness (B0):** the answer always lands in the escalated project's own directory by absolute path and is applied only after that project's revival repoints the symlink — never the project active when the reply arrives. Best-effort channel-neutral ack. Tests: `tests/test_api_escalation_inbound.py` (auth, routing, verb map, write-path, caps).
  - **Part B3 — OpenClaw inbound hook → endpoint.** The `autodev-pipeline-signals` plugin gains an `inbound_claim` subscriber (`src/inbound-escalation.ts`) that forwards an escalation-channel reply to `/api/escalation/inbound` and claims it on a 2xx (suppressing the dead-end agent session); fail-safe (routes normally) on any non-2xx/transport error. Opt-in via `AUTODEV_ESCALATION_CHANNEL` + `AUTODEV_HOOKS_TOKEN` (+ optional `AUTODEV_UI_URL`); no-op + zero behavior change when unset. The hand-maintained `openclaw-types.d.ts` stub gained the `inbound_claim` types. Tests: `autodev/plugin/tests/inbound-escalation.test.ts` (9); `npm run typecheck`/`build` clean. Operator guide in `SETUP.md`. Takes effect only after a plugin rebuild + gateway restart (deploy-time).
  - **Part B4 — escalation agent docs reversed (dual-source).** `autodev/agents/escalation/{AGENTS,IDENTITY,USER,TOOLS}.md` + the escalation-summary skill (and the byte-identical `~/.openclaw/workspace-escalation/` copies): the F13 "dashboard is the only reply path" wording now reads "dashboard **or** reply on the configured channel; the Lullabeast server writes the command either way" — while keeping "the agent never writes a command file." `## Signal Message Format` → `## Operator Notification Format` (+ a token-echo instruction); channel-named prose genericized. Also corrected a stale pre-F13 claim in `TOOLS.md` that the agent writes `escalation_output.json` (it writes only the `escalation_summary.json` advisory).

- **Observability P2-B — Pipeline-screen live telemetry rendering (surface; the final observability phase before MVP).** UI-only (`ui/index.html`), all additive; JSX validated through the bundled Babel standalone.
  - **C1 — live in-progress-phase spend on the Monitor strip.** The roadmap header strip gains a "+ this phase $X · Yk" suffix from `/api/state.current_phase_tokens`, shown alongside the completed-phase totals (additive — the live phase is not yet a metrics row, so no double-count; it clears when the phase lands). The strip's render gate was relaxed so it also shows during the first phase (live spend before any phase completes).
  - **C2 — agent-liveness pulse** in `CurrentPhasePanel`: a pulsing green dot + "active Ns ago" from `agent_activity_age_seconds` when the activity stamp is fresh, ambering as the silence approaches the ~300s stall threshold; omitted when idle / no stamp.
  - **C3 — last-attempt line** in `CurrentPhasePanel`: the dense `last_attempt_summary` (with `last_poll_reason` in parens), the poller's verdict surfaced verbatim.
  - **C5 — per-phase pain-signal chips.** Collapsed roadmap rows show compact ⚠N / ↻N / ⛔ chips (demoted gate warnings / >1 executor attempt / escalated) from the per-phase signals P2-A passes through; the expanded Run Metrics block lists the warning codes + reachability summary.
  - **`operator_action` feed rendering.** The P1-C operator events are now registered in all four activity-feed registries (`getEventBadgeColor` periwinkle lifecycle, `EVENT_TYPE_DISPLAY` "Operator", `EVENT_TYPE_DESCRIPTION`, and a `humanizeSummary` case keyed off `detail.action`) — they render with a label/colour/tooltip/prose instead of the generic fallback.
  - Tests: `tests/test_ui_p2b_live_telemetry.py` (12 contracts). Completes the Observability Phase 1+2 MVP cut; P1-D (survivorship/heartbeat) and P2-C (agent-output one-liner) remain descoped.

- **Observability P2-A — live state exposure on `/api/state` + metrics-summary pain-signal passthrough (surface; closes integrity findings 3 & 6).**
  - `GET /api/state` now surfaces the Section-6.4 phase-state outcome fields — `last_poll_reason`, `last_attempt_summary`, `last_phase_outcome`, `last_gate_warnings` (present-only, mirroring the escalation view's "absent when unset") — and the in-progress phase's spend `current_phase_tokens` (the three live `{role}_tokens_acc` dicts + a summed `{total_tokens, cost_total}`, present only when an accumulator exists). The orchestrator wrote these but the server read none of them (finding 6).
  - New always-present `agent_activity_age_seconds` (null when idle / no current agent / no stamp): `now - mtime` of the active agent's `{agent}_activity.stamp` — the orchestrator-written **sibling of `phase_state.json`** in `.autodev/pipeline/` (NOT `workspace-{agent}/`, a roadmap-path correction) — via the new `ui/server._agent_activity_age_seconds`. This is the agent-liveness proxy the Pipeline screen's pulse (P2-B) keys off.
  - `GET /api/metrics-summary` per-phase rows now pass through the canonical row's pain signals — `escalation_resets` / `nuclear_resets` / `reviewer_unverified_retries` (counters, default 0) and `gate_warnings` / `reachability_summary` (compact dicts, default null) — which the writer persisted for analysis but the reader dropped (finding 3). Feeds the P2-B roadmap ⚠/↻/⛔ chips.
  - All additive + present-only/default-stable; the `current_phase_tokens` sums reuse the hardened `_role_token_total` / `_role_cost` helpers so a malformed phase_state can't 500 the 5s-polled `/api/state`. Tests: `tests/test_api_state_live_fields.py`, `tests/test_api_state_activity_stamp_age.py`, `tests/test_api_metrics_summary_pain_signals.py`.

- **Observability P1-C — server-side `operator_action` events (capture; closes Q4 — where humans intervene).**
  - Every **successful** operator mutation now emits a durable `operator_action` pipeline event, so "interventions by type per week" is a `jq 'select(.event=="operator_action") | .detail.action'` away. Previously only an orchestrator-*consumed* escalation answer left a trace (`escalation_resolve`); a banked-then-superseded command, and every queue edit / stop / resume / git-recover / launch / switch, left none.
  - The **server** writes these (operators act while the orchestrator may be stopped) via the shared `autodev/pipeline/event_log.append_pipeline_event` — same writer function, schema, and rotation as the orchestrator's events. `event="operator_action"`, `agent="operator"`, `detail={action, target, source, command?}`, with `run_id`/phase/project read from `pipeline_state` (null-tolerant). New helper `ui/server._write_operator_event`; **19 emit sites across 16 endpoints** (`command` has 3 success paths, `stop` 2), each placed AFTER the mutation commits and OUTSIDE the queue CAS `_apply` closures (a CAS retry can't double-emit). Non-raising — telemetry never breaks an API request.
  - Scope: exactly the roadmap's listed operator actions; `POST /api/queue/trigger-next` is deliberately **excluded** (not in the roadmap's operator-action list).
  - Tests: `tests/test_api_operator_action_events.py` — writer shape + null-run_id + non-raising contract, a live `/api/stop` end-to-end emit, and a source guard that all 16 endpoint action labels have an emit site (+ a floor on the total emit count, catching bulk removal).

- **Observability P1-B — structured escalation taxonomy + planner `retry_class` (capture).**
  - **`escalation_trigger_class` enum** (`orchestrator.ESCALATION_TRIGGER_CLASSES`, 16 values) resolves *why* a phase escalated into durable structured data, so "escalations by cause" is a `jq 'select(.event=="escalation_trigger") | .detail.escalation_trigger_class'` away instead of prose-parsing the free-text reason. Hybrid resolution (`_resolve_escalation_trigger_class`): the error-coded chokepoints (provider-rejected, reset-git, resolver, roadmap-checkbox, merge, session-dead-on-arrival) are classified by a pure `_derive_escalation_trigger_class(last_error_code)`; the exhaustion / reviewer-routing / webhook / stamp-init / repo-init / preempted / git-op chokepoints — which carry no distinguishing error code at dispatch time — stamp `self.state["escalation_trigger_class"]` explicitly (17 sites); resolution prefers the explicit stamp, else derives, else `"unknown"` (a stamp outside the enum is ignored). The class is added to the `escalation_trigger` event detail (alongside `last_error_code` + `last_poll_reason`, reason unchanged), persisted to `phase_state`, and copied onto the canonical metrics row. Resolution + clear + detail are centralized in `_prepare_escalation_trigger`, called at both emit sites (main-loop dispatch + startup repo-init) before `phase_state` is persisted; the consumed stamp is cleared so the next escalation re-resolves cleanly.
  - **Planner `gate_fail` now carries `retry_class`** (`"planner_retry"`) — the executor always carried it and the reviewer conditionally, but the planner omitted it (observability integrity finding 7); all three gate_fail emits are now uniform for the activity feed's retry-source labeling.
  - Tests: `autodev/tests/test_escalation_trigger_class.py` (enum, derive map, resolve precedence, metrics-row field, **+ a source guard that every stamped literal is a valid class and the stamp floor isn't silently lost**), `autodev/tests/test_planner_gate_fail_retry_class.py` (paren-balanced source guard that all three gate_fail emits carry retry_class).

- **Observability P1-A — shared event log + `run_id` foundation (capture; first of the Observability Phase 1+2 plan).**
  - **`run_id` run identity.** A uuid is minted whenever a NEW run begins (it travels with `run_started_at`: orchestrator constructor, queue auto-advance, CLI project-switch, the server's `_clean_pipeline_state_for_project`) and preserved wherever `run_started_at` is preserved (phase advance — in-place mutation; park→revival snapshot + both restore branches; the same-project crash-resume branch). It is threaded into every `pipeline_events.jsonl` line, `run_summary.json` + the `runs_index.jsonl` line, and the canonical metrics row, so failures/escalations/cost are groupable per run (`jq 'group_by(.run_id)'`) without fragile timestamp joins. Surfaced additively on `GET /api/state` (null pre-deploy). Module-level event writers read it from `pipeline_state.json` via `_current_run_id()`; the metrics-row writer reads `self.state`.
  - **Shared event-log writer + size-based rotation** (closes Observability integrity finding 5: `pipeline_events.jsonl` grew unbounded). New `autodev/pipeline/event_log.py` is the single source of truth for the events file contract (schema `{ts, event, run_id, project, phase, agent, detail}`; `ts`/`event` canonical) — `orchestrator._write_pipeline_event` is now a thin wrapper over `append_pipeline_event`, and the same appender will back the server's operator events (P1-C). At `AUTODEV_EVENTS_MAX_BYTES` (default 25 MB) the live file is renamed to a timestamped archive `pipeline_events.<ts>.jsonl` and pruned to `AUTODEV_EVENTS_ARCHIVE_KEEP` (default 5) most-recent archives (race-safe `os.replace`; the server tail self-heals across the rename). Both env knobs coerce garbage → default.
  - Tests: `autodev/tests/test_event_log.py` (append + rotation + prune + env coercion), `autodev/tests/test_run_id_threading.py` (mint helper + threading into events/run_summary/metrics row), `tests/test_api_state_run_id.py` (`/api/state` exposure + launch-template mint). Drift-guard `test_orchestrator_queue.py::_SNAPSHOT_KEYS` updated to include `run_id` (now preserved across park→revival).

- **Project Queue expansion redesign — QT-1…4 (sub-tabs + Cost & Tokens view, per operator mock 2026-06-12).**
  - **QT-1 — per-role data on the snapshot projection.** `_compact_metrics_phases` rows gain `{planner,executor,reviewer}_tokens` + `{planner,executor,reviewer}_cost` (same keys as the metrics-summary rows) — feeding the spend table's share-by-agent bars, the per-phase BY AGENT card, and the client-summed project-level by-agent panels. Tests extended in `tests/test_queue_metrics_tokens.py`.
  - **QT-2 — shared outcome badges.** New `PhaseOutcomeBadge` pill (CLEAN / RETRIED / ESCALATED — single-word labels per the mock, compound detail like "escalated + retried" in the tooltip) adopted by BOTH the Queue spend table and the completion report's `PhaseMetricsTable` (whose bespoke text labels were removed).
  - **QT-3 — expansion sub-tabs.** The row expansion gains **Overview** (default) | **Cost & Tokens** tabs; a metrics chip quick-opens the latter, a plain row click resets to Overview. The Overview stretches on wide windows: width cap raised (`max-w-2xl` → `max-w-5xl`; escalation keeps `3xl` for advisory readability), stat cards go 2→4 responsive columns with a new Tokens card, and the Cost/Tokens cards link **"view breakdown ›"** into the tab. `QueueStatCard` accepts an optional `onClick`.
  - **QT-4 — Cost & Tokens tab** (`QueueCostTokensTab`, superseding `QueueMetricsBreakout` — removed). Monitor-style header strip: PHASES RECORDED + ACTIVE TIME, plus Total cost / Total tokens pills that expand the by-agent (full-width) / by-type + by-agent split cards; nothing expanded shows just the totals. Below, **SPEND BY PHASE**: role legend, tokens⇄$ toggle ($ suppressed when no cost captured), timeline / top-spend sort; each row renders a share-by-agent stacked bar (width normalized to the largest phase so small phases stay visible), value, % of project total, exec ×count, duration, and outcome badge; clicking a phase reveals its BY AGENT / BY TOKEN TYPE cards, honoring the tokens/$ toggle. Escalated phases tint their id amber.
  - Tests: new `tests/test_ui_queue_cost_tokens_tab.py` (12 contracts); stale assertions updated in `test_ui_queue_metrics_breakout.py` (breakout → tab) and `test_ui_queue_table_redesign.py` (expansion slice widened for the tab bar).

- **Pipeline Monitor redesign — MON-1…4 (roadmap header strip + phase expansion, per operator mock 2026-06-12).**
  - **MON-1 — per-phase model capture.** `_record_active_agent` stamps the role's configured model (`_get_agent_model`) into `phase_state.models_used` at every planner/executor/reviewer invocation (best-effort, never blocks); the canonical metrics row copies it (`models_used`, null pre-deploy) and `/api/metrics-summary` passes it through per phase. Tests: `autodev/tests/test_models_used_capture.py`, `tests/test_api_metrics_models_used.py`.
  - **MON-2 — roadmap header strip.** The prose "Run so far" line became a formal strip: a single **Total time** stat on the left; zero-suppressed **Total cost / Total tokens** toggle pills on the right (mutually exclusive). Cost expands one full-width **Cost · by agent** panel; tokens expand **Tokens · by type** + **Tokens · by agent** side by side — all via the new shared `SplitStatCard` (stacked proportion bar, per-row % of total via `fmtPct`). Phases-complete and run-level exec attempts are deliberately not shown (operator follow-up: the progress bar covers phases; attempts live in the per-phase Run Metrics boxes). `fmtUSD` now renders a nonzero sub-cent value as `<$0.01` — a sub-cent run total had slipped past the >0 gates as a zero-reading cost pill.
  - **MON-3 — header-only phase collapse.** The phase row's collapse trigger is now only the name/header block — clicks inside the expanded verification/run-metrics body no longer collapse the row. Collapsed rows show name + goal only (the initial glance summary was cut on operator follow-up — all metrics detail stays behind the expansion, keeping the roadmap view clean).
  - **MON-4 — Run Metrics block redesign.** Header with `skill:` badge and `model:` badge(s) (one per distinct model, roles + full provider id in the tooltip); four stat boxes (Duration / Exec attempts with red retry-source sub-line / Reviewer passes / Escalations); **By agent** breakout card with a tokens⇄$ toggle (the $ option renders only when the phase captured cost — established suppression) + **By token type** card, both `SplitStatCard`. Exit criteria and the Stage-J behavioral fields merged under one **Verification** header (labels now colon-less rows; "If this fails, you see" → "If this fails"). The branch footer is unchanged.
  - **Removed with their consumers:** `fmtTokenRoleSplit` and `fmtCostBreakdown` (both superseded by the split cards). `tests/test_ui_run_metrics_field_order.py` deleted (pinned the old two-column grid; ordering now pinned by `tests/test_ui_monitor_redesign.py`); stale assertions updated in `test_ui_phase4_cost_token_visibility.py`, `test_ui_phase_expansion_role_tokens.py`, `test_w4g_run_totals.py`, `test_p0_stage_j_ui_behavioral_verification.py`. New: `tests/test_ui_monitor_redesign.py` (14 contracts).

- **Observability pass — METRICS-E1…E4 (cost/token surfacing end-to-end).**
  - **E1 — degraded token-capture signal** (observability roadmap integrity finding 1): `_accumulate_role_tokens` now latches `phase_state.token_capture_degraded` and emits a `token_capture_warning` pipeline event when an attempt's session JSONL is missing (`missing_session_file`) or its session path never resolved (`no_session_path`) — the silent-zeros path that previously read identically to a genuinely free attempt (the `_sum_session_tokens` bug class). The flag is copied onto the canonical metrics row (`token_capture_degraded`, default `False`). Capture itself was **verified working** against live-run data (itsMYcraftNOW: non-zero per-role totals + class breakdowns on all post-recount rows; cost 0.0 = local models reporting no cost, zero-suppressed by design). `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py`; tests `autodev/tests/test_token_capture_degraded.py`.
  - **E2 — per-phase per-role token split.** `/api/metrics-summary` per-phase rows gain `planner_tokens`/`executor_tokens`/`reviewer_tokens` ints (via `_role_token_total`, paralleling the `planner_cost`/… split); the Run Metrics phase expansion renders them as a muted sub-line under Tokens via the new `fmtTokenRoleSplit` (hidden when all-zero; the inline token-class split is unchanged). A new cross-layer contract test drives the orchestrator's REAL writers (`_accumulate_role_tokens` → `_write_canonical_metrics_row`) and reads the row back through the server's REAL helpers, pinning the writer/reader key contract (`autodev/tests/test_token_writer_reader_contract.py`). Also: `tests/test_api_metrics_summary_role_tokens.py`, `tests/test_ui_phase_expansion_role_tokens.py`.
  - **E3 — Queue metric chips + per-phase breakout.** `_project_metrics_totals` gains `tokens_total`; `GET /api/queue` entries and `GET /api/queue/{id}/snapshot` carry it; the snapshot also returns `metrics_phases` (compact per-phase projection via the new `_compact_metrics_phases`, same keys as the metrics-summary rows). The queue COST column became **METRICS**: quiet zero-suppressed cost + token chips (`fmtTokensCompact`, "11.4M tok"); clicking a chip opens the row expansion in a metrics-breakout view (`QueueMetricsBreakout`: stat-card summary + per-phase table) — per-phase detail lives only in the expansion, never inline in the row. The completion panel's per-phase table was extracted into the shared `PhaseMetricsTable`, so the Monitor and the Queue render one markup source. Tests: `tests/test_queue_metrics_tokens.py`, `tests/test_ui_queue_metrics_breakout.py`.
  - **E4 — durable completion report from the Queue.** New read-only `GET /api/queue/{entry_id}/report`: the full `/api/metrics-summary` shape built against THAT entry's project (the endpoint body was extracted into the shared `_build_project_metrics_summary`, so the two cannot drift), plus `completion_report` (`{found, content, mtime}`) and roadmap checkbox counts. COMPLETED queue rows gain a **View report** action rendering `QueueReportModal` — the same `PipelineCompletePanel` the Monitor shows at PIPELINE_COMPLETE (new optional `phaseCounts` prop; the Generate-Docs CTA is now also gated on an `onGenerateDocs` handler being passed, since the modal must not fire the ACTIVE-project webhook) plus the report markdown. A completed project's summary is now recallable after the queue advances, instead of a one-time Monitor snapshot. Tests: `tests/test_api_queue_report.py`, `tests/test_ui_queue_report_modal.py`.
  - **Test updates for moved/renamed markup:** `tests/test_ui_phase4_cost_token_visibility.py::test_completion_per_phase_token_column` now asserts the column gates inside the shared `PhaseMetricsTable` (+ that the panel renders it); `tests/test_ui_queue_table_redesign.py::test_table_column_headers_present` expects METRICS in place of COST.

- **Browser E2E suite can now authenticate against a token-protected dashboard (`AUTODEV_UI_E2E_TOKEN`).** `tests/test_browser_path_selector.py` previously could not run against a tokenized server at all: its reachability probe used plain `urllib` (401 → every test skipped with a misleading "not reachable") and the Playwright contexts carried no credentials — so live verification needed a throwaway shim server. A new **dedicated opt-in env var `AUTODEV_UI_E2E_TOKEN`** (read at import, like `AUTODEV_UI_E2E_URL`) now flows everywhere the suite talks to the server: `_auth_headers()` adds `Authorization: Bearer` to the probe and to all four in-test `urllib` calls (queue list/DELETE cleanup, preflight POST, prune POST), and `_context_kwargs()` gives every per-test browser context `extra_http_headers` with the same bearer — so each isolated context authenticates without cookie state. **Deliberately not a fallback to `AUTODEV_UI_TOKEN`:** the conftest scrubs that var per-test precisely so a sourced `.env` cannot poison the suite, and pointing these state-mutating tests (queue add/delete, preflight → recents) at a live tokenized dashboard must be an explicit operator action. The reachability probe became tri-state (`_probe_server`: `ok` / `auth_rejected` / `unreachable` — a non-auth HTTP error like 500 deliberately reads as unreachable) keeping skips loud + narrow (P1 Stage B): a **wrong explicit token `pytest.fail`s** ("rejected AUTODEV_UI_E2E_TOKEN — wrong or rotated token"), a token-protected server **without** the opt-in skips with the actionable hint ("set AUTODEV_UI_E2E_TOKEN to run these tests"), a down server skips with the unchanged message. The no-token path stays byte-identical (`new_context()` with zero kwargs — pinned). *Tests (TDD, red→green — 9 new in `tests/test_browser_path_selector_token_auth.py`; red run 8 failed / 1 passed, the bare-context lock green by design):* `_auth_headers` with/without token, bare-vs-bearer context kwargs (+ teardown still closes only the per-test context), probe ok/401→auth_rejected/URLError→unreachable/500→unreachable, and bearer-on-probe / no-header-without-token via a captured `urllib` Request. `tests/test_browser_path_selector_fixture_scope.py`'s loader now strips `AUTODEV_UI_E2E_TOKEN` around module exec so its bare-contract pins hold even when the operator's shell exports the var (verified both ways). *Live verification (the whole point):* all **9 browser tests passed against the real token-protected production dashboard on `:18790`** — forward (32.4 s) and reversed (29.6 s) order — with the real `_TokenAuthMiddleware` validating every request; the authed in-test queue-DELETE and prune cleanups confirmed working on disk (queue back to 0 entries, recents self-swept). Same run hygiene as the prior shim run: `queue_mode` flipped `auto → manual` first and restored after (empty queue → the restore's auto-advance no-op'd `queue_empty`); the no-opt-in hint skip was also observed live against the tokenized server before the token run. *Changed:* `tests/test_browser_path_selector.py`, `tests/test_browser_path_selector_token_auth.py` (new), `tests/test_browser_path_selector_fixture_scope.py` (loader hardening), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (token usage in the browser-test snippet), and the two stale CHANGELOG callouts below (the dashboard-auth entry's `?token=`-URL suggestion was inaccurate and now points here; the fixture-speedup entry's "candidate follow-up" landed as this entry). *Callouts (not actioned):* the conftest's session prune fixture needs no token (it calls `post_setup_recent_projects_prune` in-process, not over HTTP); the queue-DELETE/prune cleanups remain best-effort `try/except` by design — under a wrong token they would silently no-op, which is why the wrong-token case hard-fails at the probe before any test runs.

- **CI clean-install gate (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) — every dependency pin must `pip install` from scratch on Python 3.11 / 3.12 / 3.13.** Catches the whole "pin too old to ship wheels for current CPython" failure class automatically: such a pin falls back to an sdist build, which then breaks (the way `PyYAML==6.0` did under Cython 3 — see Fixed below). Two-step install (`requirements.txt`, then `requirements-dev.txt`, which chains through to `ui/requirements.txt`, so all three files are exercised) plus an import smoke test of the canonical launch path (`import ui.server`).

- **Dashboard access-token auth (`AUTODEV_UI_TOKEN`) — the UI and `/api/*` are no longer unauthenticated.** A new **pure-ASGI** middleware (`_TokenAuthMiddleware` in `ui/server.py`; deliberately not `BaseHTTPMiddleware`, so the SSE stream at `/api/events/stream` is never wrapped or buffered) guards every route except `GET /health` (liveness) and `/static/*` (assets). **Token configured:** `GET /?token=<match>` → 303 to a clean `/` + HttpOnly `SameSite=Lax` cookie (30 days); a valid `Authorization: Bearer` header or the cookie passes; unauthenticated `/` gets a human-readable 401 page, everything else 401 JSON + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`. The query channel is honored **only on `/`** so tokens never land in API request logs; all comparisons via `secrets.compare_digest`. **No token configured (legacy single-user mode):** loopback requests pass through byte-identical to before; non-loopback-IP requests are refused 403. An *unparseable* client host (in-process transports — TestClient sends `("testclient", 50000)`) is deliberately treated as loopback: real TCP always carries a real IP, so the fail-open is unreachable from the network. **Resolution is dual-source per house convention:** `AUTODEV_UI_TOKEN` env (stripped) wins over `ui_token` in `ui/config.json` (new `DEFAULTS` key; empty = auth off); a non-string `ui_token` degrades to auth-off instead of 500-ing every request (numeric-coercion philosophy). The lifespan startup banner prints the tokenized access URL (Jupyter-style) or a loud open-mode warning. `install.sh` **step 10** generates `AUTODEV_UI_TOKEN` into `.env` via the existing idempotent `merge_dotenv_missing_keys` (existing values preserved; no new step — the 14-step contract test holds). *Tests (TDD, red→green — 18 new in `tests/test_ui_auth_token.py`; red run 9 failed / 9 passed, the legacy-mode + exemption locks green by design):* open-mode passthrough (api/root), non-loopback refusal (api/root) + loopback/unparseable-host allows + health-exempt-even-nonloopback, token-mode 401s (api JSON+`WWW-Authenticate`, root HTML page, SSE), bearer-grant, query-on-root 303+cookie-flags, cookie-grants-api, wrong-credentials (bearer/query/cookie) + query-token-rejected-on-api, env-override-with-strip + non-string-token-treated-unset; `tests/conftest.py` `_ENV_KEYS_TO_SCRUB` gains `AUTODEV_UI_TOKEN` so a sourced dev `.env` cannot poison the suite. The SSE test runs its request in a **daemon thread with a join timeout**: against a middleware-less server the infinite stream *hangs* `TestClient.get()` (observed live on the first red run — the pre-fix red run had to be killed), and the watchdog converts that hang into a prompt failure. *Changed:* `ui/server.py`, `install.sh`, `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_ui_auth_token.py` (new), `README.md`, `SETUP.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `GLOSSARY.md`, `.env.example`, `ui/config.example.json`. *Callouts (not actioned):* Host-header validation deferred — the cookie is domain-bound so DNS rebinding cannot ride it in token mode, and an open-mode allowlist would collide with TestClient's `Host: testserver` across 84 existing files (future `allowed_hosts` slice); the bundled systemd unit keeps its pre-existing broken `ExecStart` (no `__main__` in `server.py`) and loads no `.env` — service installs set `ui_token` in `ui/config.json`; `tests/test_browser_path_selector.py` against a tokenized live server authenticates via `AUTODEV_UI_E2E_TOKEN` (see the browser-E2E token entry below — a `?token=` URL in `AUTODEV_UI_E2E_URL` does **not** work; it breaks the tests' URL concatenation and `urllib` probe); no rate limiting (unchanged, still listed in SECURITY.md).

### Changed

- **The escalation advisory is now composed by the escalation agent via OpenClaw — the orchestrator makes zero LLM calls.** Previously the orchestrator itself POSTed a hardcoded `"model": "qwen3.5-27b"` chat-completion directly to the local llama-server (30 s sync call, blocking the escalation dispatch) to produce the dashboard `{summary, recommended_action}` advisory — duplicating machinery that already existed end-to-end: the escalation agent ships an `escalation-summary` skill with that exact output contract, and the orchestrator already had the `escalation_summary.json` reader + a post-resolution promotion site. The duplicate is deleted; the contract is now: **(1)** every escalation dispatch records an honest deterministic `escalation_message` immediately (`_record_escalation_reason` → `_compose_fallback_reason`, `status="fallback"`) so the panel shows a factual reason the instant it renders — no loader, the `"generating"` status is retired (a status only an in-process wait could clear would strand the panel after a queue auto-advance); **(2)** the dispatch clears any stale `escalation_summary.json` (`_clear_stale_escalation_summary` — without this a summary from a previous escalation/project could be promoted as the current one's); **(3)** the webhook message (shared single builder `_build_escalation_webhook_message`, plus the matching `webhook_client.py` default for the crash path) instructs the agent to compose per its skill and WRITE `escalation_summary.json` BEFORE notifying, pointing the agent's *reads* at the resolved absolute artifacts path so they survive a queue-advance symlink repoint; **(4)** the WAITING_FOR_HUMAN poll loop promotes a landed summary into `escalation_message`/`status="ready"` within one ~10 s cycle (`_promote_agent_escalation_summary`, idempotent, also run at resolution) so the dashboard upgrades in place. Fixed in the same pass: the escalation agent's `AGENTS.md` flatly forbade writing *any* pipeline file — directly contradicting its own skill (likely why agent summaries rarely appeared live); it now carves out `escalation_summary.json` as the one permitted write, the skill's stale Timing section (pre-F13 "before you submit your command recommendation") now says write-before-notify, and the runtime `~/.openclaw/workspace-escalation/` copies were synced (dual-source rule). The dead zero-caller `_read_escalation_summary` legacy reader was deleted. UI: the `EscalationCommandPanel` and queue-detail "Reviewing escalation…" loader branches are removed (states are now `fallback` → `ready`); the dead `waiting_for_human_at` prop chain into the panel went with them. *Tests (TDD, red→green):* **17 new** in `autodev/tests/test_escalation_advisory_agent_owned.py` (old helpers absent / new helpers present, fallback-immediately + never-raises, stale-clear, promote upgrade/idempotent/malformed/missing/empty, webhook-message contract incl. write-before-NOTIFY ordering + absolute-path reads + no embedded advisory, three dispatch-site wirings, poll-loop promotion, webhook_client default parity); `test_orchestrator_escalation_advisory.py` reduced to the surviving `_read_escalation_advisory` reader tests; `test_escalation_advisory_loader.py` keeps `TestComposeFallbackReason`, drops the LLM-wrapper + "generating"-ordering guards; deleted `test_orchestrator_escalation_advisory_deblame.py` / `_behavioral.py` (they pinned the deleted LLM call's input payload) and `test_orchestrator_escalation_summary.py` (dead reader); `tests/test_ui_escalation_panel_shared.py` + `test_w5_queue_snapshot_detail_pane.py` adapted; `test_escalation_agent_docs.py` + `test_webhook_client_default_messages.py` adapted to the shared-builder design. *Callouts — all three since actioned in this same `[Unreleased]` pass (see the `### Fixed` entry "Queue auto-advance no longer races the escalation agent's advisory write"):* the auto-advance symlink write race is closed by a bounded pre-advance hold (`AUTODEV_ESCALATION_SUMMARY_WAIT`), the "agent is reviewing…" pulse cue shipped, and the `escalation_failed.json` writes are now atomic.

### Removed

- **Blame attribution (the three-layer plan/impl/infra system) — executor exhaustion now escalates directly, and the orchestrator's local-LLM dependency is gone.** `run_blame_attribution()` (Layer 1: direct `qwen3.5-27b` llama-server POST, 60 s; Layer 2: string heuristics; Layer 3: hard `impl` default) is deleted along with `_llama_chat_completions_url`, `_LLAMA_ORIGIN`, and the `AUTODEV_LLAMA_BASE` env var (nothing reads it anymore). Verified-in-code rationale: **the impl "self-heal retry" was structurally inert** — gate failures increment `executor_retries` via `reset_execution("auto")`, so at `retries >= 3` an impl verdict's `reset_execution("auto")` incremented the counter PAST the gate (3→4) and the next loop iteration re-entered the blame block **without ever re-running the executor**; production behavior was up to three sequential 60 s LLM calls on the identical `failure_context.json`, then escalation anyway (the operator-path comment at `reset_execution(escalation)` documents exactly this trap — the fix was never applied to the auto path). The analyst also collapsed precise `gate_error_codes` into a vaguer 3-way label biased toward `impl` by its own prompt, and the `lessons.md` `[BLAME]` log was write-only. New behavior: after the (preserved) EX-RR surviving-output salvage, exhaustion writes `failure_context.json` and escalates with an honest action string — `Executor retries exhausted after <N> attempts (last error: <ERR_…>)` — which the dispatch copies into `escalation_trigger_reason`; the operator routes plan-vs-impl from the dashboard (`RESET_PHASE` / `RESET_EXECUTION`, which grants a properly-reset fresh budget). Exhaustion handling is now uniform across planner/executor/reviewer, and there are **no hidden retry extensions**. Data-capture changes: `blame_verdict`/`blame_context`/`blame_fires`/`prior_blame_attributions` are no longer written anywhere (phase_state, canonical metrics row, run summary, `phase_complete` event detail, failure_context schema, executor metrics-row template); **historical** rows/summaries keep their legacy fields and all readers stay tolerant (`/api/metrics-summary` still aggregates legacy `blame_fires` for old projects; the UI suppresses at 0 and its legacy renderers are deliberately retained). *Tests (TDD, red→green):* **6 new** in `autodev/tests/test_executor_exhaustion_escalates.py` (method + inner helpers fully absent, no blame fields anywhere in orchestrator source, no direct-LLM markers, exhaustion block escalates with `last_error_code` and no `reset_execution`/HTTP, failure-context still written, EX-RR ordering); deleted `test_defensive_c5_04.py` + `TestT42BlameAnalystShapeCheck`/`TestT49LlamaBaseUrl` (in `test_defensive_p4_parse_safety.py`); adapted `test_orchestrator_executor_rr_guard.py` (marker), `test_escalation_cap.py` (drops the `prior_blame_attributions` clear; keeps the load-bearing `executor_retries→0`), `test_w1_metrics_row_counters.py` (W1-A inverted to absence), `test_w2b_run_summary.py` (summary asserts key absence while fixture rows deliberately keep legacy fields to prove reader tolerance), `test_phase3_metrics_row_pain_signals.py` / `test_p0_stage_h_metrics_row_breakdown.py` / `test_p0_stage_h_reset_execution_counters.py` / `test_orchestrator_nuclear_reset.py` (fixture scrubs), `test_defensive_c1_05.py` / `test_planner_preservation.py` / `test_p0_stage_h_routing_pivot_any_plan.py` (stale docstrings; the latter's reviewer-gate pass-routing tests are untouched — that system is separate and stays). *Suites:* `autodev/tests/` 1217 passed; `tests/` 2161 passed, 22 skipped (pre-existing browser skips). *Docs (same pass):* `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (blame section → "Executor Retry Exhaustion → Escalation" with a dated historical note; failure_context schema; metrics-row example; phase_state schema; agent-owned-advisory paragraph replacing the G1 advisory/loader paragraphs; "zero model calls" claims now unqualified), `CLAUDE.md` (`AUTODEV_LLAMA_BASE` row retired; F13 escalation bullet; `phase_complete` event row; orchestrator-monolith intro), `PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md` (roster note; Blame-Heuristic-Tuning + OB-3 mooted; B7 heartbeat decision marked superseded — the shipped heartbeat is lock-only), executor `AGENTS.md` metrics-row template. *Callouts (not actioned):* the reviewer gate's `attribution: plan/impl` pass-routing is unrelated and unchanged; `lessons.md` itself stays (agents/UI seed it — the orchestrator just no longer writes `[BLAME]` lines into it); the executor's runtime `~/.openclaw/workspace-executor/AGENTS.md` copy was not hand-synced (no parity test exists for it; install.sh step 5 deploys it).

### Documentation

- **Brand rename: AutoDev → Lullabeast (Tier-1 "veneer" only — no structural/plumbing changes).** Renamed the user-facing/display product name from "AutoDev" to "Lullabeast" across documentation prose + titles (`README.md`, `SETUP.md`, `GLOSSARY.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `ui/README.md`, `autodev/docs/*.md` bodies), agent-identity docs (`escalation`/`prd-creator`/`roadmap-converter`), the plugin display name + descriptions (`openclaw.plugin.json`, `package.json`, `plugin/src/index.ts`), inert code comments/docstrings, the `LICENSE` copyright line, `requirements*.txt` headers, `install.sh` operator-facing prose, and the systemd/launchd service **descriptions** — closing the UI↔docs gap (the dashboard already shipped as "Lullabeast"). Done as a case-sensitive `AutoDev`→`Lullabeast` rename; structural-token counts (`AUTODEV_`, `.autodev`, `from autodev …`, `autodev-pipeline-signals`, `@autodev/…`, `com.autodev.ui`, `autodev-ui.service`, `autodev:` sessionStorage keys) verified **unchanged** before/after. **Deliberately preserved (deployment/integration contracts, operator-scoped to veneer-only):** the `AUTODEV_*` environment variables, the `.autodev/` runtime dir, the `autodev/` Python package + all imports, the `autodev-pipeline-signals` plugin ID, the `@autodev/pipeline-signals` npm name, the `com.autodev.ui` / `autodev-ui.service` deployment identifiers + `/tmp/autodev-ui.*` log paths, the repo directory name, the `.env` idempotency markers in `setup_helpers.py` / `.env.example` (matched against deployed `.env` files), the `autodev:` browser sessionStorage keys, and OpenClaw itself. **Deferred to a separate pass:** runtime string literals (escalation webhook/prompt/halt messages, Windows-rejection `SystemExit` text) in `orchestrator.py` / `webhook_client.py` / `ui/server.py` / `heartbeat_cron.py`, and test docstrings/comments. *Lockstep test assertions updated:* `tests/test_docs_setup_glossary.py`, `tests/test_install_sh_step_numbering.py`. *Dual-source agent docs synced* to the live `~/.openclaw/workspace-*` copies. *Changed:* 37 files (brand-token prose only); CHANGELOG history left intact as an append-only record.

- **Diagnosed the 2026-06-08 `baseball` / `Minecraft` / escalation-hold incident; two doc outcomes captured (no code change).** *Issue #1* — baseball `ERR_FILE_MISSING` ×11 came from a Unity-6 Android/iOS roadmap queued on a headless Raspberry Pi: the build + on-device-verify phase (`INFRA-E1`) is unsatisfiable on this host, and the executor kept ending its turn without writing `executor_output.json` (→ `ERR_FILE_MISSING` via `gate_scripts/utils.py` `load_json_safe`), escalating twice. Captured as a future-enhancement plan `plans/upcomming/environment-prerequisite-preflight.md` (+ catalog entry/section in `plans/upcomming/FUTURE-ENHANCEMENTS.md`) scoping a project prerequisite & environment-readiness preflight — declarable required env vars + host-capability probes, surfaced early at PRD/roadmap & queue-add time. *Issue #3* — escalation holds instead of advancing: documented in `CLAUDE.md` (Queue System) and `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (Parked-escalation revival) that the escalation park→advance decision reads `queue_mode` **once, at escalation time** (manual = hold), is **not** re-evaluated while parked, and a later `manual → auto` toggle is therefore inert — intended behavior, confirmed live (baseball parked under `manual`; toggling to `auto` ~1 h later did not advance to queued `Minecraft`). *Issue #2* — the `Minecraft` symlink / stale-queue-entry corruption — requires code changes and is handled separately.

### Fixed

- **Stop → Resume now continues the in-flight agent instead of re-running the phase from the planner — and an operator stop no longer burns an agent retry.** Hitting **Resume** after a manual stop re-ran the phase from the *planner*, re-doing completed executor work. Root cause: the orchestrator's `RETRY` handler reconstructed `current_agent` by string-matching `self.state["last_action"]`, but the STOPPED transition overwrites `last_action` to `"Stop sentinel consumed …"` *before* `RETRY` runs, so the match always fell through to `else: current_agent="planner"`. The authoritative pre-stop `current_agent` had already been erased by `/api/resume-ready`, which hard-sets `current_agent="escalation"` (load-bearing — `_should_invoke_escalation_agent()` gates the command-consumption branch on it). *Fix:* `/api/resume-ready` now stashes the pre-stop `current_agent` into a new **`resume_target_agent`** field before overwriting it; the `RETRY` handler calls the new **`_restore_resume_target_agent()`** which applies and clears that stash (defaulting to `planner` only when absent/invalid). The `last_action` string-match heuristic is **removed entirely** (it was the sole control-flow reader of `last_action`; every other read is logging). The executor's branch/commits were never git-deleted, so this is purely control-routing — no repo change. *Same pass — the adjacent stop-handling wart:* the planner and reviewer poll loops misread a `PollResult(False, "stopped")` (falsy, and not in the stall set) as a sentinel timeout and called `increment_{planner,reviewer}_retries()` — spuriously burning a retry, and in the `retries==2` edge flipping to escalation right before the loop-top consumed the stop. Both poll sites now short-circuit `reason=="stopped"` with a `continue`, deferring the clean halt to the loop-top `_check_stop_requested()`; the executor path was already retry-neutral on `"stopped"`. *Tests (TDD, red→green):* `autodev/tests/test_retry_resume_target.py` (restore-from-stash, planner default, invalid-value rejection), `autodev/tests/test_operator_stop_no_retry_burn.py` (source-structure guard that both polls short-circuit `stopped` before the increment — the convention for the monolithic `run()` loop), two new cases in `tests/test_api_resume_ready.py` (stash written; not invented when no agent). *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (new `_restore_resume_target_agent`, RETRY handler, planner + reviewer stop branches), `ui/server.py` (`post_resume_ready` stash + docstring). *Docs:* `CLAUDE.md` (F11 paragraph, sentinel-reason list), `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (RETRY row).

- **The "Reset ×N" badge (and the rest of the escalation/advisory panel) no longer sticks across phases until a manual refresh.** The badge renders on `escalation_resets > 0`. `GET /api/state` omits `escalation_resets` (and its sibling "present-only" escalation fields) once `phase_state.json` is deleted on phase advance, but the dashboard's `fetchState` merged each poll with a shallow spread `setPState(prev => ({ ...prev, ...data }))` — an omitted key can never overwrite the stale value, so the badge survived until `PipelineScreen` unmounted (refresh / navigate away). *Fix (frontend, whole-class):* a new **`ESCALATION_VIEW_DEFAULTS`** const (the escalation/advisory subset of `_compute_escalation_view`'s present-only keys — the two reset counters plus the advisory-text fields) is spread **before** `...data` in the merge, so a key the backend drops resets to its default while a present key still wins. This fixes the whole field-family in one place (the sibling advisory fields shared the identical latent bug, masked only by `undefined` init). The backend "present-only" contract is intentionally **kept** (the queue snapshot relies on it via `.get(…, default)`), so there is no API change and no `test_api_state.py` churn. *Tests (TDD, red→green):* `tests/test_escalation_view_stale_merge.py` (the defaults const exists and zeroes the counters; the merge spreads it before `...data`). *Changed:* `ui/index.html`. *Callout (not actioned):* `skill_injected` / `skill_agent` share the same staleness class (a stale skill label until the next phase writes `phase_state`) but are a different feature family — excluded, logged for a future targeted pass.

- **Queue auto-advance no longer races the escalation agent's advisory write — the dispatch holds (bounded) for `escalation_summary.json` before advancing.** In auto-queue mode the main escalation dispatch advanced to the next project immediately after the escalation webhook fired; `_select_next_queue_project` repoints the `pipeline-project` symlink, and the still-in-flight escalation agent writes its advisory *through* that symlink (OpenClaw sandboxes the write tool to the workspace — absolute-path writes are silently discarded), so the summary landed in the WRONG project's tree and the parked row kept the deterministic fallback message forever. Now, after the webhook returns SUCCESS and before `_queue_after_park_maybe_advance()`, `_wait_for_escalation_summary_before_advance` polls (~2 s) for the summary and promotes it on landing (`_promote_agent_escalation_summary`; the dispatch just cleared any stale copy, so appearance ⇒ fresh — no mtime guard needed). Bounded by the new env knob **`AUTODEV_ESCALATION_SUMMARY_WAIT`** (default 300 s; `0` disables; parsing mirrors `_stall_timeout_seconds` except the min clamp is 0, since 0 is a meaningful operator value here); on timeout the advance proceeds with the fallback advisory — the prior behavior, now narrowed to the hung-agent case. The hold engages only when the advance would actually fire (queue_mode `auto` + non-empty queue, the same read `_queue_after_park_maybe_advance` performs — in manual / single-project mode the WAITING_FOR_HUMAN poll loop already promotes within one cycle); a pending STOP breaks the hold early **without consuming** the stop sentinel (the loop-top `_check_stop_requested()` owns consumption); the repo-init dispatch (never auto-advances) and the crash handler (terminating) deliberately do not hold. Cost is ~zero wall-clock on this deployment: the escalation agent (qwen3.6-27b) and the pipeline agents (darkqwen3.6-27b-mtp) share one single-GPU llama-swap server, so the next project's planner cannot make token progress during the escalation turn anyway — the budget only bounds a hung agent. Same pass: **all three `escalation_failed.json` writes are now atomic** via the shared never-raising `_write_escalation_failed_atomic` (mkstemp + `os.replace` house rule) — repo-init dispatch (`fallback_dir`), main dispatch (`PROJECT_ARTIFACTS_DIR`; previously a bare `open()` whose write failure would have escaped into the crash handler instead of completing the HALTED_SILENT transition), and crash handler (`OPENCLAW_ROOT`). And the UI nicety from the advisory entry's callout shipped: while the advisory is `fallback` and the pipeline is `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN`, the `EscalationCommandPanel` fallback text carries a subtle **"Escalation agent is reviewing — this may update shortly."** pulse-dot cue — a hint that the text may upgrade in place, not a loader (the retired `"generating"` status stays retired; the Queue detail pane is unaffected — it suppresses the panel's advisory block via `showAdvisory={false}` and renders its own). *Tests (TDD, red→green):* **25 new** in `autodev/tests/test_escalation_summary_wait_before_advance.py` (env-knob parsing incl. 0-disables + negative-clamps; wait-helper units — already-on-disk promotes with zero sleeps, mid-wait landing returns on that tick, timeout keeps fallback, env=0 skips, stop-sentinel breaks early *without consuming*, manual-mode / empty-queue / corrupt-queue no-ops; dispatch-wiring source guards — wait after webhook, gated on SUCCESS, before advance; repo-init + crash sites do NOT wait; atomic-helper units + no-bare-`open()` source guard); **1 new** UI static-lint in `tests/test_ui_escalation_panel_shared.py` (cue gated on `"fallback"` + `humanWait`, pulse idiom, no `"generating"` revival). *Docs:* `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (advisory paragraph — known limitation narrowed to timeout-exceeded, plus the hold contract; Stage H park→advance intro; `escalation_failed.json` atomicity note), `CLAUDE.md` (new Operational Constants row; F13 bullet; queue park→advance section).

- **Browser E2E tests (`tests/test_browser_path_selector.py`) errored instead of skipping when the optional Python `playwright` package was absent — now fixed, and Playwright is treated as a first-class (preventative + reactive) dependency.** The module's only Playwright import lived *inside* the `pw_page` fixture, so on a host with the UI server reachable but `playwright` uninstalled every test raised a fixture-setup `ModuleNotFoundError` (hit on the migrated VM). **Correcting the record:** these were **fixture-setup-time** errors gated on server reachability, **not** collection-time errors — `pytest --collect-only` collects all 9 cleanly, and with the server *down* the old code skipped 9× for the wrong reason (`AutoDev UI not reachable`, masking the missing dep). *Fix (loud + narrow, per P1 Stage B):* a module-level `sync_api = pytest.importorskip("playwright.sync_api", reason="playwright not installed — pip install playwright && playwright install chromium")` gates the whole module — one clear skip naming the dependency **and** the remedy — and the now-redundant in-fixture import is removed (the fixture uses the captured `sync_api` handle). Because pinning the package (below) makes "package present, Chromium binary absent" a real state, the fixture also skips loudly on that specific launch failure (`_is_missing_browser_error` → "run: playwright install chromium"), re-raising any other launch error. *Preventative + reactive:* `playwright==1.60.0` is now pinned in `requirements-dev.txt` (with a documented one-time `playwright install chromium`), so the dev/test toolchain installs it by default while the `importorskip` + missing-binary guards keep a removed-then-missing playwright degrading to a clear skip. *Docs reframed:* the **Node** `@playwright/mcp` (the pipeline's visual-review dependency, already installed by `install.sh` step 12) was labelled "optional" in `SETUP.md` / `README.md` despite being **required for UI/INT phases** (missing → reviewer gate rejects every UI/INT phase with `ERR_VISUAL_UNVERIFIED`); both now say so, keeping `--skip-playwright` as an explicit opt-out. *Tests (TDD, red→green):* **1 new** `tests/test_browser_path_selector_playwright_guard.py` execs the module with `playwright` blocked in-process and asserts it raises `Skipped` with the dependency + remedy in the reason (fails before the gate, passes after); the 9 browser tests are otherwise unchanged. *Changed:* `tests/test_browser_path_selector.py`, `tests/test_browser_path_selector_playwright_guard.py` (new), `requirements-dev.txt`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SETUP.md`, `README.md`. *Callouts (not actioned):* the sibling `test_browser_elapsed_timer.py` / `test_browser_escalation_agent_visibility.py` are unconditional manual-MCP `@pytest.mark.skip` stubs with no Playwright dependency (no guard needed); `--skip-playwright` stays an opt-out (CI / node-less hosts need it) — the docs were reframed, not the installer control flow; a `pyproject.toml` extras group (`pip install -e .[e2e]`) would beat a `requirements-dev.txt` pin but the repo has no packaging (deferred).

- **Browser E2E fixture launches Chromium once per session instead of once per test (~9× fewer launches), with per-test isolation preserved.** `tests/test_browser_path_selector.py`'s `pw_page` previously launched and tore down a whole Chromium per test. It is now split into a **session-scoped `_pw_browser`** (one launch; it also carries the server-reachability check and the missing-binary skip) and a **function-scoped `pw_page`** that opens a fresh `browser.new_context()` per test — a clean cookies/storage/cache jar — so tests stay order-independent and one test's UI state cannot leak into the next. Verified against **real Chromium**: a cookie set in one context is invisible to another, and closing a context leaves the shared browser + sibling contexts alive. Naive full-session-scoping (a shared *page*) was deliberately rejected — these tests mutate recents/queue and would become order-dependent and failure-cascading. The 9 tests consume `pw_page` unchanged (page-level API only); `_server_ok()` now runs once per session rather than per test (benign). *Tests (TDD, red→green):* **1 new** `tests/test_browser_path_selector_fixture_scope.py` (3 cases) pins the fixture scopes and the per-test-context isolation contract by driving the extracted `_isolated_page_session` generator against a fake browser — no live browser required. *Changed:* `tests/test_browser_path_selector.py`, `tests/test_browser_path_selector_fixture_scope.py` (new). *Live verification:* all **9 tests passed against a real dashboard** in both forward and **reversed** order (~31 s each — order-independence proven live, zero skips), served by the genuine token-protected app via a throwaway loopback ASGI shim that injects the real `Authorization: Bearer` token per request (the unmodified tests have no auth support; the real `_TokenAuthMiddleware` validated every request). Run hygiene: `queue_mode` was flipped `auto → manual` first (an empty idle queue + free lock meant the queue-add test would otherwise have **auto-started a real orchestrator** on the throwaway project) and restored after — the restore's auto-advance no-op'd with `reason: "queue_empty"` as predicted; queue/recents/symlinks verified byte-restored post-run. *Callout (since landed):* at the time of this run the tests could not authenticate against a tokenized dashboard on their own (`AUTODEV_UI_E2E_URL` with a `?token=` URL breaks the tests' URL concatenation and `urllib` probe) — native token support landed as the `AUTODEV_UI_E2E_TOKEN` entry under **Added** above, which re-verified this same 9-test run directly against the token-protected production dashboard with no shim.

- **Test hermeticity — a dashboard `ui_token` in the local (gitignored) `ui/config.json` `401`'d every `setup/*`-style test that hit a non-exempt route without patching `load_config`.** `_TokenAuthMiddleware` resolves the token per-request via `_resolve_ui_token(load_config())`, reading `AUTODEV_UI_TOKEN` **or** `ui_token` from `ui/config.json`; the autouse `_scrub_autodev_env` clears only the env var, so on a developer machine with dashboard auth configured the config-file token leaked in and turned ~dozens of pre-auth tests red (e.g. `test_api_setup_launch.py::TestEndpointBasics::test_endpoint_{missing,empty}_repo_path_returns_422`, `test_api_setup_switch_project.py::TestRecentProjects::test_get_recent_projects_returns_list`). This is **not** a clean-checkout failure (no `ui/config.json` ⇒ no token) — it's a hermeticity gap: the tests depended on the *absence* of a local token. *Fix:* a new autouse `_neutralize_ui_token_auth` fixture (`tests/conftest.py`, beside `_scrub_autodev_env`) wraps `ui.server.load_config` to blank `ui_token` — **only when `AUTODEV_UI_TOKEN` is unset**, mirroring `load_config`'s own env-wins precedence, so `test_ui_auth_token.py::test_env_var_overrides_config` (which sets the env var and relies on the *real* `load_config` to enforce auth) is preserved. Tests that `patch("ui.server.load_config", …)` themselves — the entire `test_ui_auth_token.py` suite — replace the wrapper during their `with` block, so real auth enforcement is unaffected and no test-file skip is needed; `ui_token` is consumed only by `_resolve_ui_token`, so blanking it touches nothing else. *Tests (TDD):* the previously-401 `setup/*` cohort goes green with `.env` sourced; `test_ui_auth_token.py` stays fully green (auth still enforced); **1 new pin** `test_config_sourced_token_neutralized_but_env_token_enforced` locks the config-vs-env distinction. With this fixture plus the C3-05 and shadow fixes above, the full UI suite is now **2148 passed / 0 failed** (22 infra/browser skips: 11 `test_browser_*` that skip when the dashboard isn't running on `:18790`, 11 `test_skill_mode_*` manual-infra) and the pipeline suite **1233 passed / 0 failed** — superseding the C3-05 entry's interim "63 → 61" count, which was measured before this auth-hermeticity fix. *Changed:* `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_ui_auth_token.py`.

- **C3-05 — `/api/setup/launch` and `/api/setup/switch-project` left a stale `RUNNING` `pipeline_state.json` on disk when the orchestrator spawn failed.** Both endpoints write a fresh `RUNNING` state *before* spawning (T6.3 write-then-act — the child reads project state at startup, so it must be committed first), but neither undid that write when `_spawn_orchestrator` returned `{"ok": False}`. The result was a `pipeline_state.json` claiming `RUNNING` with no orchestrator process behind it; the dashboard would render a live run that does not exist, and recovery required a manual reset. *Fix:* a new best-effort, idempotent `_rollback_pipeline_state(path)` helper (`ui/server.py`, beside `_write_json_atomic`) removes the just-written file on the spawn-failure branch — **unconditionally** in `launch`, and **guarded by `_revive_id is None`** in `switch-project` so the `--revive` path (which *skips* the pre-spawn write to preserve a parked escalation's escalated-phase state) never deletes that state on a transient spawn failure. Write-before-spawn is unchanged: deferring the write would reintroduce the startup race T6.3 closed and break the still-green `test_defensive_p6_server_liveness.py::test_launch_writes_state_before_spawn_and_confirms` (asserts `order[:2] == ["write","spawn"]`). *Tests (TDD):* the two long-failing `tests/test_defensive_c3_05.py::*::test_state_file_absent_when_spawn_fails` now pass; both `test_state_file_written_when_spawn_succeeds` sanity tests and all 12 `test_defensive_p6_server_liveness.py` tests stay green; **1 new guard test** `TestC305SwitchProjectStateNotWrittenOnSpawnFail::test_revive_path_state_preserved_when_spawn_fails` locks the `_revive_id is None` condition (a revive-path spawn failure must leave the pre-existing state byte-for-byte intact — it would fail against an unconditional rollback). Full UI suite **63 → 61** failures with no new reds (verified by stashing the fix and re-running clean HEAD; the c3_05 file is now 4/4 green). **Corrects** the post-migration portability-audit entry below, which characterized these two tests as *"stale … pin[ning] the pre-T6.3 'no state write on spawn failure' contract"*: they are **not** stale — they encode the spawn-failure *rollback* contract (compatible with write-before-spawn), which was simply unimplemented until this change. *Changed:* `ui/server.py`, `tests/test_defensive_c3_05.py`, `CLAUDE.md`. *Callouts (not actioned):* resume / git-recover spawn paths read-modify-write existing state (no fresh `RUNNING` stamp), so a spawn failure there correctly leaves prior state — out of scope, correct as-is; a mid-write failure in `_write_json_atomic` can leave a stray `*.tmp` (pre-existing, unrelated to this rollback).

- **The two `test_ui_design_system_restrictions` shadow greps were red on every clean checkout — the committed Recent-projects dropdown carried a banned heavy drop shadow.** `test_no_drop_shadows_on_panels` and `test_no_shadow_on_modal` both flagged `shadow-xl` on the `ServerPathInput` recents listbox (`ui/index.html` — `shadow-xl shadow-black/50`), which the design rule (`autodev/docs/AUTODEV-UI-PRD.md` — *"No: … Drop shadows on every element"*) forbids; these are the reds the post-migration portability-audit entry below tracked as a deferred callout. *Decision:* the rule's intent is no faked depth on flat panels/cards, but a floating overlay legitimately wants subtle elevation — so rather than weaken the guard with a dropdown exception, the markup was brought into compliance. *Fix:* `shadow-xl shadow-black/50` → `shadow-md` (a subtle, test-permitted shadow already used on the Ideas chat scroll-to-bottom button; only `shadow-lg/xl/2xl` are banned). The dropdown keeps its `border` + distinct `bg` for separation, and `shadow-md` now matches the component's own "soft shadow" code comment. No test weakened or deleted — the two existing greps are the red→green anchor and remain as guards. *Tests:* `tests/test_ui_design_system_restrictions.py` 6 passed (was 2 failed / 4 passed). *Changed:* `ui/index.html` (one line). *Callouts (not actioned):* both shadow tests are mis-scoped — each greps the whole file rather than the "panels"/"modal" their names imply, and they overlap on `xl`/`2xl` (a test-quality cleanup for a later pass, out of scope here); the PRD line needs no change since the enforced contract bans only heavy shadows and `shadow-md` complies; the 2 `test_defensive_c3_05` spawn-fail reds are resolved by the C3-05 entry above.

- **The 75-min sentinel infrastructure backstop was a hardcoded literal — on local-model hardware it starved legitimately-working reviewers.** Observed live (Minecraft WORLD-E1, 2026-06-11): three consecutive reviewer attempts hit the 4500 s `poll_for_sentinel` backstop with the Tier A activity stamp refreshing the whole time — the agent was alive and mid-review, not hung; each long-context model call on the single-GPU llama host cost ~4.7 min and a thorough pass needs ~16 turns, so the cap was arithmetically unreachable and the phase escalated as "reviewer produced no output" (×3, then `Reviewer sentinel timeout cap reached`). The backstop exists for the **zombie-streaming** case (gateway alive, agent never finishing); actual agent death is already caught by `AUTODEV_STALL_TIMEOUT_*` (stamp silence) and `AUTODEV_STARTUP_GRACE_*` (no first activity), so a longer cap does not delay death detection. *Fix:* new `_infra_backstop_seconds(env_name, default_str)` helper (parsing mirrors the stall/grace helpers exactly: unset/garbage → default, min 1 s) + the three poll sites resolve `AUTODEV_INFRA_BACKSTOP_{PLANNER,EXECUTOR,REVIEWER}` (default `4500`, unchanged) instead of the literal. The effective value already surfaces per-attempt in the `[POLL][CONFIG]` log line and the `poll_start` event's `infra_backstop` field — no new observability needed. *Tests (TDD, red→green — 8 new in `test_orchestrator_poll_config_logging.py`):* helper defined / default / env override / garbage fallback / min-clamp, plus a per-agent source-level guard that each poll site resolves via the helper and no `_{agent}_backstop = 4500` literal remains. Full pipeline suite 1233 green. *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py`, `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_poll_config_logging.py`, `CLAUDE.md` (constants table + env rows), `.env` (operator value `10800` for all three roles on this host). *Callouts (not actioned):* reviewer attempts restart the whole review from scratch each retry — partial-review carryover across attempts would cut the wall-clock cost of a timeout but is a design change; `install.sh`'s comment-only Tier A `.env` block does not yet mention the new knobs; the per-turn ~4.7 min latency itself is a llama-swap/llama.cpp serving concern (one resident model, cross-agent swaps evict the 27B) outside this repo.

- **Gateway-abort client read unsolicited event frames as RPC responses — every live `sessions.abort` reported FAILED while the gateway had actually aborted the run.** The OpenClaw gateway multiplexes unsolicited `event` frames (`health`, presence, …) onto the same WebSocket that carries request/response traffic, but all three frame waits in `_attempt_abort_once` (`autodev/pipeline/webhook_client.py`) were bare `ws.recv()` calls that treated whatever arrived next as the awaited frame. Observed live (Minecraft REND-E6, 2026-06-11 02:42): a `health` event landed between the `sessions.abort` request and its response, was parsed as the response, and the abort logged `unexpected response` → FAILED — **every `abort_attempted` event in the run carried `result=FAILED`**, so prior attempts kept streaming under each retry (zombie-session overlap, the exact CORE-E6 cascade class the abort path exists to prevent; implicated as the enabler of the REND-E6 repo-destruction incident). *Fix:* new `_recv_expected_frame(ws, session_key, deadline, expect, label)` — reads frames until one matches the expected shape (`{"type": "event", "event": "connect.challenge"}` for the handshake challenge; `{"type": "res", "id": <request id>}` for the connect and abort responses — one request is in flight at a time, so id-matching is exact), **skipping** non-matching `event` frames (debug-logged) and failing the attempt on a non-matching `res` frame (a real protocol violation: nothing else was requested). A wall-clock deadline (`time.monotonic() + timeout_seconds`, shared across the attempt's three waits) bounds the skip loop — the per-recv socket timeout alone cannot, because a gateway emitting events faster than that timeout resets it on every frame. All three recv sites route through the helper; behavior on quiet sockets is byte-identical. *Tests (TDD, red→green — 4 new, 1 replaced):* `test_succeeds_with_health_event_before_abort_response` (the live failure, red pre-fix), `test_succeeds_with_events_interleaved_at_every_wait` (catches a fix applied to only one recv site, red pre-fix), `test_event_flood_bounded_by_deadline` (patched `time.monotonic`; guards the new loop against unbounded skipping), `test_skips_unknown_event_before_challenge_and_proceeds`; `test_returns_false_when_first_frame_is_not_challenge` **replaced** by `test_returns_false_when_res_frame_arrives_before_challenge` — its premise (any non-challenge first frame is fatal) was exactly the bug for event frames; the protocol-violation invariant it guarded is preserved for `res` frames. Full pipeline suite 1225 green. *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/webhook_client.py`, `autodev/tests/test_webhook_client_abort.py`. *Callouts (not actioned):* a properly-aborted attempt's `agent_end` backstop can still write a fresh `.done` into the *next* attempt's poll window (observed as a 4-second `ERR_FILE_MISSING` gate fail on REND-E6 executor attempt 2) — the `min_sentinel_mtime` guard admits it because it *is* fresh; needs a design pass on the abort→cleanup ordering. Blame layer 2/3 classifies infra-class gate errors (`ERR_GIT_DIFF_FAILED`, `ERR_DELETION_CHECK_CRASHED`) as `impl` by default, burning the impl-blame escalation cap on infrastructure faults. Setup preflight will `git init` any writable directory the operator points it at — including a parent directory of existing projects (observed live: `/home/x/projects` itself got a `preflight: initial commit`); a containment guardrail (refuse paths that contain other git repos / the configured projects) is worth a future pass.

- **Post-migration portability audit (Raspberry Pi / Python 3.11 → Ubuntu 24.04 x86_64 / Python 3.12): broken dependency pins, residual hardcoded home paths, broken service-file launch form, repo hygiene.** **(1) Pins:** `PyYAML==6.0` → `6.0.2` — 6.0 ships no cp312+ wheels and its sdist build fails under Cython 3 (the `cython_sources` AttributeError), so `pip install -r requirements.txt` failed on any Python ≥ 3.12; `websocket-client==1.2.3` (2021) → `1.8.0` (pure-Python so it *installed*, but four years stale; the gateway-abort path uses only the stable `WebSocket.connect(header=, suppress_origin=)`/send/recv API, unchanged across the bump — `test_webhook_client_abort.py` green). All other pins (fastapi/uvicorn/aiohttp/requests/python-multipart/pytest/httpx) are 2025-era and verified clean-installing on 3.12. **(2) Paths:** the two *functional* hardcoded home paths that escaped the earlier path remediation are now derived — the `validate-repo-path` error-message example in `ui/server.py` builds from `os.path.expanduser("~")`, and `test_escalation_docs.py`'s `WEBHOOK` default resolves the in-repo `autodev/pipeline/webhook_client.py` (was `/home/pi/.openclaw/webhook_client.py`, which skipped the webhook-prompt contract test on every machine but the original Pi); un-skipping it surfaced that its assertion still pinned the pre-F13 "peer/TOOLS.md" contract, now updated to the NOTIFY-only contract (names the Signal/message-tool channel, forbids writing `escalation_output`). Repo-wide audit found no other functional absolute paths: remaining `/home/pi` strings are deliberate ❌-WRONG doc examples (agent `TOOLS.md`s, a SKILL.md sample) or incident-history comments; `ui/config.json` and `.cursor/mcp.json` are confirmed gitignored-not-committed (`ui/config.example.json` is the committed template), and the committed `autodev/config/mcp.json` carries no machine paths. **(3) Canonical launch — `python -m ui.server` from the repo root.** `ui/server.py` gains the missing `main()` / `__main__` block (loopback bind on the configured port), closing the access-token entry's "pre-existing broken ExecStart (no `__main__`)" callout; both service files (`ui/autodev-ui.service` ExecStart, `ui/com.autodev.ui.plist` ProgramArguments) switch from the script form `python3 ui/server.py` — which dies with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ui'` (package-absolute imports) — to `python3 -m ui.server`; the two drift-guard lint tests now pin the module form; README documents the command and the failure mode of the script form. **(4) Hygiene:** `.gitignore` gains `.ruff_cache/` and `.claude/settings.local.json` (`__pycache__/`, `.autodev/`, `*.log` already covered). **(5) Git identity is now a stated, enforced install prerequisite.** The pipeline commits inside project repos (init commits, phase merges, the executor agent's own commits), so a fresh machine with no `user.name`/`user.email` broke mid-pipeline at the first commit — it only ever worked because the original dev box had `~/.gitconfig` set out of band, and on the new VM it surfaced as 6 failing init/queue-add tests. Three layers: `install.sh` probes `git config --global user.name`/`user.email` right after the git-binary check and **fails fast** with the two exact `git config --global …` commands (prerequisite check, not a new numbered step — the 14-step contract holds); the README prerequisites bullet documents the same commands; and the server is now **hermetic** regardless — a new `_ensure_repo_git_identity(repo_path)` (called after both `git init` sites — preflight auto-repair, Mode A — and before the Mode B structure commit) sets a **repo-local** fallback identity (`Lullabeast Pipeline <pipeline@lullabeast.local>`) only when no identity resolves (repo-local/global/system never overridden), so init-time and every later pipeline commit in that repo cannot die on identity. Project-scoped-at-init was chosen over relying solely on global config per the hermeticity argument; repos added to the queue from outside the init paths still rely on the install-time global check. *Tests (9 new):* `tests/test_init_project_git_identity.py` (fallback set when none resolves / resolvable identity never overridden / Mode A + Mode B init succeed with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` pointed at an empty file and author/committer env scrubbed) and `tests/test_install_git_identity.py` (static lint: both keys probed, missing-identity branch `fail`s, message carries the verbatim fix commands, check precedes dependency install). *Also:* `test_run_started_at_lifecycle.py`'s park-snapshot source-grep window widened 1000 → 1600 chars — the snapshot dict's inline comments had pushed `run_started_at` past the window, a latent red test on every clean checkout. *Tests:* pipeline suite 1221 green; UI suite 2142 green (was 2127 + the 6 git-identity-blocked failures now fixed + 9 new), remaining failures reproduced identically on clean HEAD and unrelated to this pass: 2 design-system shadow greps vs the committed Recent-projects dropdown, 2 `test_defensive_c3_05` spawn-fail tests that still pin the pre-T6.3 "no state write on spawn failure" contract (T6.3 deliberately writes state *before* spawn), and 9 browser-test errors from playwright not being a declared dev dep. *Changed:* `requirements.txt`, `ui/requirements.txt`, `ui/server.py`, `install.sh`, `ui/autodev-ui.service`, `ui/com.autodev.ui.plist`, `tests/test_infra3_systemd_unit.py`, `tests/test_infra3_launchd_plist.py`, `tests/test_init_project_git_identity.py` (new), `tests/test_install_git_identity.py` (new), `autodev/agents/escalation/tests/test_escalation_docs.py`, `autodev/tests/test_run_started_at_lifecycle.py`, `.gitignore`, `README.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new). *Callouts (not actioned):* the 2 design-system reds and the 2 stale `c3_05` spawn-fail tests predate this pass and are tracked separately; a stray root `package-lock.json` (npm run at repo root) is untracked litter, safe to delete.

- **Roadmap phase-stats regex rejected plain-number phase IDs (`INFRA-1`) — PROGRESS blank everywhere for such projects.** Found by the first live E2E queue smoke run (Cursor MCP-browser, autodev-smoke → `PIPELINE_COMPLETE`): every PROGRESS/phases failure in the run traced to one cause — `_PHASE_LINE_RE` demanded a letter-prefixed phase number (`[A-Z]\d+`, e.g. `CORE-E6`), while the canonical parsers (`gate_scripts/phase_resolver.py`, `ui/roadmap_parser.py`) accept any backticked ID. The smoke project's `INFRA-1`/`CORE-1` therefore read as **zero phases** (`phases_total: 0`) in `GET /api/queue`, the snapshot endpoint, the repo-roadmap-hint, and — worse — `_validate_roadmap_content` would call such a roadmap `no_phase_lines` invalid, even though the pipeline itself runs it to completion. *Fix:* the shared regex moves to named groups with the ID grammar relaxed to `[A-Z]+-[A-Z]?\d+` (accepts both `CORE-E6` and `INFRA-1`; priority tokens stay strict), and `_roadmap_phase_checkbox_stats` now counts `completed` from the **matched phase lines'** checkbox (previously it counted every `- [x] ` line in the document, so a stray checked task bullet could inflate completed past total — latent second bug fixed in the same pass); the validator reads `m.group("id")`. *Tests (TDD, red→green — 4):* stats accept plain-number IDs / completed counts only phase lines / queue enrichment with plain-number roadmap / validator accepts plain-number IDs (`test_p0_validate_roadmap_strict.py`); 175 tests green across all regex consumers (validator strict + setup-validate + repo-hint + w3b + queue + snapshot suites; the start-of-line anchor pin still passes). *Changed:* `ui/server.py`. *Callout:* priority tokens (`LOW|MEDIUM|HIGH|CRITICAL`) remain stricter than `phase_resolver` (which accepts any priority text) — not demonstrated as a problem; widen only if a real roadmap hits it.
