  - **T3.7 — unique-temp atomic `session.json` writer.** `_atomic_write_json_file` used a fixed `<path>.tmp` temp name, so two concurrent `session.json` writers (a chat turn racing the auto-fired readiness job, or a GET /session salvage write) collided — one truncated the other's temp before `os.replace`, corrupting the file (then read back as `None`, silently reverting the conversation to empty). It now uses `mkstemp` (unique temp in the same dir, cleaned on failure), hardening all 17 callers; the two inline fixed-`.tmp` writers (`/convert`, `post_ideas`) were refactored to call it (no duplicated idiom left).
  - **T3.8 — chat-send scrubs a stale `.done` before polling.** Turn numbers are reused across retries, so if attempt 1 wrote `turns/{n}.done` late (after a 408), attempt 2 (same n) latched onto it via the poll's bare existence check and instantly "succeeded", returning attempt 1's stale reply. The chat-send handler now calls the existing, unit-tested `_ideas_scrub_stale_turn_artifacts(idea_dir, turn_n, attempt_start_wall)` immediately before the poll — mirroring the unlink `/convert` and `/fix-roadmap-format` already do (the helper was previously referenced only from tests).
  - **T3.9 — SSE event stream no longer drops repeated events; dead cross-drain code removed.** RV-1.6 anchored at the fallback (ring-buffer) branch's `for q in _sse_clients: q.get_nowait()` — which drains **every** client's queue — plus an `if event != last_event` dedup that silently dropped legitimately-repeated events. That code lived in `_stream_events`, which is **defined but never called** (the live `events_stream` endpoint uses its own inline generator that already reads only its own queue). The fix: the live generator now delivers every event from the client's own queue (the dedup drop removed), and the dead `_stream_events` function + its exclusively-supporting `_sse_notify_event` (never `.set()` anywhere) were removed per the no-dead-code standard. The roadmap's assumption that `_stream_events` was the live fallback was corrected.

  *Changed:* `ui/server.py` only — `load_config` (T3.1), `_read_json_file` (T3.2), `_run_preflight_checks` (T3.3), `post_ideas_convert` + `post_ideas_fix_roadmap_format` (T3.4 + T3.7 writer refactor), `_write_escalation_files` (T3.5), new `_atomic_symlink_swap` + three call sites (T3.6), `_atomic_write_json_file` + `post_ideas` (T3.7), `post_ideas_message` (T3.8), `events_stream` inline generator + removal of dead `_stream_events`/`_sse_notify_event` (T3.9). *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `tests/test_phase3_server_hardening.py` (19 tests: 16 new-behavior red + 3 characterization). *Existing tests updated to the new mechanism (not silenced):* the obsolete remove-then-symlink mocks across `tests/test_api_setup_launch.py` (24 boilerplate `os.symlink`/`os.remove`/`lexists` trios collapsed to `_atomic_symlink_swap` stubs; the dead `_symlink_patches()` helper removed; the three `TestSymlinkSetting` assertion tests rewritten to assert on `_atomic_symlink_swap` — incl. replacing the now-obsolete remove-before-symlink-ordering test with an atomic-single-call test), plus the same trio in `tests/test_queue_api.py` and `tests/test_defensive_c3_05.py`. Targeted regression scope **690 passed, 0 regressions** (4 `test_browser_path_selector.py` preflight failures were confirmed *not* a Phase 3 regression — they reproduce identically on a clean baseline; root cause was a pre-existing stale brand string from the Lullabeast rename, fixed separately above). *Callouts (not actioned):* (a) `_tail_events_file` is now reachable only from tests (its sole production caller `_stream_events` was removed) but remains independently tested — kept as a tested utility, flagged for future rewiring/removal; (b) several touched sites still use the deprecated `datetime.utcnow()` — a broad migration is out of scope.

- **Phase 1 gate output & boundary hardening (T1.1–T1.5).** The defensive-hardening roadmap's first tranche — the deterministic gates now degrade gracefully on wrong-typed MiniMax JSON instead of crashing, and the path-boundary security guard matches its documented `realpath` contract. Five fixes, TDD red→green.

  - **T1.1 — executor manifest type-coercion.** `executor_gate.py` built `expected_files = data.get("file_manifest", []) + data.get("tests_written", [])`; `.get(k, [])` only rescues an *absent* key, so a present-but-non-list value (MiniMax emits `"foo.py"` instead of `["foo.py"]`) raised `TypeError` — crashing the gate **before the MiniMax file-deletion guard ran** and writing no `last_error_code` (an opaque FAIL with the deletion guard silently skipped for that attempt). The gate now validates both fields are lists and, on a non-list, records `ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED` and returns `FAIL` (fresh-session retry, self-heal feedback intact).
  - **T1.2 — reviewer non-dict verdict + non-string evidence-path guards.** `(data.get("behavioral_verification") or {}).get("verdict")` (and the regression sibling) — `or {}` only rescues a *falsy* value, so a truthy non-dict crashed `.get`. The behavioural line is reachable with a non-dict only on a **non-behavioural phase** (on a behavioural phase the upstream `_check_behavioral_verification` contract check intercepts a non-dict first → `BEHAVIORAL_UNVERIFIED`), where it crashed into a needless human escalation (`ROUTE_ESCALATE`); both verdict reads are now normalized via `isinstance(...)` (→ `None` / `{}`). The behavioural fix is live; the regression line is defense-in-depth — not independently crash-reachable behind its `requires_regression_verification` short-circuit + the same upstream gate. Separately, the behavioural/regression **evidence** loops passed a truthy non-string `file_or_screenshot_or_log` straight into `os.path.isabs` (`TypeError`); they now reject a non-string with a clean contract problem (→ `BEHAVIORAL_UNVERIFIED` / `REGRESSION_UNVERIFIED`), mirroring `_check_visual_verification`'s existing `isinstance(path, str)` guard.
  - **T1.3 — tdd-list coercion.** A non-list planner `tdd_test_structure` made the coverage comprehension iterate a string per-character (spurious `ERR_TDD_COVERAGE_MISMATCH` warning polluting the gate-feedback channel the reviewer adjudicates) or crash on a non-iterable; it is now coerced to `[]`. The executor's own `tests_written` needs no re-coercion here — its list type is already enforced upstream by the T1.1 guard.
  - **T1.4 — `abspath` → `realpath` path-boundary guard.** CLAUDE.md's Security Constraints state the manifest/evidence boundary is an `os.path.realpath` comparison, but the code used lexical `os.path.abspath` + `commonpath` — so an agent-created symlink **inside** the workspace pointing **outside** it passed the bounds check (and the subsequent `os.path.exists` followed it). Both sides are now `realpath`-resolved across all four boundary loops (executor manifest + behavioral-artifact; reviewer behavioral + regression evidence). Resolving **both** sides is load-bearing: the workspace is itself reached via the `pipeline-project` symlink, so resolving only the target would wrongly reject every legitimate in-workspace path.
  - **T1.5 — cross-OS separator hygiene.** `WORKSPACE_DIR.rstrip("/")` → `.rstrip(os.sep)` in the `pipeline_state.json` parent-dir derivation (POSIX-equivalent on all supported targets — Linux/macOS/WSL2; correctness hygiene matching the `os.sep` sibling already in the file).

  *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/gate_scripts/executor_gate.py` (T1.1 validate-then-concat; T1.3 coercion; T1.4 manifest + behavioral loops `realpath`; T1.5 `os.sep`), `autodev/pipeline/gate_scripts/reviewer_gate.py` (T1.2 line-502/514 `isinstance` normalization + behavioural/regression evidence string-guard; T1.4 both evidence loops `realpath`; two helper docstrings). *Tests (TDD, red→green — 11 red + 1 characterization):* `autodev/tests/test_executor_gate_behavioral_artifacts.py` (+6: non-list `file_manifest`/`tests_written` → `ERR_VALIDATION_FAILED`; string + int `tdd_test_structure`; manifest + behavioral-artifact symlink-escape → `ERR_PATH_TRAVERSAL`), `autodev/tests/test_reviewer_gate_behavioral_verification.py` (+3: non-string evidence path, symlink-escape, non-dict block ignored on a non-behavioural phase), `autodev/tests/test_reviewer_gate_regression_prior_phase.py` (+3: non-string + symlink-escape regression evidence; non-dict-block characterization locking the short-circuit invariant). One stale static-lint assertion was **updated, not silenced**: `test_planner_executor_reviewer_artifacts_paths.py` pinned the old `os.path.abspath(os.path.join(WORKSPACE_DIR, relative_path))` boundary literal → updated to the `os.path.realpath(...)` form (its I/O-vs-boundary path-separation intent is unchanged). Pipeline suite **1070 green**. *Docs:* two reviewer-gate helper docstrings (`realpath` both-sides + string requirement); `CLAUDE.md`'s Security-Constraints `realpath` claim is now accurate (the wording reconciliation is owned by roadmap Phase 7 T7.4). *Callout (out of scope — not actioned):* `_check_visual_verification`'s `visual_smoke_artifacts` path has a string guard but **no** `commonpath` workspace-boundary check (behavioural/regression evidence do) — a real asymmetry, tracked as a separate follow-up.

- **Context-overflow discarded-verdict race closed (Layer 2 — overflow-aware sentinel hold).** When a pipeline agent's turn died mid-tool-loop with `stopReason:"error"` / `"Context overflow: estimated context size exceeds safe threshold during tool loop."`, the autodev-pipeline-signals plugin's `agent_end` backstop wrote the `{agent}_output.done` sentinel **unconditionally** — so `poll_for_sentinel` returned `succeeded`, the gate read a missing verdict (reviewer `CONTRACT_FAILURE` / executor `ERR_FILE_MISSING`), and the orchestrator escalated/retried. But OpenClaw then auto-compacts and **resumes the same session**, which writes a valid verdict seconds-to-minutes later — into the void (observed live on solitaire2 CORE-E6: escalated 14:40:00, a clean PASS landed 14:43:13 and was discarded; the phase had in fact passed). *Fix:* `poll_for_sentinel` gains an optional `sentinel_acceptor` predicate, consulted **only** when a fresh `.done` is observed (after the `min_sentinel_mtime` guard); a new `Orchestrator._make_overflow_aware_acceptor(role, session_key, attempt_start)` returns a predicate that **holds** the sentinel (keeps polling) while the session's last assistant row is a recoverable overflow and no verdict is on disk — accepting the instant `{role}_output.json` is fresh+parseable, when the hold budget is spent, or for any non-overflow end. The hold is bounded by the existing stall / startup-grace / 75-min backstop timers **plus** a new `AUTODEV_OVERFLOW_HOLD_BUDGET` (default 900 s) ceiling, and a raising acceptor fails open. Wired at the three phase-agent poll sites (planner/executor/reviewer); the non-fatal completion-review poll is deliberately excluded (no verdict, no stall stamp). **Every non-overflow termination accepts immediately, so the common path is byte-identical.** New `sentinel_overflow_hold` pipeline event (one per hold episode). *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/sentinel_poller.py` (`sentinel_acceptor` kwarg + hold branch + docstring), `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (module helpers `_is_recoverable_context_overflow` / `_resolve_session_jsonl_path` / `_verdict_is_fresh_and_parseable`, constant `_OVERFLOW_HOLD_BUDGET_SECONDS` + `_resolve_overflow_hold_budget()`, the `_make_overflow_aware_acceptor` factory, three wired poll sites, the completion-review exclusion comment). *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `autodev/tests/test_sentinel_poller_acceptor.py` (+5) and `autodev/tests/test_overflow_aware_sentinel_hold.py` (+11 — incl. the exact 14:43 verdict-present regression and an OpenClaw-wording drift guard); one proximity-window guard in `test_phase_state_outcome_fields.py` widened 2500→3000 to accommodate the new kwarg inside the reviewer poll call (the reviewer site still records `last_attempt_summary` / `last_poll_reason` — unchanged intent). Pipeline suite **1058 green**. *Docs:* `CLAUDE.md` (Sentinel Polling Rules, event catalogue, Operational Constants), `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md`, `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md`. *Not changed:* the agent JSON-first/sentinel-second contract is unchanged (no agent-doc edits).

- **MISSING_ARTIFACTS executor directive now reaches the executor (dead `artifact_instruction` write retired).** When the reviewer gate returns `MISSING_ARTIFACTS` (the phase archive or metrics row is absent), the orchestrator re-invokes the executor to produce them — but the corrective instruction was written to a phase-state field `artifact_instruction` that **no code ever read** (and no executor `AGENTS.md` mentioned), and the executor webhook was invoked with no `message=`, so the re-invoked executor ran on its generic default prompt — blind to what was missing, apt to re-omit the same artifacts, burn its 2-retry budget, and escalate to a human. This is the executor-side twin of the reviewer's already-fixed `unverified_instruction` dead write. *Fix (mirrors the shipped `reviewer_retry_directive` / `_invoke_reviewer` template):* the handler now writes a one-shot `executor_retry_directive`; a new `_invoke_executor(session_key, token)` helper delivers it as the webhook `message=` and clears it after delivery (one-shot), and the sole executor invocation routes through it. Because `message=` **replaces** the executor's default prompt, the directive was rewritten to be self-contained — it states the prior implementation is preserved on the branch (do not re-implement), points the fresh session at `current_phase.json`, and names the two artifacts + ordering. The file-based `failure_context.json` self-failure-feedback channel is **untouched**: on an ordinary self-failure retry no directive is set, so `_invoke_executor` passes no message and the executor receives its default prompt and reads `failure_context.json` from disk exactly as before. Closes the "explicit deferred follow-up" the C1 reviewer-half fold forecast. *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (new `_invoke_executor` helper; MISSING_ARTIFACTS handler writes `executor_retry_directive` + a self-contained directive + updated comment; executor call site routed through the helper; dead `artifact_instruction` write deleted). *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `autodev/tests/test_missing_artifacts_orchestrator.py` (+5: directive reaches `invoke_agent_webhook(message=…)`; one-shot clear; no-directive → no message [self-failure path intact]; executor-role target; a source removal + self-containment guard). One stale source-grep guard was updated (not silenced): `test_executor_abort_on_retry.py`'s retry-abort regex now matches `self._invoke_executor(` instead of the old `invoke_agent_webhook("executor"` literal — the abort-before-invoke behavior it guards is unchanged. Pipeline suite **1043 green**. *Docs:* `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` §7 (MISSING_ARTIFACTS pseudocode + prose: `artifact_instruction` → `executor_retry_directive`, reframed as `message=`-delivered).

- **`write_state()` no longer crashes on a documented minimal reset (missing `last_action`).** `Orchestrator.write_state()`'s success-path `[INFO]` log line indexed `self.state['last_action']` directly, *after* the atomic `os.replace` had already committed the write. A `pipeline_state.json` hand-reset following the documented checklist (`pipeline_status` / `current_agent` / `current_phase` / `current_phase_raw_id`) omits `last_action`, so the first `write_state()` on orchestrator spawn raised `KeyError: 'last_action'`; the `except` handler then logged a misleading `[ERROR] Failed to write state` and re-raised, crashing `trigger-next` on first spawn (hit live during an E2E smoke-test state reset). The log line now reads both keys via `.get()` so a log statement can never raise after the write has succeeded; the genuine write-failure path (`mkstemp` / `json.dump` / `os.replace`) is unchanged and still re-raises (the existing C3-01 re-raise tests stay green). *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (`write_state` log line + docstring). *Tests (TDD, red→green):* `autodev/tests/test_defensive_c3_01.py` — new `TestWriteStateMissingLastActionDefensive::test_write_state_missing_last_action_does_not_raise`. *Docs:* `CLAUDE.md` reset-protocol + reset-checklist notes.

### Changed

- **Phase 5 state-enum & transition hardening (F5 / F12).** The state-machine audit's final, lowest-risk tranche — the `VALID_STATES` enum and its single writer are now internally consistent and fail loudly. Two fixes, TDD red→green:

  - **F5 — `IDLE` is documented external-only (kept out of `VALID_STATES`).** `CLAUDE.md`'s reset protocol tells operators to set `pipeline_status:"IDLE"`, but `IDLE ∉ VALID_STATES` — a contract that worked only because external resetters write the file directly, and that a future contributor could "helpfully" break by adding `IDLE` to the enum or routing `transition_state("IDLE", …)` through it. `IDLE` stays **out** of `VALID_STATES` (locked decision); a new code comment on the list names it an external-only reset/entry status that is never a `transition_state` target. The roadmap's "resolved to `RUNNING` at startup" wording was **corrected to match the code**: there is no explicit `IDLE → RUNNING` step — startup treats `IDLE` as non-terminal and the first real `transition_state` (`"Invoking Planner"` → `WAITING_FOR_SENTINEL`) overwrites it.
  - **F12 — `transition_state` raises on an invalid target instead of a silent no-op.** It previously did `print(...) + return` (no write) when handed a status outside `VALID_STATES`, leaving a caller's prior `self.state` mutation (e.g. a `current_agent="escalation"` set just before the call) neither persisted nor rolled back. It now **raises `ValueError`**. In the live loop the raise is caught by `run()`'s top-level `except` and routed to escalation (→ `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN`, operator notified); outside the loop (CLI / startup) it surfaces as a traceback. All 62 callers pass valid literals, so no real path changes — this only converts a latent silent failure into a visible one (chosen over a `HALTED_SILENT` fallback, which would route a programming error into the exact silent sink F10/F11 are shrinking). Sequenced after F5 so the stricter check is consistent with `IDLE`'s deliberate exclusion.

  *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (F5 — `VALID_STATES` external-only comment; F12 — `transition_state` raises `ValueError` + expanded docstring), `CLAUDE.md` (reset-protocol `IDLE`-external-only note + `transition_state` raise note), `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (`pipeline_status` schema enum: added the missing `QUEUE_HALTED` + the `IDLE`-external-only note). *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `autodev/tests/test_phase5_state_enum_hardening.py` (+5: `IDLE` absent from `VALID_STATES`; the enum comment documents it; no `transition_state("IDLE")` call site exists repo-wide; an invalid target raises **and** does not write; a valid target still writes). The two behavior tests construct via `Orchestrator.__new__` to unit-test `transition_state` in isolation (hermetic — independent of ambient `OPENCLAW_ROOT`). Pipeline suite **1037 green**; UI suite **1943 passed / 14 skipped**.

- **Phase 4 queue write concurrency (F9 — optimistic version-CAS).** `pipeline_queue.json` is written by two independent OS processes — the UI server (`add`/`delete`/reorder/`parent`/`mode`) and the spawned orchestrator (`ACTIVE`/`COMPLETED`/park/promote) — with no file lock. `os.replace` made each write atomic but last-full-write-wins, so an interleaved UI + orchestrator write **silently dropped one side's update** (the documented "single-writer / alternating windows" assumption was already violated — UI endpoints wrote with no liveness guard before the write). F9 closes that with a `queue_version` compare-and-swap, no new lock. TDD red→green (15 new tests: `autodev/tests/test_queue_version_cas.py`, `tests/test_queue_version_cas_api.py`).

  - **One shared CAS loop, both processes.** New pure primitives in `autodev/pipeline/queue_semantics.py` (already imported by both the orchestrator and the server): `queue_version` key + `read_queue_version` (legacy/missing → 0, additive schema, no migration), `bump_queue_version` (the single +1 site), and `mutate_queue(read_fn, write_fn, current_version_fn, mutate_fn)` — read → capture base version → apply a **pure, idempotent, id-keyed** `mutate_fn` → re-read the on-disk version immediately before `os.replace` → commit `base+1` only if unchanged, else re-read/re-apply/retry (bounded by `QUEUE_MAX_CAS_RETRIES`, 8). `QueueAbort` lets a `mutate_fn` commit nothing (targeted row vanished under it); `QueueVersionConflict` is raised on exhausted contention. Each process drives it through a thin wrapper: orchestrator `_mutate_queue` (+ `_peek_queue_version`) over `_read_queue`/`_write_queue`; server `_mutate_queue_file` (+ `_peek_queue_version`) over `_read_queue_file`/`_write_queue_file`. Version stamping lives only in the two atomic writers, so a direct write still advances the version.

  - **All 24 write statements (21 functions) converted.** Orchestrator (9): `_promote_answered_escalations` (now CAS-writes then rebases the caller's in-memory queue), the four writes inside `_select_next_queue_project`, `_queue_promote_children_after_parent_completed`, `_queue_update_active_entry`, `_queue_park_active_entry`, `_queue_restore_parked_entry_to_active`. Server (15): `patch_queue_mode`, `put_queue_order`, `post_queue_add`, `delete_queue_entry`, `post_queue_clear`, `patch_queue_position`, `patch_queue_parent`, `post_queue_entry_revalidate`, `_queue_demote_stale_active_entries`, `_queue_mark_matching_entry_active`, the three writes inside `_queue_run_trigger_next_logic`, and the two `post_setup_launch` synthetic-entry writes. Each endpoint's validation (404/409/preflight) re-derives **inside** the closure so a retry re-validates against the fresh queue. `post_queue_entry_relaunch` writes nothing — untouched.

  - **Non-idempotent side effects kept outside the retried closure.** The two complex writers interleave effects with the queue write: `_select_next_queue_project` repoints the `pipeline-project` symlink (and later writes `pipeline_state`, the run manifest, the banked command); `_queue_run_trigger_next_logic` calls `_spawn_orchestrator`. Only the id-keyed entry-state tweak runs inside `mutate_queue`; the symlink/spawn/state writes run **once**, outside — so a CAS retry never double-repoints or double-spawns. A new `_skip_and_requeue_group` helper shares the skip logic between the in-memory walk and the CAS re-apply.

  - **Failure surface.** On exhausted contention the orchestrator's best-effort internal writes log the `QueueVersionConflict` (already inside a broad `try/except`); the server maps it to **HTTP 503** (transient, retryable) via one `@app.exception_handler`, and `_maybe_autostart_queue` swallows it so a CAS race during best-effort autostart never 503s the originating `add`/`parent`/`revalidate` (whose own write already committed). The two best-effort server queue-alignment helpers swallow it too.

  - **Residual (documented, deferred).** Lock-free CAS on a bare file leaves a microsecond window between the pre-write version check and `os.replace`; closing it fully needs a lock (rejected — nesting against `pipeline.lock`) or a per-write `queue_write_token` nonce (deferred follow-up). Negligible for two low-frequency writers on one host. Two stale tests were updated to the new mechanism (not silenced): a `_write_queue(queue_data)` source-grep → `_mutate_queue(_activate)`, and a `_promote_answered_escalations` unit test now seeds the queue on disk (the CAS re-reads it — production callers always do).

  *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/queue_semantics.py` (CAS primitives), `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (`_read_queue`/`_write_queue` docstrings + version stamping; `_peek_queue_version`/`_mutate_queue`; 9 write sites; `_skip_and_requeue_group`), `ui/server.py` (`_write_queue_file` version stamping; `_peek_queue_version`/`_mutate_queue_file`; 15 write sites; `QueueVersionConflict`→503 handler; `_maybe_autostart_queue` guard), `CLAUDE.md` (Queue System concurrency paragraph + two "single-writer preserved" assurances), `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (new *Queue write concurrency (F9)* subsection + promote sentence). *Tests:* `autodev/tests/test_queue_version_cas.py` (+8), `tests/test_queue_version_cas_api.py` (+7); updated `test_w2a_run_manifest.py`, `test_orchestrator_nuclear_reset.py`.

- **Phase 3 gate & resolver fail-closed consistency (F4 / F6 / F7).** The state-machine audit's third tranche — every deterministic gate/resolver now fails in one predictable direction and a safety guard can no longer silently turn itself off. Three fixes, TDD red→green:

  - **F4 — a `phase_resolver` failure now escalates instead of running blind.** `phase_resolver` exits `1` on roadmap-not-found / non-absolute path / write failure, but both consumers handled only rc `0`/`2`. On rc `1` (or unexpected output) the **startup** path (`_run_startup_planner_phase_zero_and_branch`) invoked the planner blind (empty `raw_id`, no `current_phase.json`), and the **advance** path — the shared `_advance_to_next_pending_phase` helper (phase-complete / SKIP / PROCEED) — fell through to a silent `return "break"`, dead-ending the orchestrator at status `RUNNING` with no phase and no operator signal. Both sites now route an unactionable resolver verdict — rc 1, an unexpected rc/output, **or** the subprocess crashing — to escalation via the established idiom (`current_agent="escalation"` + honest `escalation_trigger_reason` carrying the rc + stderr, `last_error_code=ERR_PHASE_RESOLVER_FAILED`, `transition_state("RUNNING")`), reusing the main-loop escalation dispatch (no new escalation code). The advance helper returns `"continue"` and the startup returns `"enter_main_loop"`, so the next loop iteration fires the webhook + advisory + queue-park. This completes the F4 promise the Phase 2 entry flagged ("resolver-error handling … F4 (Phase 3) will make that escalate uniformly"). The startup `except` (resolver subprocess crash) escalates too (locked decision) rather than its old "Proceeding; planner must self-orient" blind path.
  - **F6 — the MiniMax deletion guard now fails closed on *every* error class.** `executor_gate.py`'s unaccounted-deletion check already failed closed on a missing `phase_base_commit` (`ERR_MISSING_BASE_COMMIT`) and a `git diff` returning non-zero (`ERR_GIT_DIFF_FAILED`), but its surrounding `except Exception` only printed `[GATE WARN] … skipping` and **fell through to `return "PASS"`** — silently disabling the guard whenever git raised (missing, killed, timeout, OSError). It now records `ERR_DELETION_CHECK_CRASHED` and returns `FAIL` (fresh-session retry), matching its two siblings. CLAUDE.md forbids weakening this guard — the crash path was the one hole left.
  - **F7 — the gate-contract docs now describe the *two* signalling conventions (doc-only).** CLAUDE.md's "Gate Script Interface Contract" documented a single universal exit-code / JSON-on-stdout protocol that only the resolver/init gates (`phase_resolver.py`, `repo_init_check.py`) actually follow. The verdict gates (planner, executor, reviewer) always **exit 0** and signal via a stdout verdict string, with failure detail on side channels (`executor_gate_detail.json` / `gate_warnings.json` / `last_error_code`). The contract was rewritten to state both conventions; the false universal sentence and the MiniMax-section "exit 1 with this error code in the JSON" wording were corrected, and PIPELINE-SPEC's "fails closed with exit code 1" for `ERR_MISSING_BASE_COMMIT` was fixed (the executor gate returns `FAIL`/exit 0; the code rides `last_error_code`). The three runner wrappers were **deliberately left unchanged** (they already fail safe on `CalledProcessError`; capturing stdout is purely diagnostic — locked doc-only decision).

  *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (F4 — `_advance_to_next_pending_phase` resolver-error tail → escalation + returns `"continue"`; `_run_startup_planner_phase_zero_and_branch` `else` + `except` → escalation; both docstrings), `autodev/pipeline/gate_scripts/executor_gate.py` (F6 — deletion-check `except` fails closed with `ERR_DELETION_CHECK_CRASHED`), `CLAUDE.md` (F7 contract rewrite + MiniMax-guard wording/fail-closed classes + F4 narrative), `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (F7 `ERR_MISSING_BASE_COMMIT` wording + F4 advance-helper escalation edge).

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* `autodev/tests/test_phase_advance_helper.py` — **replaced** `test_advance_resolver_error_returns_break` (it encoded the bug) with `test_advance_resolver_error_routes_to_escalation` + added `test_advance_resolver_unexpected_output_escalates` (extended the `_ResolverRun` stub to carry stderr); new `autodev/tests/test_startup_phase_zero_escalation.py` (rc-1 + subprocess-crash both escalate); `autodev/tests/test_executor_gate_unaccounted_deletion.py` — new `test_deletion_check_crash_returns_fail_not_skip` (the rc≠0 + missing-base siblings stay green); new `tests/test_gate_contract_doc_consistency.py` (CLAUDE.md names both conventions; PIPELINE-SPEC no longer claims the executor gate exits 1). Pipeline suite **1024 green**; UI suite **1935 passed / 15 skipped**.

- **Phase 2 operator-command & terminal-state recovery (F3 / F10 / F11).** The state-machine audit's second tranche — the operator control/recovery surface. Three fixes, TDD red→green:

  - **F3 — `SKIP` / `PROCEED` now re-resolve the next pending phase instead of re-running the just-closed one.** Both handlers set `current_phase=0` but never cleared `current_phase_raw_id` or re-ran `phase_resolver`, so the planner re-ran the stale phase; worst case, `PROCEED` on a chronically-failing phase looped straight back to escalation — the opposite of its "advance past this phase" UI promise. The proven reviewer-PASS advance sequence was extracted into a shared `Orchestrator._advance_to_next_pending_phase(*, trigger)` helper (returns `"continue"`/`"break"` for the caller to apply to the main loop) and is now called from **all three** sites (phase-complete, SKIP, PROCEED) so they cannot drift. The helper also clears the per-phase pipeline artifacts (removing a latent stale-`failure_context.json` leak on SKIP/PROCEED). Resolver-error handling is unchanged (`"break"`) for all three callers — F4 (Phase 3) will make that escalate uniformly.
  - **F10 — the two *silent* `HALTED_SILENT` sinks now escalate (notify ⟺ escalate).** Outbound Signal is owned solely by the escalation agent, so "the operator was notified" and "the pipeline escalated" are the same event — a halt-and-notify design is incoherent. **(b)** An unknown/unrecognised reviewer-gate verdict previously did `transition_state("HALTED_SILENT"); return`; it now sets `current_agent="escalation"` + `transition_state("RUNNING", …)` + `continue` (the established CONTRACT_FAILURE / `*_UNVERIFIED` cap idiom), so the next loop iteration fires the full escalation dispatch (advisory + Signal + dashboard answer). The diagnostic `_rev_next` default flipped `"halted"`→`"escalation"` to keep the `[REVIEWER_GATE]` log / `reviewer_verdict` event honest. **(a)** Activity-stamp-init failure: `_init_activity_stamp_or_halt` was **renamed** `_init_activity_stamp_or_escalate` and now routes to escalation (same idiom) instead of halting; its three call sites (planner/executor/reviewer) `continue` on a False return instead of `return`. If the workspace is so broken that the dispatch's own `phase_state` write also fails, the run degrades to the existing escalation-delivery-failed `HALTED_SILENT` — an honest silent halt (the `stamp_init_failed` event is still recorded). `HALTED_SILENT` is now reached only by genuine escalation-**delivery** failure or an unhandled exception.
  - **F11 — `HALTED_SILENT` is operator-recoverable from the UI.** `POST /api/resume-ready` (previously `STOPPED`-only) now accepts `STOPPED` **or** `HALTED_SILENT`, transitioning to `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN` + `current_agent="escalation"` so the operator can issue a recovery command without the phase-destroying `git-recover` (which stays as the heavy fallback). The dashboard's `StoppedRecoveryPanel` is reused for `HALTED_SILENT` at all three render sites (CurrentPhasePanel gate, the queue/hub `isActiveStopped` card, and the header Resume button), with distinct "Intervention Required" / "Halted during phase" copy driven by a new `pipeline_status` prop.

  *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (new `_advance_to_next_pending_phase` helper + 3 call sites; unknown-verdict → escalation + `_rev_next` default; `_init_activity_stamp_or_halt`→`_init_activity_stamp_or_escalate` routes to escalation + 3 call sites `return`→`continue`), `ui/server.py` (`post_resume_ready` accepts `HALTED_SILENT`), `ui/index.html` (3 render gates widened + `pipeline_status`-driven panel wording), `CLAUDE.md` + `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (Terminal states, stamp bootstrap, event catalogue, SKIP/PROCEED, resume-ready, Silent Halt Behavior).

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `autodev/tests/test_phase_advance_helper.py` (helper signature + per-outcome behaviour + the 3 callers delegate); new `tests/test_api_resume_ready.py` (HALTED_SILENT→200, STOPPED regression, RUNNING→409 naming both, docstring); new `tests/test_ui_f11_halted_silent_resume.py` (render-map completeness + distinct copy; JSX transpile clean). **Replaced** 3 tests asserting the now-removed `HALTED_SILENT` behaviour: `test_unknown_verdict_transitions_to_halted_silent` → `test_unknown_verdict_routes_to_escalation` (in `test_reviewer_routing_dispatch.py`), and the `HALTED_SILENT` assertions + docstring in `test_pipeline_events_stamp_init_failed.py` → escalation-routing (+ `_init_activity_stamp_or_halt` renamed suite-wide in `test_initialize_activity_stamp_strict.py` / `test_polling_mechanism.py`). Pipeline suite **1019 green**; UI suite **1933 passed / 13 skipped** (JSX transpile clean).

- **Phase 1 escalation-recovery fixes — an escalated project is now actually recoverable (F1 / F8 / F2 / F13).** A state-machine correctness audit found escalation recovery broken via every channel; verified live (a `proj` escalation sat stuck in `QUEUE_HALTED` for ~2 h with a dashboard `STOP` banked-but-never-consumed, and the escalation agent had refused its own webhook). Four fixes, TDD red→green:

  - **F8 — a parked `ESCALATION` that is the next/only work is revived to `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN`, not stranded.** `_select_next_queue_project` gained an escalation fallback that runs **only after** startable (`READY`/`SKIPPED_PENDING`) and banked (`ESCALATION_ANSWERED`) entries are exhausted ("READY wins"): the lowest-position parked `ESCALATION` is (re)activated in `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN` (symlink repointed + escalated phase restored from `parked_state_snapshot`). A single/last escalation no longer flips to `QUEUE_HALTED`, and an escalated sibling left after the active project completes is brought up instead of ending `PIPELINE_COMPLETE`. This **supersedes the prior "parked `ESCALATION` counts as `all_blocked`" rule** — `QUEUE_HALTED` is now reserved for genuinely-stuck queues (only `BLOCKED` / dead `DEPENDENCY_HOLD`). Dashboard answers in `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN` are delivered live and consumed.
  - **F1 — `POST /api/stop` accepts `QUEUE_HALTED`.** The orchestrator consumes the stop sentinel at the top of every loop iteration (incl. `QUEUE_HALTED`), so a genuinely-stuck queue can now be halted from the UI instead of returning 409. (The originally-planned in-loop "keep-alive + drain" was **dropped as dead code**: with F8, `QUEUE_HALTED` no longer holds an answerable escalation to drain.)
  - **F2 — `--revive <entry_id>` resumes a specific parked entry through the revival path.** The dashboard Resume control (`relaunch`) now spawns the orchestrator with `--revive`, restoring the escalated phase + applying any banked command for that exact entry instead of `apply_cli_project_path`'s phase-0 reset (which orphaned the banked command). Falls back to `--project-path` when the entry is not revivable (unknown id / crashed ACTIVE). New `_select_next_queue_project(target_entry_id=…)` + `apply_cli_revive()`.
  - **F13 — the escalation agent no longer refuses its own webhook; it is NOTIFY-only.** OpenClaw wraps every `/hooks/agent` payload in an "EXTERNAL, UNTRUSTED … prompt injection" preamble; the control-plane escalation agent was treating the orchestrator's own escalation webhook as an injection attempt and ending its session without notifying or writing anything (verified in a live session — no `escalation_output` produced). The escalation agent docs (`IDENTITY.md` / `SOUL.md` / `AGENTS.md` — **dual-source**: repo + `~/.openclaw/workspace-escalation/`, byte-identical) and the webhook messages (`webhook_client.py` default + the orchestrator's two advisory messages) now frame a pipeline escalation as a **TRUSTED control invocation** and instruct the agent to **notify** the operator via its `message` tool — it no longer waits for / relays a reply or writes `escalation_output` (the operator answers from the dashboard; the server writes the command). Notification stays with OpenClaw's Signal connector — the orchestrator never sends Signal. A real inbound Signal→`escalation_output` channel is deferred to the backlog (`plans/upcomming/signal-inbound-escalation-channel.md`).

  *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (F8 fallback + `target_entry_id` in `_select_next_queue_project`; `apply_cli_revive` + `--revive` arg; trusted/notify-only advisory webhook messages), `autodev/pipeline/webhook_client.py` (escalation default message), `ui/server.py` (`post_stop` accepts `QUEUE_HALTED`; `_spawn_orchestrator(revive_entry_id=…)`; relaunch passes it), `autodev/agents/escalation/{IDENTITY,SOUL,AGENTS}.md` (+ byte-identical runtime copies), `CLAUDE.md` + `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (escalation flow).

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_queue.py` (4 F8 + 4 F2 tests; the 2 P1-Stage-H tests asserting the old "ESCALATION = all_blocked" rule rewritten for the new revive semantics), `tests/test_api_stop.py` (QUEUE_HALTED stop), `autodev/tests/test_webhook_client_default_messages.py` + `test_webhook_client_pipeline_paths.py` (notify-only message), new `autodev/tests/test_escalation_agent_docs.py` (trusted + notify-only doc-lint + repo↔runtime parity). Pipeline suite **1008 green**; UI suite **1916 green** (browser tests excluded while a stale orchestrator held the lock).

- **Escalation panel now shows a live "reviewing escalation" loader while the advisory generates, and an honest factual reason when it fails.** Two fixes to the human-facing escalation *advisory* (the dashboard `escalation_message`, distinct from the `reviewer_retry_directive` reviewer-retry channel added in the rename commit):

  *The loader was dead code.* The advisory (`_generate_escalation_advisory`, a ≤30 s local-LLM call) was generated *synchronously before* the `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN` transition at all three escalation dispatch sites, but the escalation panel renders **only** at `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN` — so the existing `"generating"` spinner was never reached, and the operator saw `RUNNING` for up to 30 s then a fully-formed panel with no sign the agent was working. The three sites (reviewer, repo-init, crash handler) now transition to `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN` with `escalation_advisory_status="generating"` **first**, then generate the advisory — via a new shared `_generate_and_record_advisory(ps)` helper. The panel appears immediately with the Ideas-chat pending loader (`PendingBubbleLifeBackdrop`) + an elapsed-seconds timer (`ChatElapsedSeconds`, driven off `waiting_for_human_at`), then flips to the advisory within one 3 s poll. The advisory dict is still produced before the escalation webhook message is built, so the operator Signal notification is unchanged.

  *Honest fallback.* When the advisory hangs/times out/fails (returns `None`), the panel used to show a generic "Automated review unavailable…". A new `_compose_fallback_reason(ps)` now records a deterministic, factual `escalation_message` from hard signals (`last_error_code`, the already-honest `escalation_trigger_reason`, and `failure_context.json`'s `failing_agent`/`gate_error_codes`/`attempt_number`) — and **never** the phase's `failure_language` (presenting that expected-failure description as observed reality is the fabrication behind the earlier misleading "blank white page" message). The UI fallback branch renders this message, keeping the generic line only when it is empty.

  *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (new `_compose_fallback_reason` + `_generate_and_record_advisory`; the 3 escalation dispatch sites reordered to transition before generating). `ui/index.html` (`EscalationCommandPanel` generating branch → Ideas loader + timer; fallback branch → render `escalation_message`; `waiting_for_human_at` threaded as a prop at both render sites). No `ui/server.py` change — `waiting_for_human_at` was already surfaced by `/api/state`. `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` §7.

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `autodev/tests/test_escalation_advisory_loader.py` — `_compose_fallback_reason` unit tests (CONTRACT_FAILURE via trigger reason, executor via `failure_context`, generic degradation, **no `failure_language` leakage**), `_generate_and_record_advisory` ready/fallback-path tests, and source-level reorder guards (transition before advisory at all 3 sites + exactly one direct `_generate_escalation_advisory()` call, inside the helper). The shared-panel render test (`tests/test_ui_escalation_panel_shared.py`) stays green. Full pipeline suite **994 green**; UI suite **1922 passed** (JSX transpile clean; the one full-run `test_api_ideas_message` 408 failure is the pre-existing ideas flake — passes in isolation).

- **`queue_mode="auto"` now auto-starts a READY project when the pipeline is idle — from `add` / `parent`-clear / `revalidate`, not just the manual→auto toggle.** Closes a latent gap: a project added to an idle/auto queue (or added after `PIPELINE_COMPLETE`, or made READY by clearing a parent / revalidating) used to strand until a human ran **Run next project** or toggled the mode. The orchestrator's between-project advance (`_select_next_queue_project`) only runs while it is alive, so nothing started a freshly-eligible project when the orchestrator was dead. Server-only fix; `orchestrator.py` unchanged.

  *Server (`ui/server.py`):* generalized `_maybe_auto_kick_queue_after_manual_to_auto` → `_maybe_autostart_queue(config)` — added an explicit `queue_mode=="auto"` gate (`not_auto_mode`) so it is safe to call from any mutation, and made it **non-raising** (wraps `_queue_run_trigger_next_logic`; a 409/500 becomes `{"attempted": true, "ok": false, "reason": "already_active" | "spawn_failed"}` instead of propagating). The three READY-making endpoints call it and surface an additive **`auto_start`** field (`add` re-reads its row so the returned `state` is truthful — `ACTIVE`/`READY`/`FAILED`; `parent` only on the clear→READY branch; `revalidate` on its `{ok,checks,entry}` wrapper). The mode toggle keeps its manual→auto transition guard and still reports under `auto_advance`. Guards unchanged (`orchestrator_lock_held` / `pipeline_status_busy` / `queue_has_active`); the single-writer model is preserved (the helper acts only while idle). A spawn failure marks the row `FAILED` (inherited from `_queue_run_trigger_next_logic`).

  *UI (`ui/index.html`) — removal:* deleted the now-dead client-side post-add shim in `onAddToQueue` (refetch queue → `if isAuto && !hasActive && readyCount===1` → `POST /api/queue/trigger-next`). The server owns auto-start now; the shim would otherwise race it into the 409 "already ACTIVE" path. The manual **Run next project** button is unchanged.

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `TestQueueAutostartOnReady` (15 tests) covering the matrix for add / parent-clear / revalidate (auto+idle starts; manual / pipeline-busy / queue-has-active / lock-held / blocked-parent skip; spawn-failure non-raising → row `FAILED`; 409 → `already_active`; `id` + entry fields preserved). **Test-safety hardening (beyond the original plan):** a real `_spawn_orchestrator` resolves the real `OPENCLAW_ROOT` / `AUTODEV_PIPELINE_ROOT` (the mock config and the env-scrub both fall back to real paths), so a stray test auto-start mutates the **live** `pipeline_state.json` — observed during the red run. Added a suite-wide autouse `_disable_queue_autostart` in `tests/conftest.py` (opt-in via `_uses_real_autostart` on `TestQueueAutostartOnReady` + `TestPatchQueueMode`) plus a per-file `_no_real_orchestrator_spawn` net in `test_queue_api.py`. Full UI suite **1923 passed / 13 skipped** (1 pre-existing full-run flake, `test_preflight_path_has_datalist_link`, passes in isolation); pipeline suite **983 green** (orchestrator untouched).

  *Docs:* `CLAUDE.md` Queue System (new "Server-side auto-start on idle" subsection) + the `QUEUE_HALTED` / `trigger-next` note; `GLOSSARY.md` `READY` row. *Out of scope (callouts):* the server `_queue_run_trigger_next_logic` vs. orchestrator `_select_next_queue_project` duplication; surfacing an "auto-started <name>" toast in the UI (the queue refetch already reflects the ACTIVE row).

- **Renamed the reviewer gate's `INFRA_FAILURE` → `CONTRACT_FAILURE` and gave its retry actionable feedback (Phase 4, gate-feedback methodology).** When the reviewer session ends without a parseable `reviewer_output.json`, the gate returned `INFRA_FAILURE` and the orchestrator soft-retried *blind*. "INFRA" was a mislabel: genuine transport/provider failures are peeled off **upstream** (stall detection, dead-on-arrival, provider-rejection), and the `autodev-pipeline-signals` plugin's `agent_end` handler (`agent-end-handler.ts`) writes the `.done` sentinel **unconditionally** (no `event.success` guard — verified against source), so this branch is reached whenever the session ends without a verdict **for any reason** (clean give-up OR abort/crash/limit-hit). The sentinel proves only that `agent_end` fired, not a clean final action. Because the recovery is identical for give-up and abort, the cause is **diagnostic, not routing**: the verdict (`CONTRACT_FAILURE`), error code (`ERR_REVIEWER_CONTRACT_FAILURE`), counter (`reviewer_contract_retries`), and escalation tag (`CONTRACT_FAILURE_SOFT_RETRY_EXHAUSTED`) all renamed in lockstep; a cheap defensive `_escalate_if_provider_rejected` re-check sits at the top of the branch as the only case-(a) hedge. Phase 4 of `plans/Active/gate-feedback-methodology-roadmap.md` (Q7, D4).

  *Actionable retry (was blind):* the contract retry now re-invokes the reviewer in a **fresh** session (new `_reviewer_session_key` appends a `-c{N}` suffix only when `reviewer_contract_retries > 0` — deterministic distinct keys; hygiene, not a resume fix, since the prior session already ended) carrying a **one-shot, self-contained corrective directive** delivered as the webhook `message=` by the new `_invoke_reviewer` helper (read → deliver → clear). The directive must be self-contained because `message=` overrides the reviewer's default message, so it re-asserts both the inputs to read and the JSON-then-sentinel output contract. **Architecture rule:** structured data the agent *analyzes* → file (executor `failure_context.json`, reviewer `gate_warnings.json`); a one-shot directive that *frames* the invocation → `message=` (the reviewer's single directive channel).

  *C1 reviewer-half fold (latent-bug fix + removal):* the sibling `unverified_instruction` field (written by the VISUAL/BEHAVIORAL/REGRESSION_UNVERIFIED handler) was a **dead write** — no reader, no AGENTS.md mention — so those retries were *also* blind. It is retired by routing the UNVERIFIED handler through the same unified `reviewer_retry_directive` field + `_invoke_reviewer` delivery; **the three UNVERIFIED retries flip from blind → fed.** The executor-side `artifact_instruction` (MISSING_ARTIFACTS → executor) is the same dead-write pattern on a different surface and is the explicit deferred follow-up using this same template.

  *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/gate_scripts/reviewer_gate.py` (verdict + error-code rename + honest comment). `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (`_reviewer_session_key` + `_invoke_reviewer` helpers; CONTRACT_FAILURE branch rename/counter/tag + defensive provider re-check + directive write; reviewer invocation routed through `_invoke_reviewer`; `_rev_next` map; `reset_phase` init + RR-4 comment; `reset_execution` comment; UNVERIFIED handler writes `reviewer_retry_directive`, dead `unverified_instruction` removed). `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` §7 (pre-check reframe to the verified backstop mechanism + diagnostic-not-routing, schema entries, reset matrix), `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md` (counter table), `CLAUDE.md` (historical clause left intact). No agent-doc edits (the webhook-message channel needs none).

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* renamed `test_reviewer_infra_failure.py` → `test_reviewer_contract_failure.py` and `test_infra_failure_orchestrator.py` → `test_contract_failure_orchestrator.py`; new delivery + one-shot + fresh-session-key guards (`_invoke_reviewer` mocks `invoke_agent_webhook` and asserts the directive **reaches** `message=`, not just a phase_state write — the R-C rule); the write-only `*_unverified` assertions **rewritten** to delivery assertions (the dead-write trap that hid C1) with an explicit UNVERIFIED→`message=` delivery test; `test_traffic_cop_retired.py`, `test_reviewer_routing_dispatch.py` (KNOWN_VERDICTS), `test_done_file_logic.py`, `test_reviewer_gate_visual_verification.py` updated. Full pipeline suite **983 green**.

- **All three synchronous one-click Ideas→OpenClaw flows now use the chat send's idle-detection poll + late-reply recovery, so a slow agent's output is never stranded.** The reported bug: clicking **Fix Format** on the "Roadmap Format Invalid" modal (`POST /api/ideas/{id}/fix-roadmap-format`) sends work to a *different, slower* agent (`roadmap-converter`, not `prd-creator`) on a **naive fixed 180 s deadline loop with no idle detection and no frontend recovery** — a legitimately-slow correction 408'd, the corrected roadmap the agent finished a moment later was silently stranded on disk, and the user was stuck on "Fix Format failed." An audit of every one-click webhook flow found the same naive loop in `convert` (480 s) and `clarity-check` (60 s, below the measured 118 s single-call floor). All three now reuse the chat send's `_poll_sentinel_with_idle_detect` (activity-stamp stall detection: `ideas_idle_threshold` stall window + per-flow infra backstop + `startup_grace=None`). The chat send (`/message`) was already correct and is unchanged; `readiness` and `completion-review` are fire-and-forget with frontend polling and were audited as adequate (unchanged).

  *Shared helper (no duplicated poll logic):* `_poll_sentinel_with_idle_detect` gained two backward-compatible keyword params — `rescue_stranded_reply_md=True` (default; the chat path is untouched) and `extra_done_paths=()`. The roadmap flows pass `rescue_stranded_reply_md=False` because `fix-roadmap-format` **pre-writes the malformed input** to `roadmap_draft.md` before the agent runs, so the helper's stranded-`.md` rescue would otherwise surface the *uncorrected* roadmap as success (the central correctness trap). `convert` passes `extra_done_paths=(verification_draft.done,)` so success still requires BOTH artefacts — the dual-sentinel contract is preserved, not weakened to a single sentinel. `FORMAT_CORRECTION_TIMEOUT` bumped 180→600 and `CLARITY_TIMEOUT` (new, 600, replacing the 60 s literal) are now infra backstops, not the primary failure signal; `CONVERT_TIMEOUT` stays 480. Both roadmap endpoints standardise on a single `time.time()` capture for the poll's `attempt_start_wall` and the op metric.

  *Frontend recovery:* `doFixRoadmapFormat` previously only showed "Fix Format failed" on error — it now attaches the HTTP status to the thrown error and, on a 408, starts `startRoadmapRecoverPoll` (the same mechanism `convert` already uses) to pick up the late corrected roadmap and **auto-continue setup with the fresh content**. `startRoadmapRecoverPoll(ideaId, onRecovered)` gained an optional callback; `continueSetup`/`_continueSetupAfterStalenessOk` gained an optional `contentOverride` so the `/api/setup/validate-roadmap` POST validates the freshly-recovered roadmap rather than the not-yet-flushed `roadmapContent` React state (this also fixes a latent same-tick stale-state read on the Fix-Format **success** path). `_runConvert` now triggers recovery on the HTTP 408 status (robust), keeping the prior message-string match as a fallback. Backend late-salvage already existed — `GET /session` merges a late `roadmap_draft.md`+`.done` into `roadmap_content` (`_merge_roadmap_draft_into_session_data`) — and covers both roadmap flows unchanged.

  *Plugin typed-hook symmetry (`autodev/plugin`):* `convert`/`format-correction` run as `roadmap-converter`, but `recordPipelineActivity` (the typed-hook path: `model_call_started`/`ended`, `after_tool_call`) gated the Ideas stamp-touch on `agentId === "prd-creator"` and so **skipped** them — their idle detection worked in production only via the agent-event-stream path. The Ideas branch now gates on `isIdeasSession(sessionKey)` **alone** (any Ideas agent), matching the event-stream path, so both paths refresh `prd_creator_activity.stamp` for roadmap-converter. The now-unused `PRD_CREATOR_AGENT_ID` import was removed. `dist/` rebuilt (gitignored); live effect needs `openclaw plugins install` + gateway restart (operator step).

  *Removed:* `_idea_roadmap_done_sentinel_fresh` (`ui/server.py`) — its only two callers (convert, format-correction) migrated to the helper, whose pre-poll `.done` unlink subsumes the freshness guard. No dead code or stale tests left behind.

  *Changed:* `ui/server.py` (`_poll_sentinel_with_idle_detect` params; `post_ideas_fix_roadmap_format`, `post_ideas_convert`, `post_ideas_clarity_check` migrated; `FORMAT_CORRECTION_TIMEOUT`, new `CLARITY_TIMEOUT`/`CLARITY_POLL_INTERVAL`; `_idea_roadmap_done_sentinel_fresh` deleted). `ui/index.html` (`doFixRoadmapFormat`, `_runConvert`, `startRoadmapRecoverPoll`, `continueSetup`, `_continueSetupAfterStalenessOk`). `autodev/plugin/src/stall-detector.ts` (Ideas gate) + `utils.ts` import. `CLAUDE.md` (share-the-chat-stamp note + `AUTODEV_IDEAS_IDLE_THRESHOLD` row + config dual-source line).

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* `tests/test_ideas_stamp_detect.py` (+4: rescue-default-preserved, rescue-disabled stalled/timeout, `extra_done_paths` dual-gate); `tests/test_p0_stage_k_format_correction_smoke.py` (+1 regression: a stall 408s and does NOT surface the pre-written malformed roadmap); new `tests/test_ui_fix_format_recovery.py` (static-contract tests: status-into-catch, 408→recover-poll, `onRecovered`, `contentOverride` on both continue fns, fresh-content happy path, convert 408-status trigger, event-safe button guard); `tests/test_api_ideas_clarity_check.py` (timeout test rewritten to patch the helper + a new stall case; the brittle `Path.__new__` interception removed); `autodev/plugin/tests/stall-detector.test.ts` (the "non-prd-creator does not write" test **inverted** to assert roadmap-converter DOES write, + a production `agent:`-prefixed format-correction case). Full suites green: UI **1901**, pipeline **975**, plugin **61** (typecheck clean, esbuild bundle rebuilt + deployed to the live gateway).

- **Fixed a regression in the "Continue to Setup" button introduced by the recovery work above.** The button is wired `onClick={continueSetup}`, so React passes a `SyntheticEvent` as the new `contentOverride` argument; the initial `contentOverride != null` check treated that event object as a roadmap override and threw in `rm.includes(...)`, silently killing the button. `continueSetup` and `_continueSetupAfterStalenessOk` now honour `contentOverride` only when `typeof === "string"` (a non-string event/`undefined` falls back to the `roadmapContent` state), making every call site event-safe. *Changed:* `ui/index.html`. *Tests:* `tests/test_ui_fix_format_recovery.py::test_continue_to_setup_button_is_event_safe` + the updated `test_continue_setup_accepts_content_override` (pins the typeof guard; the `!= null` shape must be absent).

- **Roadmap validator made phase-bounded — retired the fixed 30-line Behavioral-Verification search window.** Live bug (Tick-Tac-Toe — Minimalist Edition): the "Roadmap Format Invalid" modal reappeared on every Continue/refresh even after a *successful* format-correction. Root cause was **not** the recovery and **not** the agent (it produced a complete, correct BV block) — it was a pre-existing conflict between the validator's `_BV_SEARCH_WINDOW = 30` ("the *whole* BV block must fit within 30 lines of the phase header", boundary-tested at +30/+31) and the canonical phase structure, which places the Behavioral Verification block **last** (after Entry/Exit/TDD/Done Criteria — see `roadmap-generation` SKILL.md). The last phase's long Done-Criteria list pushed its (complete) block's 3rd sub-bullet to phase+31, one line past the window, so `_validate_roadmap_content` reported it missing. `_validate_roadmap_content` now finds each phase's BV block + sub-bullets within that **phase's own section** (phase header → next phase header / EOF) instead of a fixed line window; this accepts a complete block at any depth, still rejects a missing/incomplete one, and prevents a neighbouring phase's block from satisfying this one. `_BV_SEARCH_WINDOW` and the `body_end`/`window_end` machinery were removed. *Changed:* `ui/server.py`. *Tests:* the deliberate `+30 passes`/`+31 fails` window-boundary tests in `tests/test_p0_validate_roadmap_strict.py` and the `within_30`/`beyond_30_fails` tests in `tests/test_p0_validate_roadmap_behavioral.py` were **replaced** with phase-bounded equivalents (complete block deep/far within a phase passes; a block belonging to the next phase does not satisfy the prior one), and module docstrings updated. The live UI server (`autodev-ui`) was restarted so the fix takes effect; the real roadmap now validates (`valid=True`).

- **Demoted three interpretive executor-gate checks from hard FAILs to reviewer-adjudicated warnings (Phase 3, gate-feedback methodology).** `ERR_MANIFEST_FILE_MISSING` (a declared file not on disk), `ERR_TDD_COVERAGE_MISMATCH` (a planner-listed test absent from `tests_written`), and `ERR_BEHAVIORAL_ARTIFACTS_MISSING` (missing/empty/malformed `behavioral_smoke_artifacts`) no longer return `FAIL` and burn an executor retry before the reviewer ever sees the work. The gate records each as a non-blocking warning in a new reviewer-facing `gate_warnings.json` and PASSes; the reviewer reads it and adjudicates — accept-and-proceed, or reject-with-specifics into a `blocking_issue` on the **existing** `ROUTE_EXECUTOR` loop (no new retry mechanism). Phase 3 of `plans/Active/gate-feedback-methodology-roadmap.md` (Q2 + Q4, D3).

  *Surgical carve-out:* the `ERR_PATH_TRAVERSAL` boundary checks are interleaved in the same manifest/behavioral loops and are **NOT** demoted — a path escaping the workspace stays a hard `FAIL` (CLAUDE.md Security Constraints). The reviewer's own independent checks (file_manifest existence, test quality, gate-enforced `behavioral_verification`) are the backstop the demotion relies on; because the gate PASSes, **no retry budget is consumed**.

  *Observability (full):* `_emit_gate_warnings` drains `gate_warnings.json` on the executor-PASS path into one `gate_warning` pipeline event (`{count, codes, files}`, agent `"executor"`) and stashes a compact `last_gate_warnings` onto `phase_state`, surfaced in the canonical metrics row's new `gate_warnings` field. **Critical lifecycle divergence from the reachability advisory:** the file is **preserved** (not removed) so the reviewer can read it — staleness is instead prevented by the gate's start-of-run `_clear_gate_warnings()` (the executor gate always runs before the reviewer) and by enumerating the file in the four per-phase artifact-lifecycle sites. UI renders `gate_warning` in the yellow advisory family across all four event maps (`getEventBadgeColor` / `EVENT_TYPE_DISPLAY` / `EVENT_TYPE_DESCRIPTION` / `humanizeSummary`), reusing the kept `P3_LAST_ERROR_CODE_TITLES` map as warning labels (the codes are no longer `last_error_code` values but remain valid warning identifiers).

  *Changed:* `autodev/pipeline/gate_scripts/executor_gate.py` (`GATE_WARNINGS_JSON` + `_gate_warnings_path`/`_clear_gate_warnings`/`_write_gate_warnings`; the three checks accumulate into a `warnings` list instead of returning FAIL; written on PASS, **not** removed). `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (`_emit_gate_warnings`, called on the PASS path right after `_emit_reachability_advisory`; `gate_warnings` field in `_write_canonical_metrics_row`; `gate_warnings.json` added to the four artifact-lifecycle sites). `ui/index.html` (four event maps). Agent docs applied to **both** the repo and the live `~/.openclaw/workspace-{agent}/` runtime (byte-identical): `reviewer/AGENTS.md` gains the `gate_warnings.json` input + adjudication step 8; `executor/AGENTS.md` + `planner/AGENTS.md` reword the demoted codes from "hard fail" to "gate warning the reviewer adjudicates." `CLAUDE.md`, `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (§4.5 variant + the Executor Output Gate check list), and `AUTODEV-UI-PRD.md` (sample `last_error_code`) updated.

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `autodev/tests/test_executor_gate_demoted_warnings.py` (PASS+warning for each demoted check, aggregation, clean-pass writes no file, stale-clear, and two **load-bearing** hard-keep tests proving `ERR_PATH_TRAVERSAL` still FAILs); `test_executor_gate_behavioral_artifacts.py` rewritten to keep only the surviving invariants (no-block exemption, valid happy-path, path-traversal FAIL) — the demoted-FAIL cases were deleted, not left green; new `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_gate_warnings.py` (event emission, compact stash, **file-preserved** assertion, stale-stash clear, metrics-row field, two structural guards); reviewer drift-guard extended (`tests/test_p0_reviewer_agent_md.py`); new `tests/test_ui_gate_warning_rendering.py` (four-map static lint + non-blocking-copy check). Full suites green: pipeline **975**, UI **1887** (+ JSX transpile guard on `index.html`).

- **Completion-summary "Run Metrics" phase-detail card regrouped into two readable columns.** The expandable per-phase card now shows **Duration · Skill · Cost** in the left column and **Exec attempts · Reviewer passes · Escalations** in the right (was left: Duration/Reviewer passes/Skill, right: Exec attempts/Escalations/Cost). Implemented by replacing the flat row-major `grid grid-cols-2` (which interleaved the columns child-by-child) with two explicit column `<div>`s, each `space-y-1` — so the conditional, zero-suppressed Cost shortens only the left column instead of shifting Escalations across columns. The `formatExecAttemptsBreakdown(...)` call, the `cost_total > 0` zero-suppression guard, and `.toFixed(2)` are preserved verbatim.

  *Changed:* `ui/index.html` (the `Run Metrics:` card in the completed-phase dropdown).

  *Tests:* new `tests/test_ui_run_metrics_field_order.py` — block-scoped source-order assertions pin the left column (`Duration < Skill < Cost`), the right column (`Exec attempts < Reviewer passes < Escalations`), and the discriminating left-precedes-right check (`Cost` before `Exec attempts`, red against the old layout). Because the UI suite is grep-only and cannot catch a white-screen-inducing malformed-JSX regression, the edited `text/babel` block was additionally validated through an in-browser Babel transpile (clean).

- **Gate-failure executor retries now preserve the work and carry a feedback note — symmetric with the reviewer-rejection path.** Previously a self-failure retry ran `git reset --hard HEAD` (reverting the executor's work), cleared `failure_context.json`, and re-invoked the executor with the generic default message, so each fresh session restarted **blind** ("start completely fresh") and rebuilt the whole phase (e.g. UI-E3's repeated 24-min attempts). It now mirrors the proven `ROUTE_EXECUTOR` (reviewer-rejection) mechanics: the working tree is **preserved**, `failure_context.json` survives tagged `source: "gate"` with a concise `retry_guidance` note, and the executor makes targeted fixes on its prior work. Each retry is still a fresh OpenClaw session (distinct attempt key, prior session aborted — no thread reuse, no context accumulation), which is *why* the note is file-based: the fresh session reads it at startup.

  *Decisions:* the hard `git reset --hard HEAD` is kept **only** for `ERR_UNACCOUNTED_DELETION` (restores files MiniMax deletes under context pressure); every other failure preserves the tree — applied to both `reset_execution("auto")` and the operator `RESET_EXECUTION` command (`RESET_PHASE` remains the clean-slate option). Feedback rides `failure_context.json`, not the webhook message — the executor already reads that file.

  *Changed:* `orchestrator.py` — `reset_execution` gates the hard reset on the deletion code and no longer clears `failure_context.json` (`reset_phase` still clears it on phase advance); `write_failure_context` tags `source: "gate"` + a deletion-aware `retry_guidance`. `executor/AGENTS.md` "Scenario A" rewritten (preserved work → read `failure_context.json` → targeted fixes; the false `git clean -fd` claim removed). `reviewer/AGENTS.md` `blocking_issues` guidance strengthened to name the exact file + line/area + variable/function (D2 — the reviewer→executor handoff). Agent docs applied to both the repo and the live `~/.openclaw/workspace-{agent}/` runtime (byte-identical). `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` §"Two Retry Scenarios" + the `RESET_EXECUTION` command row updated.

  *Q6 audit:* the output-contract reminder ("produce `<x>_output.json`, write `<x>_output.done`") is already injected consistently across the planner/executor/reviewer webhook default messages — now locked by a regression guard.

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `autodev/tests/test_executor_self_failure_feedback.py` — preserve-on-non-deletion, reset-on-deletion, `failure_context` survives, `source:"gate"` + `retry_guidance`, the two doc drift-guards, and the Q6 reminder lock. Pipeline suite **957**; UI **1875** (the lone `test_p0_stage_k_format_correction_smoke` failure is a pre-existing full-run flake — passes in isolation).

### Removed

- **Scrapped the deterministic PRD-verbatim executor-gate check (`ERR_PRD_VERBATIM_MISSING`) and the entire `prd_verbatim` vocabulary.** The check grepped `git ls-files` for literal `pass_criteria[].traces_to: "prd_verbatim:<literal>"` anchors and failed the executor when a literal was absent from tracked source. It verified *vocabulary, not function*: because `prd.md` is itself a tracked file, anchors "passed" by matching the PRD rather than the code, while correct work was hard-failed over formatting drift — a markdown-stripped anchor halted a correct `svg-pic2 / CORE-E6` build ~10 self-failures deep, and the reviewer passed the same work on pass 1 once it was finally reached. Functional correctness is already owned by the reviewer + behavioral verification, so the check was net-negative and is removed outright (Phase 1 of `plans/Active/gate-feedback-methodology-roadmap.md`).

  *Removed (`autodev/pipeline/gate_scripts/executor_gate.py`):* `_PRD_VERBATIM_PREFIX`, `_GREP_CHUNK_SIZE`, `_extract_prd_verbatim_anchors`, `_tracked_files`, `_check_prd_verbatim_anchors`, and the `evaluate_executor` call site. The shared `_write_executor_gate_detail` helper is **kept** — the `ERR_UNACCOUNTED_DELETION` guard still uses it. The `prd_verbatim` value is dropped from the reviewer `criterion_source` enum (now `behavioral | test | regression_prior_phase | free`) across `reviewer/AGENTS.md`, the `orchestrator.py` normalizer docstring + enum comment, and the `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` schema example. Authoring vocabulary removed: the planner's `prd_verbatim:` anchor form (`planner/AGENTS.md`, `traces_to` enum 4→3 forms), the PRD `### Verbatim Strings` contract (`prd-creator/AGENTS.md`), and the roadmap-converter "Preserving PRD Verbatim Strings" guidance. The `ERR_PRD_VERBATIM_MISSING` UI title (`ui/index.html`) and `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` §7 paragraph + gate-pseudocode line are gone.

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `autodev/tests/test_executor_gate_no_prd_verbatim.py` pins that a missing `prd_verbatim` anchor no longer fails the gate (red against pre-scrub code, green after); deleted `test_executor_gate_prd_verbatim.py` (478 lines covering the removed behavior); drift-guards updated to the post-scrub state — `test_p0_planner_agent_md.py` (4→3 anchor forms), `test_ui_observability_p1_render_fixes.py` (title-map), `test_orchestrator_failure_context_behavioral.py` (enum docstring). Full suites green: pipeline **948**, UI **1867** (+ JSX transpile guard on `index.html`).

  *Dual-source:* the `planner` / `reviewer` / `prd-creator` `AGENTS.md` edits were applied to **both** the repo source and the live `~/.openclaw/workspace-{agent}/` runtime copies (verified byte-identical). *Decision (deliberate, in scope):* no active coercion was added for a stale `criterion_source: "prd_verbatim"` arriving from a pre-scrub `failure_context.json` — it is inert (no code branches on the value; the executor self-heal only routes on `behavioral`/`regression_prior_phase`) and the file is a per-phase transient artifact.

### Fixed

- **Reviewer contract failures no longer render as a green ✓ in the attempts ladder (Phase 5, gate-feedback methodology).** The attempt dots keyed the reviewer row off `reviewer_retries`, which a reviewer `CONTRACT_FAILURE` (formerly `INFRA_FAILURE`) never increments — so a reviewer that failed to emit a verdict three times and then escalated rendered a green "passed" slot, and a contract retry in progress showed a neutral/blue slot (failures invisible). `reviewer_contract_retries` lives only in `phase_state`, so the frontend never saw it. Fix: `GET /api/state` now surfaces `reviewer_contract_retries` (from `phase_state`; `last_error_code` was already exposed via `_compute_escalation_view`); `getAgentAttemptDotStates` folds it into the reviewer row's effective failure count (`revEff`/`rEff = min(rev + revContract, 3)`), gated on `revContract > 0` so every existing attempt-dot path is a strict no-op when no contract failures occurred (red is reused — no new palette colour). The `ERR_INFRA_FAILURE` error-code title, the `reviewer_verdict` enumeration, and the `humanizeSummary` case were renamed to the honest `ERR_REVIEWER_CONTRACT_FAILURE` / `CONTRACT_FAILURE` (no `INFRA_FAILURE` string remains in `index.html`). *Changed:* `ui/server.py` (`/api/state` phase_state block), `ui/index.html` (`getAgentAttemptDotStates` + `revContract`/`revEff`/`rEff`; `AgentAttemptRow` prop + `allRetries.reviewerContract`; `CurrentPhasePanel` destructure + 3 render rows + parent pass-through + initial `pState`; three label sites). *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `tests/test_api_state.py::TestGetStateReviewerContractRetries` (exposed + defaults-zero); new source-anchor guards in `tests/test_ui_current_phase_panel.py` (prop plumbed, reviewer row uses the gated contract signal, render sites bind it, `humanizeSummary` maps CONTRACT_FAILURE not INFRA, error-code title renamed); the stale `H23_ERR_INFRA` copy assertion in `tests/test_ui_p3_hints_h01_h26.py` rewritten to the new `ERR_REVIEWER_CONTRACT_FAILURE` copy. Full UI suite **1909 green** (incl. the JSX transpile guard).

- **Completion-summary "Total Time" no longer reports calendar wall-clock — it is now the sum of per-phase durations.** `GET /api/metrics-summary` computed `total_duration = max(Σ per-phase duration_seconds, run_summary.json total_duration_seconds)`; the latter is **calendar** wall-clock (`run_end − run_start`) and spans idle nights across days, so for `svg-pic2` it showed **74h 21m** when only **19h 21m** of phase work had occurred (the panel had earlier shown 54h 59m on an older boot). The `max()` is removed — total is now purely the per-phase sum, which already excludes between-phase idle and (via the existing `Active = Total − Hold` split) attributes escalation waits to Hold. Live svg-pic2 now reports **Total 19h21m / Active 12h12m / Hold 7h08m**.

  *Removed (`ui/server.py`):* `_read_run_summary_duration` — its sole caller was the deleted `max()`. `run_summary.json` and the orchestrator's `_write_run_summary` are **kept** (the `/api/metrics-global` analytics endpoint reads other fields); its `total_duration_seconds` field is now **write-only, intentionally retained** as a distinct calendar-span datum.

  *Investigated (in scope at user's request, no code change):* whether `reset_phase()` not resetting `phase_start_time` corrupts the Active/Hold split. Forensic reconstruction of svg-pic2 from `pipeline_events.jsonl` + `metrics.jsonl` showed the split is honest after the calendar fix — active-per-executor-attempt is 8–44 min across all 14 phases (no mid-phase-downtime outliers), and the `RESET_PHASE`'d phase (`UI-E3`) reconstructs cleanly. A reset phase's pre-reset work is *real active time the operator experienced*, so resetting the clock would **under-report** it — less honest, not more. Left unchanged. The one residual edge — *non-escalation* mid-phase downtime (crash / manual STOP / sleep) counting as active — is now a documented known-limitation rather than left silent.

  *Changed:* `ui/server.py` — `total_duration = total_duration_summed` (rationale comment rewritten), the `max(0, …)` Active clamp (now load-bearing) commented, `get_metrics_summary` docstring updated. `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` `GET /api/metrics-summary` — Total-time semantics + the known limitation.

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* replaced `test_metrics_summary_uses_max_of_run_summary_and_phase_sum` — which **enshrined the bug** (asserted `total == 9999` when run_summary was larger) — with `test_metrics_summary_ignores_run_summary_duration` (asserts the phase sum `360` even when run_summary is `9999`); added `test_metrics_summary_active_clamped_when_hold_exceeds_duration` pinning the clamp. Read-only spot-check against live svg-pic2 confirmed `total_duration_seconds == 69685` (not `267679`). Full suites green: pipeline **957**, UI **1879** (the lone `test_api_ideas_convert` full-run failure is a pre-existing flake — passes in isolation) + in-browser JSX transpile guard on `index.html`.

- **Reviewer `INFRA_FAILURE` no longer false-escalates a cloud reviewer through a dead-end SSH "recovery" — the traffic-cop health / SSH-recovery / model-swap machinery is retired.** A live run (`svg-pic2 / INFRA-E1`) paged a human for what was a transient reviewer-output fluke. Forensics from the raw runtime files showed a 3-bug cascade: the reviewer (Kimi K2.6, OpenRouter) emitted malformed JSON → `reviewer_gate.py` correctly returned `INFRA_FAILURE`; the orchestrator's handler probed the **local** llama `/health` at `127.0.0.1:11434` via `check_traffic_cop_health()` and got a false "unhealthy" (the real model runs on the configured `llama-local` baseUrl `<llama-server-host>:11434`, which `wait_for_model_stable()` had reported **stable, 7 models** seconds earlier in the same log); "unhealthy" took the SSH-recovery branch with an empty `recovery` config → `ssh` exit 255 → escalation (`INFRA_FAILURE_RECOVERY_FAILED`), bypassing the self-healing soft-retry that would have re-invoked the reviewer and cleared the fluke. The model was never unhealthy — the escalation agent and the advisory both ran on that same local model in the same incident.

  *Decision:* all pipeline agents run on cloud providers (only `escalation` is `llama-local`), and agent/model liveness is already owned by the OpenClaw activity-stamp hooks (`startup_grace`/`no_first_activity` for cold start, `stalled` for mid-turn death, in `poll_for_sentinel`). The traffic-cop health probe, the SSH auto-restart, and the executor→reviewer GPU model-swap wait are vestigial for an all-cloud pipeline — and were the direct cause of the page — so they were fully retired.

  *Removed (`autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py`):* `check_traffic_cop_health()`; the entire `INFRA_FAILURE` "unhealthy → SSH recovery" branch (10-minute cooldown, SSH subprocess, exit-code routing); `wait_for_model_stable()` and its two executor→reviewer handoff calls (OB-6); the `reviewer_infra_recovery_*` phase-state fields (`_attempts`/`_attempted`/`_timestamp`/`_exit_code`/`_succeeded`) and the `recovery`-config reads; the `INFRA_FAILURE_RECOVERY_FAILED` / `INFRA_FAILURE_RECOVERY_EXHAUSTED` escalation reasons. `INFRA_FAILURE` now collapses to an unconditional soft retry — re-invoke the reviewer in a fresh session (counting `reviewer_infra_retries`, **kept**, cap 3) → escalate `INFRA_FAILURE_SOFT_RETRY_EXHAUSTED`. The executor→reviewer handoff transitions directly. The local llama-server and its non-gating callers (escalation agent, blame-L1 analyst, failure analyst, escalation advisory) are unchanged.

  *Tests (TDD, red→green):* new `autodev/tests/test_traffic_cop_retired.py` (5 source-level guards — both methods gone; no SSH/recovery/health/model-wait text in the `INFRA_FAILURE` branch; soft-retry + `INFRA_FAILURE_SOFT_RETRY_EXHAUSTED` present; handoff has no model wait); `test_infra_failure_orchestrator.py` rewritten to the soft-retry-only contract (the four SSH/recovery/cooldown/exit-code tests deleted; counter-separation pared to `reviewer_infra_retries`); `test_polling_mechanism.py` OB-6 test rewritten to assert the handoff has neither `time.sleep(60)` nor `wait_for_model_stable`. Full pipeline suite **956 green**.

  *Docs (same pass):* `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` §7 (INFRA_FAILURE → soft-retry-only, with a dated historical note) + the OB-6 handoff note + the `phase_state` schema/reset-matrix entries; `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md` (counter table → two counters; §5.7 mitigation-retired note; §5.9 "SSH Recovery Interface" section deleted; local-first note); `CLAUDE.md` (`AUTODEV_LLAMA_BASE` consumer row; corrected the stale "`heartbeat_cron.py` queries the traffic cop" claim — it is lock-only, no model query). *Callout (out of scope):* the escalation advisory still presents the phase's `failure_language` as if observed (the "blank white page" text in this incident); making the advisory faithful to the actual failure class is a separate change.

### Added

- **Drift-guard audit remediation — remove the vestigial `status` field, correct stale timing docs, add two parity guards.** A unified-source drift-guard audit (brief + ledger in `plans/Active/drift-guard-audit-findings.md`) found two drifts already in the tree and several unguarded invariants; this pass closes the first two groups under TDD. **Removal validated, not assumed:** `pipeline_state.json`'s `status` field was confirmed dead by git lineage — `pipeline_status` has been canonical since project init (`6187d53`), while `status` was added later by the git-recover feature (`6a5769e`) as a redundant lockstep co-write, written by exactly one site and read only by an unreachable `or _ps.get("status")` fallback (every writer always sets `pipeline_status`, so no state file can carry `status` without it).

  *Removed (`status` field):* the lone writer (`ui/server.py` git-recover) and the dead fallback read (`ui/server.py` completion-review gate, now `_ps.get("pipeline_status")`), plus the `status` key from 21 pipeline-state test fixtures (Case A: drop the redundant key; the one Case-B status-only fixture renamed to `pipeline_status`).
