No production behaviour change — the fallback was unreachable.

  *Docs corrected:* **FD-1** infrastructure backstop `3600 / 7200 / "45-minute"` → **4500 s (75 min)**, uniform, across `CLAUDE.md` (poll sample, prose, Operational Constants table), the `heartbeat_cron.py` rationale comment, and `PIPELINE-SPEC.md`. **FD-3** `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` Tier-A stall defaults `planner 900s / executor 1800s / reviewer 900s` → the current two-knob reality (300 s stall threshold + a separate 600 s startup-grace window). **FD-4** the `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` "Staggered Timeouts" bullets described a 15-min cron SIGTERM that `heartbeat_cron.py` says was **removed** — rewritten to the real restart-only-when-dead crash recovery (`STALE_FLIGHT_THRESHOLD_MINUTES = 3`; a live orchestrator is never interrupted). `CLAUDE.md`'s `status`/`pipeline_status` narrative corrected (the false "`transition_state()` writes both" claim → it writes only `pipeline_status`; reset protocol, reset checklist, and the `SETUP.md` field list de-list `status`).

  *Added (drift guards — TDD red→green demonstrated via reversible injected drift):* `autodev/tests/test_skill_mapping_resolves_to_disk.py` — every mapped discipline in `skill_mapping.yaml` resolves to a real `SKILL.md` on disk (`completion` special-cased reviewer-only; a new mapping with no skills fails loudly); `tests/test_agent_id_set_parity.py` — the agent roster agrees across its four copies (`register_agent.AUTODEV_AGENT_IDS` source of truth, `ui/server._WORKSPACE_SYNC_AGENT_IDS`, the five `install.sh` `for agent in …; do` loops, and the `autodev/agents/` directories).

  *Tests (TDD, written first):* FD-2 red-first — `tests/test_api_git_recover.py` asserts git-recover writes `pipeline_status` and **not** `status` (red before the writer removal); `tests/test_api_completion_review_trigger.py` gains a fallback-free regression proving the gate works on `pipeline_status` alone. Full pipeline suite **955 green**; UI suite **1867 passed / 22 skipped** (the one full-run failure, `test_p0_ideas_convert`, is a pre-existing ideas/convert flake that **passes in isolation** — not a regression from this pass).

  *Out of scope (callouts):* the audit's Tier-2 (UI render-completeness of backend value sets) and Tier-3 (a code↔config port guard; the operational-constants prose table) items are deferred to a follow-up; this pass closes only FD-1/FD-2/FD-3/FD-4 + the two guards.

- **Observability remediation Phase 2 — emit + render the SILENT lifecycle/destructive events.** Five state transitions changed pipeline/queue state with **no timeline record**: an operator watching the activity feed could not see a nuclear reset, a queue stall, a project being parked after escalation, a parked project being revived with a banked answer, or a child being held on its parent. Phase 2 makes each a first-class event — emitted by `orchestrator.py` **and** rendered in the feed in the same change (so we don't recreate Phase 1's emitted-but-not-rendered gap).

  *Orchestrator (`autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py`) — five new `_write_pipeline_event` emits:* **`nuclear_reset`** (`{nuclear_resets, reason, phase}`) in `nuclear_reset_phase()` after the counter/`reset_log` write and before `reset_phase()` wipes the pointer — records the destructive *action* (the dispatch loop already emits `escalation_resolve` for the *command*); **`queue_halted`** (`{reason}`) inside the `if halt_if_no_eligible:` branch of `_select_next_queue_project()` right after `transition_state("QUEUE_HALTED")` (the reason-clearing `else` stays emit-free); **`queue_parked`** (`{reason, phase, entry_id, entry_name}`) in `_queue_park_active_entry()` after the successful write (one emit for all four call sites); **`queue_revived`** (`{entry_id, entry_name, command}`) in the revival branch; and **`dependency_hold`** (`{parent_id, entry_id, entry_name}`) on a genuine READY→DEPENDENCY_HOLD transition (an already-held entry is gated out before the assignment, so no re-emit). The four queue events use `agent="queue"`; `nuclear_reset` uses `agent="escalation"`. **`_apply_pending_escalation_command` now returns the applied command** (or `None` on no-pending-file / write-failure) — the minimal bridge that lets the revival branch name the banked command; the return value was previously unused by every caller, so adding it is non-breaking.

  *UI (`ui/index.html`):* each of the five events gains a badge colour (`getEventBadgeColor` — `nuclear_reset` red/destructive, `queue_halted` amber, `queue_revived` green, `queue_parked`/`dependency_hold` slate), a display label (`EVENT_TYPE_DISPLAY`), a hover description (`EVENT_TYPE_DESCRIPTION`), and a prose case (`humanizeSummary`, with `queue_halted` reason-mapped). There is no event allowlist — the feed already rendered unknown events via `default:` fallbacks, so these maps are pure enrichment.

  *Tests (TDD, written first, all green):* `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_nuclear_reset.py` (+1 — the `nuclear_reset` emit, patching `AUTODEV_PIPELINE_ROOT` since `_make_orch` doesn't); `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_queue.py` (+6 — halted emit, the halt-if-no-eligible-false negative guard, parked, revived via the real-apply path, dependency_hold, and the no-re-emit no-spam pin); and a new `tests/test_ui_observability_p2_render_events.py` (20 static-lint — 4 render maps × 5 events). Full pipeline suite **903 green**; UI suite **1871 passed / 13 skipped** (the two full-run failures — `test_api_ideas_convert` and `test_p0_stage_k_format_correction_smoke` — are pre-existing flakes in unrelated subsystems that **pass in isolation**, not Phase-2 regressions); JSX `text/babel` transpile clean (white-screen guard).

  *Docs:* `CLAUDE.md` event catalogue (+5 rows) and `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` event-catalogue section (parallel P2 table) updated in the same pass. *Out of scope (callouts):* the `all_completed`→`QUEUE_HALTED` labelling is pre-existing and only *recorded* honestly here, not changed; durable persistence of these signals into the metrics row is Phase 3; surfacing them in a queue/phase-history UI surface beyond the feed is a separate enhancement.

- **Escalation invalid-command heal — recoverable `STOP` + `escalation_command_invalid` signal (closes PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md §5.2).** The escalation-command consumer dead-ended any empty / missing / unrecognised `command` in `escalation_output.json` to `HALTED_SILENT` + queue `FAILED` — unrecoverable, with no UI exit (the documented CORE-4 incident: the agent wrote `{"command": "WAITING_FOR_HUMAN"}`, a state, not a command). It now emits a `[WARN]` log + a new **`escalation_command_invalid`** event (`{received_command, defaulted_to: "STOP"}`) and defaults to `STOP` — recoverable via the Resume control, matching the sibling fallbacks (`_apply_pending_escalation_command`, JSON-parse failure) that already default to `STOP`; the queue entry is no longer marked `FAILED`. The read default at the consume site is intentionally left `""` so empty / missing / unknown all flow through the one signalling path.

  *Agent contract hardened (Surface 2):* the escalation webhook default message (`webhook_client.py`) now names the required `command` field and enumerates the offerable verbs — `RETRY`, `RESET_PHASE`, `RESET_EXECUTION`, `RESET_REVIEWER`, `PROCEED`, `STOP` — with `{"command": "STOP"}` as the no-instruction default. `autodev/agents/escalation/AGENTS.md` adds a never-write-`.done`-without-a-valid-`command` rule; moves **`SKIP`** to an on-request-only "secret-menu" section (never offered proactively — skipping a phase another phase depends on can cascade); and documents **`NUCLEAR_RESET`** as a cap-gated last resort the agent surfaces **only** when `escalation_resets >= 3` (and `nuclear_resets < 2`), carrying the dashboard's last-resort warning. The webhook message deliberately omits both secret-menu verbs.

  *Tests (TDD, written first, all green):* `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_escalation_invalid_command.py` (10 — 7 behavioural driving `run()` one iteration incl. the real `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN` value, empty/missing variants, the queue-not-`FAILED` guard, the event payload shape, and a valid-`STOP`-takes-elif regression guard; 3 source-text guards locking in the `HALTED_SILENT`/`FAILED` removal) and `test_webhook_client_default_messages.py` (+3 — command-field requirement, offerable-verb enumeration, secret-menu omission). The `test_w2b_run_summary.py` floor stays green (removing the lone escalation `HALTED_SILENT` transition leaves 2, the required minimum; the new `STOPPED` transition keeps that guard green too). Full pipeline suite 891 green; UI suite 1815 passed / 13 skipped.

  *Docs:* `CLAUDE.md` event catalogue + terminal-states note; `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` Resume Commands note + Silent Halt Behavior bullet; `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md` §5.2 marked resolved; `GLOSSARY.md` `HALTED_SILENT` nuance. *Out of scope (callout):* the other `HALTED_SILENT` sites (escalation *delivery* failure, reviewer-verdict, stamp-init, exception double-fault) remain — they are delivery/contract failures, not bad-command handling.

- **P1 Stage G2 — Nuclear reset (operator escape hatch, cap 2).** Past the escalation reset budget (`escalation_resets >= 3`), the only controls were Abandon Phase / Stop — wrong when the operator fixed an *external* root cause (infra/config, outside the repo) *after* the retry budgets were already spent and wants a clean re-run. A new **`NUCLEAR_RESET`** command + **"Reset Everything & Restart Phase"** button closes that gap: a destructive true-fresh-start governed by its own `nuclear_resets` counter (cap **2**), **independent** of `escalation_resets`.

  *Orchestrator:* new `Orchestrator.nuclear_reset_phase()` — a thin wrapper over `reset_phase()` that reuses its mechanics verbatim (git reset to `phase_base_commit`, delete phase branch, wipe outputs, zero every retry counter, clear `prior_blame_attributions` by the wholesale phase_state replacement, re-plan from the planner with `_current_attempt_retry_class = "initial_attempt"`) and differs only in governance: it increments `nuclear_resets` and appends a `NUCLEAR_RESET` `reset_log` entry, then delegates to `reset_phase()`. The escalation-command dispatch gained a `NUCLEAR_RESET` branch that sends the same Signal "cap reached" notice the other resets use when `nuclear_resets >= 2`, else calls `nuclear_reset_phase()`. **`reset_phase()` now preserves `nuclear_resets` and `reset_log`** alongside the pre-existing `escalation_resets` (so the cap accumulates across the very reset it governs, and the audit trail survives) — this also **fixes a latent bug** where the `RESET_PHASE` dispatch appended a `reset_log` entry that `reset_phase()` then silently wiped. All three governance fields zero only on genuine phase advance (lazy re-init at 0; `nuclear_resets: 0` added to the three increment-fallback literals).

  *Server (`ui/server.py`):* `NUCLEAR_RESET` added to `VALID_COMMANDS`; **deliberately NOT** in `RESET_CAP_COMMANDS` (it must stay available exactly when `escalation_resets >= 3`). `_validate_command_request` gained a defaulted `nuclear_resets=0` param (the STOP-only `/api/pipeline/stop` call site is unaffected) and a `nuclear_resets >= 2 → 409` check; the three escalation call sites read and pass `nuclear_resets`. **Decision A (spec-literal):** the server enforces *only* the `nuclear_resets >= 2` cap — the "only when `escalation_resets >= 3`" rule is a UI-visibility guarantee, consistent with how `PROCEED`/`RESET_REVIEWER` availability is already UI-gated; a direct-API call is bounded by the cap-of-2, the `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN` precondition, and the `reset_log`. `_compute_escalation_view` surfaces `nuclear_resets` as a present-only field (resolving the "Forward note for G2" left on `_compute_escalation_view` during G3). The Monitor (`/api/state`) spreads the whole view (`response.update(_view)`), so it surfaces automatically; the Queue snapshot endpoint **cherry-picks** fields from the view into an explicit return dict, so `nuclear_resets` is lifted out there too (default 0, mirroring `escalation_resets`) — see the validation follow-up below.

  *UI (`ui/index.html`):* `NUCLEAR_RESET` added to `ESCALATION_CMD_DEFS` in its **own** `'nuclear'` group (NOT `'recover'`, which is hidden at `capReached` — the nuclear button must appear precisely then). The shared `EscalationCommandPanel` takes a `nuclear_resets` prop, computes `showNuclear = (escalation_resets >= 3) && (nuclear_resets < 2)`, and renders the button **outside** the `capReached` recover ternary. A dedicated `NuclearResetConfirmModal` carries the canonical destructive copy with a **WARNING** on its own emphasized line. Threaded into **both** call sites (Monitor `pState.nuclear_resets`, Queue `hubNuclearResets` off the per-entry snapshot), so the G3-consolidated panel gates the button identically in both views; Queue deferral rides the existing generic `target_project_path` dispatch.

  *Stage H interaction:* `NUCLEAR_RESET` is the 7th bankable escalation-panel command and rides the existing promote → revive → apply path with **zero** Stage-H code changes (the path is command-agnostic). Because revival restores the escalated-phase pointer incl. `phase_base_commit` before applying the banked command, a revived `NUCLEAR_RESET`'s `git reset --hard` lands on the revived project's repo (INVARIANT A). **Deliberately descoped:** making the escalation *agent* proactively offer `NUCLEAR_RESET` over Signal — the agent docs (`autodev/agents/escalation/AGENTS.md` / `USER.md`) are intentionally untouched; the dispatch handles the UI-banked path regardless, and not advertising a destructive command to a natural-language surface removes a destructive-by-natural-language trigger. A future addition, not this stage.

  *Tests (TDD, written first):* `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_nuclear_reset.py` (6 — wipe/replan, cap independence, the `nuclear_resets`/`reset_log` survive-`reset_phase` coupling pins, the audit-entry append, and the banked-`NUCLEAR_RESET`-revives Stage-H pin), `tests/test_api_command.py` (+4 — VALID/not-RESET_CAP membership, `nuclear_resets >= 2 → 409`, accepted-when-`escalation_resets >= 3`, and the parametrized valid-commands case), `tests/test_ui_nuclear_reset_button.py` (6 static-lint — defs membership, not-in-recover-group, two-cap gating, modal dispatch, the WARNING modal, and prop-threaded-in-both-views). Full pipeline suite 876 green; UI suite 1811 passed / 14 skipped (the two full-run failures — `test_api_ideas_convert` and `test_browser_path_selector::test_queue_add_autorepairs_git` — are pre-existing flakes in unrelated subsystems that **pass in isolation**, not G2 regressions); JSX `text/babel` transpile clean (white-screen guard).

  *Validation follow-up (post live-UI validation pass):* a browser validation confirmed the full first nuclear-reset cycle end-to-end on the **Monitor** — button gated correctly, the WARNING modal verbatim, `POST /api/command` 200, the planner actually re-invoked, and `phase_state.json` landing at `nuclear_resets: 1` / `escalation_resets: 3` with the `NUCLEAR_RESET` `reset_log` entry. The second-cycle / cap-hidden checks and the Queue-parity check were left unconfirmed when the OpenClaw backend died mid-run, so the behaviours they would have covered were **pinned with tests instead of relying on a flaky live env**: `test_two_nuclear_resets_accumulate_to_cap` (nuclear_resets 0→1→2 with a per-reset audit entry), `test_dispatch_nuclear_branch_enforces_cap_in_source` (the real dispatch branch gates `>= 2` → Signal notice, else `nuclear_reset_phase()`), and `test_snapshot_returns_nuclear_resets`. **That last test caught a real B9 defect:** the Queue `GET /api/queue/{id}/snapshot` endpoint builds an explicit return dict (it does **not** `response.update(_view)` like `/api/state`), so `nuclear_resets` was never surfaced to the Queue panel — `hubNuclearResets` would stay `0` and the Queue-view nuclear button would never hide at the cap. **Fixed** by lifting `nuclear_resets = _view.get("nuclear_resets", 0)` into the snapshot return dict (mirroring `escalation_resets`). The Monitor path was always correct (it spreads the view), which is why the live Monitor validation passed. 3 follow-up tests added (all green); a full UI-suite re-run after the snapshot fix was **clean — 1813 passed / 15 skipped / 0 failed** (the two earlier full-run flakes did not recur, confirming they were env/ordering flakes, not G2 regressions).

- **P1 Stage H — Parked-escalation revival (close the bank → revive → apply loop; recover from `QUEUE_HALTED`).** When a project escalated under an auto-queue it parked as `ESCALATION` and the queue advanced, but the loop never closed: banking the operator's answer wrote only the per-project `pending_escalation_command.json` and left the entry in `ESCALATION`, which selection/`trigger-next` never re-pick. The banked answer was orphaned, and when the escalated project was the last one the orchestrator dead-ended at `QUEUE_HALTED` with no UI way out. Two findings (confirmed by code-trace, both anticipated by the plan's Phase-1 gate) made this larger than the original "state-flip" sketch: (1) `pipeline_state.json` is **global** and selection unconditionally resets it to a blank phase-0/planner state, so a naive revival would *restart* the project rather than resume the escalated phase; (2) a relaunch into `QUEUE_HALTED` carries `current_agent="escalation"`, so the startup gate skips selection and — because `QUEUE_HALTED` is intentionally not in the loop-exit set — the loop polls `escalation_output` forever (a silent spin, not a clean exit).

  *Closed loop:* a new **`ESCALATION_ANSWERED`** queue-**entry** state (anchored in `autodev/pipeline/queue_semantics.py`; **not** a `pipeline_status`, so deliberately absent from `VALID_STATES`). The orchestrator owns the flip: `_promote_answered_escalations` (a pre-pass in `_select_next_queue_project`) promotes `ESCALATION → ESCALATION_ANSWERED` for any parked row whose project has a banked `pending_escalation_command.json`; selection then admits it as a second eligible class and **revives** it. **Single-writer preserved** — the server still writes only the per-project pending file; every `pipeline_queue.json` `state` write stays orchestrator-owned (no new locks; `CLAUDE.md`'s two-writer model unchanged).

  *Resume-correctness (capture & restore):* `_queue_park_active_entry` now snapshots the global phase pointer the queue-advance would destroy into `parked_state_snapshot` (`current_phase`, `current_phase_raw_id`, the five retry counters, **`phase_base_commit`**, `phase_start_time`). The revival branch restores that snapshot **instead of** the phase-0 reset, so the banked command (`RESET_PHASE` / `PROCEED` / `SKIP` / …) acts on the *escalated* phase. `phase_base_commit` is load-bearing: `reset_phase()` guards its `git reset --hard` on it, so without it a revived `RESET_PHASE` would resume on a dirty tree. `escalation_resets`/`reset_log` are **not** snapshotted (they live in the per-project `phase_state.json`, which survives via the symlink). **INVARIANT A:** `update_symlink` stays first and shared in the activation block — the branch splits only the `self.state` write — so the restored pointer and the banked command always act on the *revived* project's repo (a pinned test guards against a refactor moving the split above the symlink repoint, which would `git reset --hard` the wrong project).

  *`QUEUE_HALTED` recovery (INVARIANT B):* new `Orchestrator._maybe_revive_on_queue_halted()` runs once at `run()` startup, before the `current_agent`-gated startup function. It promotes banked answers and revives a parked project; if there is genuinely nothing to consume it returns `False` and `run()` exits **cleanly** instead of spinning — but it continues into the loop when an in-place `escalation_output.done` is already pending, preserving the pre-existing restart-with-an-in-place-answer recovery. `QUEUE_HALTED` is deliberately **not** added to the loop-exit set (that would kill the in-loop poll for an in-place answer). A new **`answered_pending_revival`** halt-reason (orchestrator + `ui/server.py:_queue_trigger_next_halted_reason`, tested before `all_blocked`) marks the queue as recoverable rather than dead-stalled. The UI reuses the existing `POST /api/queue/{entry_id}/relaunch` behind a **"Resume banked answer"** button (shown only when the queue is halted with ≥1 `ESCALATION_ANSWERED` entry) and an **"Answer banked"** queue pill + compact detail card (which deliberately does **not** re-render the full `EscalationCommandPanel`). The deferred-bank endpoint now also accepts an already-`ESCALATION_ANSWERED` entry (re-banking a newer command), still without writing the queue.

  *Tests (TDD, written first, all green):* 28 new tests — `autodev/tests/test_queue_semantics_escalation_answered.py` (5), `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_queue.py::TestParkedEscalationRevival` (15, incl. the resume-correctness gate `test_revived_entry_restores_escalated_phase_pointer_not_phase_zero` with explicit phase-0 negatives, the INVARIANT-A symlink-target guard, the INVARIANT-B clean-exit-not-spin trio, and the headline `test_multi_project_park_advance_bank_resume_end_to_end`), `tests/test_queue_api.py` (+3: deferred accepts answered, parked-fallback accepts answered, `answered_pending_revival` reason), and UI static-lint `tests/test_ui_h_escalation_answered.py` (4) + `tests/test_ui_queue_escalation_uses_shared_panel.py` (+1). Pre-existing deferred-write and halt-reason tests stay green unchanged (the payoff of the single-writer design). JSX `text/babel` transpile clean (white-screen guard).

  *Validation follow-up (post UI-validation pass):* a browser validation surfaced two UX gaps in the answered-entry surface, now fixed. **(B1) The "Answer banked" pill was masked** whenever a parked entry carried a `live_pipeline_status` (parked `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN`, or a global `QUEUE_HALTED`/`STOPPED`), because `queueRowDisplay` consulted the live-status map first — so the operator's banked answer wasn't visible on the row. `queueRowDisplay` now short-circuits to the "Answer banked" pill for an answered/banked parked entry **before** the live-status override. **(B3ii) The "Resume banked answer" recovery button never appeared in the common case** — a single project parked `ESCALATION`, the orchestrator exited to `QUEUE_HALTED`, then the operator banks an answer. The button gated on `ESCALATION_ANSWERED`, but only the *running* orchestrator promotes `ESCALATION → ESCALATION_ANSWERED`; with it dead the row stays `ESCALATION`, so the button (and the "Answer banked" detail card) stayed hidden and recovery fell back to the generic relaunch. Fix: `GET /api/queue` now exposes a read-only `has_banked_answer` per parked-escalation entry (probes `pending_escalation_command.json`; never writes the queue — single-writer preserved), and the UI treats `ESCALATION + has_banked_answer` the same as `ESCALATION_ANSWERED` for the pill, the `answeredEntries` gate, and the detail card. Functional recovery already worked via relaunch; this makes the dedicated affordance reach the case it was built for. **(B5/B6 triage — no code change):** the live headline run was incomplete (couldn't reliably stage A-escalates-then-advances within the run window) and the live `QUEUE_HALTED` revival landed on phase 0 — both traced to a *pre-phase / repo-init escalation* (empty `parked_state_snapshot` by design — there is no escalated phase to resume) or an injected fixture, NOT a capture bug: the mid-phase escalation path parks with `current_phase_raw_id` intact (gate handlers set `current_agent="escalation"` + status `RUNNING`, and the escalation block parks at the still-set phase before advancing). Locked by `test_park_captures_state_snapshot` (non-empty capture), `test_revived_entry_restores_escalated_phase_pointer_not_phase_zero` (mid-phase restore), and a new `test_revival_with_empty_snapshot_restores_phase_zero_gracefully` (pre-phase revival is graceful + correct-by-design). Follow-up tests: `tests/test_queue_api.py::TestGetQueue::test_has_banked_answer_flag_for_parked_escalation`, `tests/test_ui_h_escalation_answered.py` (+3: pill-precedence, answeredEntries-covers-banked, detail-card-covers-banked). **(B4) `trigger-next` halt-reason consistency:** a parked `ESCALATION` row with a banked answer the orchestrator hadn't promoted yet reported `all_blocked` (generic stall toast) while the UI already showed the **Resume banked answer** button — an internal inconsistency. Extracted `_entry_has_banked_answer(entry)` (a read-only `pending_escalation_command.json` probe, now the single source for both `GET /api/queue`'s `has_banked_answer` and the halt-reason — DRY), and `_queue_trigger_next_halted_reason` now returns `answered_pending_revival` for an answered-or-banked parked row so the toast matches the Resume affordance. Guarded by `test_queue_halted_reason_revival_for_unpromoted_banked_escalation` + `test_queue_halted_reason_all_blocked_when_no_banked_answer` (no over-broadening — a parked `ESCALATION` with no banked answer still reports `all_blocked`). Removed the now-unused `REVIVABLE_ANSWERED_STATES` import from `ui/server.py`.

- **P1 Stage F — Reachability advisory (COMPLETE-phase only).** Executor gate now runs a static import-graph walk from `verification.entry_point.command` after every blocking check has passed, and surfaces a single summarising `reachability_warning` pipeline event per phase when manifest files cannot be reached from the entry point. Advisory only — never fails the phase; the gate exit code is unchanged. **Scoped to `COMPLETE-*` phases**: reachability-from-entry is a whole-artifact property, and running it per phase would misclassify the routine add-then-wire roadmap pattern (e.g. `DATA-E1` adds a localStorage utility; `DATA-E2` wires it in a later phase) as orphan code. Warning copy is hedged accordingly ("declared in manifest but not reached from entry point — orphan, or wiring landed in a different entry's path"); the UI summary reads *"N file(s) not reached from entry — a.py, b.py, c.py. Confirm intent: orphan vs. wiring landed elsewhere."*

  *Resolver coverage:* Python (`python`, `python -m`, `uvicorn`, `flask`, `gunicorn`, `hypercorn`) via `ast` parsing; JS/TS (`node`, `npm`, `vite`, `tsx`, `ts-node`, `yarn`, `pnpm`, plus `npx <tool>` which strips the wrapper before classification) via pure-Python regex parsing. Other languages emit a single `no_resolver` warning. Test-runner entries (`pytest`, `jest`, `vitest`, `playwright`, `cypress`, `mocha`, `unittest`) emit a distinct `reachability_not_applicable` event so visibility is preserved without polluting the warning channel.

  *Two-channel artifact pattern:* advisory gate output is written to a new `executor_advisory_detail.json`, separate from the existing `executor_gate_detail.json` (which remains the failure-detail channel consumed by `write_failure_context` for executor self-heal). The orchestrator's new `Orchestrator._emit_reachability_advisory(raw_id)` drains the advisory file on the executor PASS path, emits one summarising `reachability_warning` event + one `reachability_not_applicable` event when set + one event per diagnostic, then removes the file. Future advisory checks extend the advisory file's envelope rather than co-tenanting in the failure file. Promotion of reachability to a blocking (and per-phase) gate is **P3 Stage A**, gated on resolver coverage matching the demo project mix, measured false-positive rate < 10%, and a `pure_library: true` suppression mechanism for the add-then-wire false-positive class.

  *Added (new files):* `autodev/pipeline/gate_scripts/reachability/__init__.py`, `registry.py`, `python.py`, `js_ts.py` (co-located with `utils.py` so the existing gate sibling-import mechanism applies — no second `sys.path` hack). UI rendering in `ui/index.html`: `reachability_warning` events get a yellow badge, `reachability_not_applicable` get a slate badge, both distinct from failures (red), gate diagnostics (purple), and `gate_fail` (amber). Documentation in `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` §4 (Reachability Advisory subsection) + new §4.5 (Advisory Checks vs Blocking Gates pattern + promotion criteria) + event-catalogue rows; `CLAUDE.md` event-catalogue rows + Gate Script Interface Contract advisory-channel paragraph.

  *Changed:* `phase_resolver.py` now parses `verification.md`'s `## Entry point` section into a structured `entry_point: {command, ready_signal}` dict written onto `current_phase.json` — additive field; pre-existing consumers ignore unknown keys. **Each bullet parses independently** (departs from `_extract_behavioral_verification`'s all-or-nothing rule because reachability needs only `command`; coupling it to `ready_signal` would silently disable the check whenever the unrelated field was absent). `executor_gate.py` gains `_check_reachability_advisory`, `_write_advisory_detail`, and `_clear_advisory_detail` helpers; the existing `_clear_executor_gate_detail()` on the PASS path stays untouched — the new advisory file is on its own channel. `orchestrator.py` adds `executor_advisory_detail.json` to all four per-phase artifact cleanup lists (`reset_phase`, `reset_execution`, `write_failure_context`, the trailing reset path).

  *Tests (TDD, written first, all green):* 75 new tests across `autodev/tests/test_reachability_python.py` (8 tests), `test_reachability_js_ts.py` (8), `test_reachability_unsupported_language.py` (17), `test_phase_resolver_entry_point.py` (7), `test_executor_gate_reachability_advisory.py` (10), `test_orchestrator_reachability_events.py` (7), and `tests/test_ui_reachability_warning_rendering.py` (9). The load-bearing scoping guard is `test_non_complete_phase_skips_check_entirely` — it pins the design decision that prevents per-phase cry-wolf and would catch any future regression that drops the COMPLETE-prefix gate. Full pipeline suite: 842 passing. UI suite: 1812 passing (1 pre-existing Ideas-convert flake, unrelated subsystem).

### Changed

- **P1 Stage G3 follow-up — single-source the per-entry snapshot for the Queue escalation panel.** G3 made `EscalationCommandPanel` one shared component but exposed a **pre-existing data-sourcing bug** in the Queue view: the panel's **display** advisory came from `GET /api/state` (hardcoded to the **active** symlink project) while **dispatch** correctly targeted the **selected** queue project (`targetProjectPath`). For a *parked* (non-active) `ESCALATION` entry the panel therefore described the **wrong/empty** project while acting on the right one. Dispatch was never misrouted (the server re-validates the target's real state), but the operator could read project B's advisory while issuing a command to project A. The bug was broader than the headline: the **whole advisory block** (headline, advisory summary, suggested action, internal reason, advisory status) came from the active project, the **cap counter** (`escalation_resets`) read `0` for parked entries, and **button visibility** (`executor_output_exists` / `merge_probe_passed`) was hard-defaulted because the snapshot omitted those fields entirely.

  *Fix:* `GET /api/queue/{id}/snapshot` is now the single **complete** source for the *selected* entry's escalation view, and the Queue UI reads **all** escalation fields from the snapshot. The panel now describes exactly the project the dispatch targets.

  *Changed (`ui/server.py`):* extracted **`_compute_escalation_view(project_root, *, phase_state_path, queue_halted_reason, current_phase_raw_id)`** — one computation consumed by **both** `/api/state` (the active project) and the snapshot (the selected entry), so the two can no longer drift (the exact failure mode that caused this bug). It returns always-present probes (`executor_output_exists`, `planner_output_exists`, `phase_branch_exists`, `merge_probe_passed`) plus the present-only phase_state advisory fields (mirroring `get_state`'s "absent when unset" semantics). `get_state` was refactored to call it — **behavior-preserving** (its tests stay green unchanged); the two phase_state fields the snapshot sources *live* (`last_action_timestamp`, `waiting_for_human_resolved_at`) stay `get_state`-local. `get_queue_entry_snapshot` was un-gated (was `if is_active_project and project_path:`, now runs the helper for **any** entry against the **entry's own** `project_path`) and gained **`_resolve_entry_raw_id`** so a parked entry's merge/branch probes use its OWN phase id (from its `phase_state.json` / `current_phase.json`), skipping gracefully when unresolvable. The snapshot return dict gained `escalation_headline`, `escalation_advisory_status`, `escalation_recommended_action`, `executor_output_exists`, `planner_output_exists`, `phase_branch_exists`, `merge_probe_passed`. Live `pipeline_state`-sourced fields (`pipeline_status`, retries, `last_action`, …) still stay `null` for non-active entries. **Forward note for G2:** `nuclear_resets` joins the helper's present-only list when G2 lands — the single wiring point so its button gates correctly in **both** panels without re-introducing two sources.

  *Changed (`ui/index.html`, QueueScreen):* deleted the Queue `/api/state` escalation `useEffect`, the 5 advisory `useState` hooks, and `selectedLiveForEscalationMsg`; the 5 advisory values are now snapshot-derived `const`s computed beside the existing eligibility locals in `QueueActionHub`. The `<EscalationCommandPanel>` Queue call-site **prop names are unchanged** (so the G3 prop-pass-through tests keep passing); only the variables' source changed (state → snapshot). No new polling — the advisory refreshes on selection / `snapshotVersion` bump, consistent with the eligibility fields.

  *Tests (TDD, written first / RED before implementing):* `tests/test_w5_queue_snapshot_detail_pane.py` — **inverted** `test_snapshot_phase_state_fields_null_when_not_active` → `…_from_own_project_when_not_active` (it formerly asserted the bug), and **added** `test_snapshot_parked_escalation_returns_own_advisory_not_active_project`, a no-phase_state defaults variant, and a parked 500-cap variant. UI static-lint: `test_ui_escalation_header_no_blame.py` now asserts the Queue reads `snapshot.escalation_headline` (was `/api/state`); migrated `test_ui_escalation_command_regressions.py`'s queue-identity test to assert the snapshot effect's `[selectedId, snapshotVersion]` dependency and the removal of `selectedLiveForEscalationMsg` / `setEscalationMsg`; added `test_ui_queue_escalation_uses_shared_panel.py::test_queuescreen_does_not_fetch_api_state_for_escalation` (scoped to the QueueScreen block, since `/api/state` legitimately appears in the Monitor + App bootstrap). All `/api/state` tests stay green unchanged (proving the helper extraction is transparent). Full pipeline suite: **849 passing**. UI suite: **1788 passing, 13 skipped** (2 pre-existing flakes — Ideas-convert + format-correction smoke — both pass in isolation; neither references the escalation surface). JSX `text/babel` transpile clean (white-screen guard).

  *Docs:* `autodev/docs/AUTODEV-UI-PRD.md` (Escalation Command Panel — the Queue now sources all escalation fields from the per-entry snapshot, so a parked entry shows its own advisory). `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` carries no snapshot-contract section, so it is untouched.

- **P1 Stage G3 — consolidate the two escalation panels into one shared component.** The escalation/recovery surface was implemented twice — `EscalationCommandPanel` (Pipeline Monitor) and a near-verbatim inline copy inside `QueueActionHub` (Queue view) — which had drifted in state-sourcing, fallback copy, button rendering, and modal/dispatch wiring (G1's de-blame had to be applied to both by hand). G3 makes `EscalationCommandPanel` the **single shared component rendered by BOTH views**; the Queue's forked inline render is removed. The three advisory states (generating / ready / fallback) and the G1 de-blame (advisory-or-clean-headline + the "Internal reason" disclosure) now exist in exactly one place. **Decision (user-confirmed): the Queue stays light** — the Monitor-only orchestrator-lifecycle chrome (Restart Orchestrator button, the "command submitted — waiting" screen, the orchestrator-down warning) is prop-gated **off** in the Queue, preserving today's Queue behaviour; deferred dispatch has no live orchestrator to restart. **Scope:** G3 only — G2 (nuclear reset) is a separate later change.

  *Changed:* `ui/index.html` — `EscalationCommandPanel` gains four control props (`targetProjectPath`, `onDispatched`, `showOrchestratorLifecycle`, `showCommandSentScreen`); its buttons now map over the shared `ESCALATION_CMD_DEFS` (single source of truth) with the **exact prior Monitor visibility rules** (recover hidden at cap; Re-run Reviewer needs executor output; Mark Complete needs merge AND not-cap); STOP now uses the dedicated `StopConfirmModal` in **both** views (the Monitor previously showed the generic `ConfirmationModal` for STOP); the fallback copy is unified to one context-neutral string (*"Automated review unavailable — the analysis could not be generated. See the pipeline log for full context."*), replacing the Monitor's "See Pipeline log…" and the Queue's self-referential "Open Pipeline Monitor…". Dispatch is unified onto `POST /api/command` with an optional `target_project_path`: the Queue's **deferred-hold is preserved** — a command issued for a *parked* (non-active) ESCALATION project is written as `pending_escalation_command` and fires when the orchestrator picks that project back up — and STOP rides the same path (server-equivalent to `/api/stop` while `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN`, the panel's only render state). The Monitor call site passes `showOrchestratorLifecycle`/`showCommandSentScreen={true}`; the Queue passes `targetProjectPath` + `onDispatched={fetchQueue}`.

  *Removed:* the Queue's forked escalation render and its now-dead machinery — `renderQueueEscalationButton`, the `hub*` display locals (`hubAdvisoryText`/`hubHeaderText`/`hubCounterText`/`hubRecoverCmds`/`hubAdvanceCmds`/`hubStopDef`/`hubHidden*Items`), the `advisoryExpanded` `useState` (**which also removes a rules-of-hooks violation** — it sat after the function's early `return`), the `capReached` local, the Queue dispatch trio (`handleCommand`/`hubModalConfirm`/`hubModalDismiss`), and the `hubModalCommand`/`hubConfirmBusy` state; plus the panel's `sendCommand` (folded into the unified dispatcher) and its dead `isResetDisabled` const. **The general "Stop Pipeline" header control is untouched and still posts `/api/stop`** (the RUNNING-pipeline kill-switch via the `pipeline_stop_requested` sentinel); only the escalation panel's STOP branch moved to `/api/command`.

  *Tests (TDD):* this consolidation is dominated by **test migration**, and the original roadmap done-criterion *"existing API + UI + orchestrator tests pass unchanged"* is only partly accurate — backend tests are unchanged-green, but UI static-lint tests structurally coupled to TWO panels **had to migrate** when two panels became one. New `tests/test_ui_escalation_panel_shared.py` (8: single component in both views, three advisory states, fallback unified, STOP→`StopConfirmModal`, unified dispatch carrying `target_project_path`, Monitor button-set reproduced off `ESCALATION_CMD_DEFS`, general-Stop `/api/stop` caller retained). Rewrote `tests/test_ui_escalation_header_no_blame.py` (de-blame asserted on the single component; `"Internal reason"` now appears **exactly once**; the Queue feeds the de-blamed fields as props). Replaced `tests/test_ui_queue_escalation_parity.py` (its premise — two hand-maintained panels — no longer exists) with `tests/test_ui_queue_escalation_uses_shared_panel.py` (Queue renders the shared component; deferred-hold `target_project_path` preserved; lifecycle chrome stays off). Migrated `tests/test_ui_escalation_command_panel.py`: **removed the fake-coverage pure-logic classes** that re-implemented panel logic in Python and never read `index.html` (`TestEscalationCommandPanel`, `TestEscalationCommandPanelIntegration`, `TestConfirmationModal`, `TestResetCapEnforcement`), removed the duplicate GRID-label class, repointed the structural classes at the shared component, and rewrote the handleCommand class for the allowlist-free design. Migrated the one `target_project_path` test in `tests/test_ui_escalation_command_regressions.py`. Added `tests/test_queue_api.py::TestPostCommandDeferred::test_deferred_holds_stop_for_parked_project` (a Queue STOP for a parked, non-active project is HELD, not acted on immediately — global status `RUNNING`). Full pipeline suite: **849 passing**. UI suite: **1777 passing, 14 skipped** (3 pre-existing flakes in unrelated subsystems — Ideas-convert, browser path-selector, format-correction smoke — all pass in isolation; none reference the escalation surface).

  *Docs:* `autodev/docs/AUTODEV-UI-PRD.md` (Escalation Command Panel section — now describes one shared component rendered identically in both views, with the deferred-hold and the Queue-stays-light gating) and `plans/Active/post-p0-ordered-roadmap.md` (G3 done-criterion ticked; the "tests pass unchanged" note corrected). The `NUCLEAR_RESET` / `nuclear_resets` documentation in `PIPELINE-SPEC.md` and the `CLAUDE.md` operational-constants row remain for **G2**.

- **P1 Stage G1 — de-blame the escalation surface.** When the pipeline escalates to a human, the panel headline no longer renders the raw internal blame-attribution string (e.g. *"Impl blame cap reached (4x): [L3] Insufficient evidence for confident attribution; defaulting to impl."*). The headline now reads in plain English — the LLM advisory summary when `escalation_advisory_status === "ready"`, otherwise a clean, deterministic `escalation_headline` (e.g. *"Phase REND-E1 needs your input"*) — and the raw trigger reason is demoted into the panel's collapsible "Internal reason" disclosure (preserved, not deleted). The pre-escalation LLM advisory is de-blamed at the source: it is no longer fed `escalation_trigger_reason` or `prior_blame_attributions`, and the project's user-voice `failure_language` now reaches it on **every** escalation that carries it (the `reviewer_retries >= 2` gate was removed), so executor-self-failure escalations get the product's own copy instead of parroted jargon. **Scope:** G1 only — `run_blame_attribution()` and the L1/L2/L3 logic are untouched (a separate parked investigation); G2 (nuclear reset) and G3 (panel consolidation) are not in this change.

  *Added:* `Orchestrator._clean_escalation_headline(raw_id=None)` (deterministic phase-level headline, derived from the phase id so it can never echo the blame string); a new additive `escalation_headline` phase_state field written at all three escalation triggers (main loop, crash handler, repo-init) and served by `GET /api/state`.

  *Changed:* `_generate_escalation_advisory` drops the two blame-framed keys from the LLM payload and builds the `behavioral_verification` block whenever `failure_language` is present (regardless of `reviewer_retries`); its system prompt no longer names a `reviewer_retries >= 2` precondition. `ui/index.html`: both escalation panels — `EscalationCommandPanel` (Pipeline Monitor) and `QueueActionHub` (Queue view) — bind the headline to the advisory-ready / `escalation_headline` expression and carry an "Internal reason" disclosure line (applied by hand to both until G3 consolidates them). `ui/server.py` passes `escalation_headline` through `/api/state`.

  *Removed:* the `reviewer_retries >= 2` gate on the advisory's `failure_language` block, and the two tests that pinned the removed behavior — `test_user_message_behavioral_block_is_none_when_reviewer_retries_lt_2` and `test_system_prompt_instructs_verbatim_failure_language_quote` (the latter is now fully covered by the new deblame test; keeping it would be a redundant passing artifact).

  *Tests (TDD, written first, all green):* `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_escalation_advisory_deblame.py` (4 — payload omits both blame keys; `failure_language` present at `reviewer_retries < 2`; system prompt has no retry precondition), `autodev/tests/test_orchestrator_escalation_headline.py` (5 — phase-id headline, explicit-arg, generic fallback, "unknown" sentinel → generic, blame-ignoring), `tests/test_api_state_escalation_headline.py` (2 — served when present, omitted when absent), `tests/test_ui_escalation_header_no_blame.py` (7 — old bindings gone in both panels, clean bindings present, field wired through, raw reason relocated to disclosure). Full pipeline suite: 849 passing. UI suite: 1821 passing, 13 skipped (1 pre-existing Ideas-message timeout flake, unrelated subsystem — passes in isolation).

  *Docs:* `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` (`escalation_headline` schema entry, reworked `escalation_trigger_reason` description, `failure_language` note, and a "P1 Stage G1 — escalation advisory de-blame" contract paragraph) and `autodev/docs/AUTODEV-UI-PRD.md` (Escalation Command Panel header section).

- **OpenClaw truncation-settings audit — raised the bootstrap cap and fixed the post-compaction refresh so the Stage A universal rules actually reach the agents.** Stage A relocated the universal wiring + testing-quality rules into each role's `AGENTS.md` on the premise that "OpenClaw loads AGENTS.md as primary context every turn." The premise is correct, but a runtime audit (`plans/Active/metaprompt-2-truncation-settings-audit.md`) found two truncation regimes were silently defeating it: (1) the per-file bootstrap cap `bootstrapMaxChars` defaults to **12000**, while every pipeline role's `AGENTS.md` is larger (planner 15.5k, executor 20.5k, reviewer 23k) and its `## Always-Apply:` sections begin past byte ~10k — so the rules were cut off at injection; (2) after a context compaction, OpenClaw re-injects only the `AGENTS.md` sections named in `agents.defaults.compaction.postCompactionSections` (default `["Session Startup","Red Lines"]` — names our docs do **not** contain) capped at `postCompactionMaxChars` (default 1800), so the rules were dropped entirely on every compaction. Compaction was confirmed real and recurring in production (20 session checkpoints across pipeline agents; one executor phase compacted 3× with `compactionSummary.Constraints/Rules: "None"`).

  *Changed (live `~/.openclaw/openclaw.json`, atomic + backed up):* `bootstrapMaxChars = 32000` on all six AutoDev agents (planner, executor, reviewer, escalation, prd-creator, roadmap-converter); `contextLimits.postCompactionMaxChars = 8000` on the three pipeline roles (the two Always-Apply sections measure ≤4.6k combined — 8k is sized headroom, not a full-doc dump); `agents.defaults.compaction.postCompactionSections` merged to `["Always-Apply: Integration Wiring", "Always-Apply: Testing Quality", "Session Startup", "Red Lines"]` (our headers first, OpenClaw's defaults preserved). Only AutoDev agents were touched — the non-AutoDev `personal-assistant` entry and `compaction.mode: "safeguard"` were left intact. No change to regimes 2 (`maxSkillsPromptChars`) or 4 (`textChunkLimit`), nor to `bootstrapTotalMaxChars` — measured and found not load-bearing for our agents (a valid null result).

  *Added (installer seed, dual-path):* `setup_helpers.ensure_openclaw_context_limits()` idempotently enforces all three keys on existing installs and is wired into `install.sh` step 8 (with a summary line and `CTX_LIMITS_STEP` status); `register_agent._build_new_entry` now seeds the same keys so freshly registered agents are born correct. The two installer modules carry duplicate constants by necessity (`register_agent` runs as a standalone script) and are kept in lockstep by a drift-guard test. Canonical values live in `setup_helpers` (`AUTODEV_BOOTSTRAP_MAX_CHARS`, `AUTODEV_POSTCOMPACTION_MAX_CHARS`, `AUTODEV_POSTCOMPACTION_AGENT_IDS`, `AUTODEV_POSTCOMPACTION_SECTIONS`).

  *Removed:* the stale Stage A leftover `~/.openclaw/workspace-executor/skills/testing-quality-executor/` (a pre-Stage-A `SKILL.md` for the deleted `testing-quality` discipline; the workspace dir is now correctly empty and is re-cleaned by `_clean_workspace_skills()` on every injection). The repo itself was already clean — all surviving `testing-quality`/`integration-wiring` references are intentional guard tests and docs.

  *Tests (TDD, written first, all green):* `autodev/tests/test_setup_helpers.py` — 11 new tests covering the helper (bootstrap on all six; post-compaction cap on the pipeline three only; sections seeding/merge/dedupe; idempotency; unrelated-key preservation; non-AutoDev-agent isolation; missing-file error) plus two static drift guards (`test_postcompaction_sections_match_real_agents_md_headers`, `test_postcompaction_cap_covers_largest_always_apply_block`) that fail if an `AGENTS.md` header is renamed or its Always-Apply block outgrows the cap. `autodev/tests/test_register_agent.py` — 3 new tests (per-agent bootstrap seed, pipeline-only post-compaction seed, and the cross-module constant drift guard). Full suites: 776 (`autodev/tests/`) + 1803 (`tests/`) passing.

- **Agent guidance efficiency pass — tightened wording and removed stale infra/model facts across the six system-agent bootstrap docs.** Editorial pass over `autodev/agents/{planner,executor,reviewer,escalation,prd-creator,roadmap-converter}/` guidance files, applied identically to the repo source-of-truth and the live `~/.openclaw/workspace-<agent>/` runtime copies (verified byte-for-byte). Fidelity rule held throughout: tighten only genuine waste; preserve intent, emphasis, and every domain-specific value. 13 files, +16/−31 lines.

  *Changed (efficiency):* planner `AGENTS.md` collapsed the duplicated `planner_retries > 0` retry instruction (it was stated in full in both Inputs and Retry Behavior). reviewer `AGENTS.md`'s `prd.md` Inputs bullet no longer repeats the full "PRD wins → `attribution: \"plan\"`" routing rule verbatim — that rule remains stated once in *What to Actually Review*; the `prd.md`-before-`current_phase.json` reading order is preserved. planner/executor/reviewer `AGENTS.md` dropped the provenance aside "(Formerly an injected base skill; now part of your standing identity.)" from the Integration Wiring intro (3 identical occurrences — no actionable content).

  *Removed (stale, drift-prone facts):* baked-in model names and fixed context-window sizes from every agent doc that named them — executor (`Qwen3-Coder-Next … 64K … Q3_K_XL`, Sonnet fallback), reviewer/prd-creator/roadmap-converter (`MiniMax M2.7 via OpenRouter`), escalation (`Anthropic Claude Sonnet (cloud)`), and the `65K`/`96K`/`32K` numbers in executor/reviewer `AGENTS.md`/`TOOLS.md`/`IDENTITY.md`. The live `openclaw.json` had already drifted from all of these (worker agents are now Kimi K2.6 / planner Sonnet 4.6 / escalation local Qwen); per-agent model is resolved from config each invocation, so naming it in identity docs only misleads operators running a different config. Generic "read efficiently / context-aware" discipline is kept (model-agnostic).

  *Removed (dead capability):* the escalation agent's local-model Health Check guidance (`## Health Check Capability` section + the `curl …/health` and `ps aux | grep llama` shell bullets in `escalation/AGENTS.md` and `escalation/TOOLS.md`). The `:9000` traffic-cop endpoint it cited is retired (PIPELINE-SPEC §8) and absent from code/config. Read-only `ls`/`find`/`cat` access retained.

  *Corrected:* escalation `USER.md` resume-command count `five` → `seven` — the operator-facing count once Stage G ships (8 total commands minus the never-shown-to-operator `SKIP`); the agent's full 7-command reference table in `AGENTS.md` is unchanged.

  *Tests:* no new tests and no guard-phrase edits — no guarded token was reworded. The six content-guard suites that pin these docs stay green as the regression net: `autodev/tests/test_agents_md_universal_rules.py`, `autodev/tests/test_p0_stage_g_data_path_integration.py`, and `tests/test_p0_{planner,executor,reviewer,roadmap_converter}_agent_md.py` (28 passed, before and after).

  *Docs / out of scope:* this entry. The architecture docs (`PIPELINE-SPEC.md`, `PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md`, `AUTODEV-PROJECT-VISION.md`, `PIPELINE-ROADMAP.md`) still carry stale worker-agent model names (e.g. "MiniMax M2.5") and `:9000` traffic-cop references; they describe pipeline architecture (a separate source of truth) and were intentionally not touched in this agent-doc pass.

- **P1 Stage A refactor — universal rules moved from injected base skills into role `AGENTS.md`.** The original P1 Stage A (see the superseded `### Added` entry below) delivered the right outcome through the wrong mechanism: it injected `integration-wiring` and `testing-quality` as always-present workspace skills. An always-applied rule is standing identity, not a per-phase skill, and `skills/` is meant for the *variable* layer — so the rules were relocated to primary context and the base-skill machinery removed. Outcome is unchanged: every phase still applies the universal wiring + testing-quality rules. Unblocks a simpler P2 Stage B (multi-skill bundles) by keeping the workspace skills directory single-layer.

  *Added:* `## Always-Apply: Integration Wiring` and `## Always-Apply: Testing Quality` sections in each of `autodev/agents/{planner,executor,reviewer}/AGENTS.md`, carrying the full rule content from the former SKILL.md files (compressed on inline; every rule preserved — verified by a source-vs-AGENTS.md diff). OpenClaw loads AGENTS.md as standing primary context every turn, so these now have higher attention prominence than a `skills/` subdirectory had. **(Injection confirmed by the later truncation-settings audit — see the entry at the top of this section. That audit also found the injection was being truncated at OpenClaw's 12000-char `bootstrapMaxChars` default and dropped entirely on compaction until the caps were raised; the Stage A premise holds, but only once those caps reach the sections.)**

  *Removed:* `SkillManager.BASE_DISCIPLINES` constant; the always-inject loop and the `is_base` parameter / `base=true|false` log markers in `autodev/pipeline/skill_manager.py` (`inject_skill` reverts to single-discipline injection, keeping the single-cleanup-at-start contract); the `_record_injected_skill` base-discipline filter in `autodev/pipeline/orchestrator.py` (dead once the constant is gone); the `INTEGRATION` / `TEST` / `E2E` entries in `autodev/config/skill_mapping.yaml` (those prefixes are now intentionally unmapped — re-adding them would point at a deleted directory); and the six `SKILL.md` files under `autodev/skill-library/integration-wiring/` and `autodev/skill-library/testing-quality/` (deleted via `git rm`; retrievable from history).

  *Behavioural nuance:* `phase_state.skill_injected` for `INTEGRATION` / `TEST` / `E2E` phases is now `null` because the prefix is unmapped (previously `null` because the injected base discipline was filtered out). Same recorded value, different mechanism — re-pinned by a new regression guard so the change is intentional, not silent.

  *Tests:* deleted `autodev/tests/test_skill_manager_base_skills.py` (9) and `autodev/tests/test_record_injected_skill_filters_base.py` (3) — they pinned the un-shipped mechanism. Reverted 5 tests in `test_skill_manager.py` to single-discipline expectations and added 3 negative regression guards (`test_base_disciplines_constant_absent`, `test_no_base_token_emitted_in_log_lines`, `test_deleted_disciplines_have_no_surviving_mapping`). New `autodev/tests/test_agents_md_universal_rules.py` (6) pins the universal rules' presence in each AGENTS.md so they can't silently regress. Full pipeline suite (762) and UI suite (1794) green.

  *Docs:* `CLAUDE.md` Skill Injection section and `autodev/docs/PIPELINE-SPEC.md` §Skills (overview, resolution algorithm, `skill_injected` schema) rewritten to single-discipline injection, with a historical note recording the layered model. `plans/Active/post-p0-ordered-roadmap.md` P1 Stage A status updated to SHIPPED + REFACTORED.

### Fixed

- **Activity feed: every already-emitted P0→P1 outcome now renders specific, correct prose (closes the observability audit's STALE + GENERIC-RENDER reporting gaps; remediation roadmap Phase 1).** Frontend render-only (`ui/index.html`) — the orchestrator already emitted all of these; the gap was purely how the feed displayed them. **Fixed (highest-severity, STALE):** the `abort_verify_failed` prose **and** its hover description said the pipeline *"halted to avoid stacking attempts"* — the **opposite** of the P1 soft-continue behavior (`_handle_stall_outcome` launches the next attempt anyway), so an operator read "stopped" while the run was still going. Both surfaces now state the soft-continue reality and the stale "halt"/"stacking" wording is deleted (not stubbed). **Added specific render cases** for signals that previously fell through to generic fallbacks: the four missing `escalation_resolve` commands — `RESET_EXECUTION` / `PROCEED` / `NUCLEAR_RESET` (reads as destructive) / **`SKIP` (risk-flagged with a "⚠ may cascade on dependent phases" cue** — `SKIP` is a removed "secret-menu" verb, so its *post-hoc log line only* must reinforce, not normalize, its cascade danger; this adds no control and does not touch the escalation command panel); the two contract-shape `reviewer_verdict` cases `BEHAVIORAL_UNVERIFIED` / `REGRESSION_UNVERIFIED` (mirroring the existing `VISUAL_UNVERIFIED` line, with the hover enumeration extended to match); a full four-map case (color + label + hover + prose) for **`escalation_command_invalid`** — a recoverable safety-heal that previously rendered as a dark-default badge + raw-JSON dump, now an amber "Invalid Command" with "defaulted to STOP (recoverable; resume to continue)" prose; and eleven missing `P3_LAST_ERROR_CODE_TITLES` (`ERR_PRD_VERBATIM_MISSING`, `ERR_REGRESSION_PRIOR_PHASE`, `ERR_REGRESSION_UNVERIFIED`, `ERR_BEHAVIORAL_UNVERIFIED`, `ERR_BEHAVIORAL_ARTIFACTS_MISSING`, `ERR_VISUAL_UNVERIFIED`, `ERR_MISSING_BASE_COMMIT`, `ERR_TDD_COVERAGE_MISMATCH`, `ERR_MANIFEST_FILE_MISSING`, `ERR_PATH_TRAVERSAL`, `ERR_STATUS_NOT_COMPLETE`) so a real gate failure shows its specific reason instead of the generic default title. **Tests (TDD, written first, 24 red→green):** new `tests/test_ui_observability_p1_render_fixes.py` pins map/case completeness for each surface, the `escalation_command_invalid` amber-family badge, the `SKIP` cascade cue, and a drift-guard asserting the `abort_verify_failed` surfaces no longer contain "halt"/"stacking" and do contain "soft-continue". The static-lint slices are anchored on the prose `humanizeSummary` switch (`case 'x': {`) because the badge-color switch shares the same `case` labels. Existing `test_ui_activity_feed_section6_events.py` / `_humanize.py` / `test_ui_activity_feed.py` (100) unaffected; JSX `text/babel` transpile clean (white-screen guard). **Scope:** no backend edits and no new event types (those are roadmap Phase 2); `CLAUDE.md`'s event catalogue is unchanged because `escalation_command_invalid` was already catalogued. **Callout (in-scope, actioned):** the `reviewer_verdict` hover enumeration was extended in the same pass so it does not stay stale. **Deferred (roadmap Phase 2/3):** emitting the still-SILENT lifecycle events (`nuclear_reset`, `queue_halted`/`parked`/`revived`) and persisting per-phase pain signals to the durable metrics row.

- **Ideas chat: the idea-switch staleness guard is now applied uniformly to every idea-scoped async resolve — and the dead notification-poll code is removed.** Follow-up to the heal-poll fix below: the same stale-closure bug (an in-flight resolve repainting the previous idea's data after the user switches) also lived in `startReadinessPoll`, `startRoadmapRecoverPoll`, the idea-load effect's own `/session` + `/readiness` resolves, and the one-shot `fetchPrdSectionDiff` / `refreshAnnotations`. Each now bails on `ideaId !== currentIdeaIdRef.current` (or `currentIdeaId !== …` inside the load effect) before its state write. These guards use a **plain `return`** — *not* the heal poll's `stopLateHealPoll(); return` — because `readinessPollRef` / `roadmapRecoverPollRef` are **re-armed by the newly-selected idea**, so a stale resolve calling `stopReadinessPoll()` would clear the *new* idea's poll (a real kill-the-new-poll hazard when switching between two ideas mid-assessment). The readiness poll's **global `refreshIdeas()`** (it updates the sidebar X/10 for *every* idea) is deliberately ordered **before** its guard, so a background assessment that finishes after the user has navigated away still refreshes the list ("see X/10 without switching ideas") — only the foreground readiness-strip writes (`setReadinessStatus`/`setReadinessData`) are idea-gated. The readiness poll keeps its existing unmount-only `[]` cleanup (its interval lifecycle is already managed by the idea-load effect); no `[currentIdeaId]` cleanup is added. **Removed (dead code):** `startNotificationPoll` had **zero call sites**, so `refreshMessages` (its only caller) never ran, `notificationPollRef` never held a live interval, and `stopNotificationPoll` (called once in the idea-load effect) was always a no-op — the whole vestigial island is deleted rather than guarded. **Tests (TDD, written first, 7 red→green):** new `tests/test_ui_ideas_poller_idea_switch_guards.py` pins the removal, the readiness/roadmap/load-effect/one-shot guards, the plain-return-no-self-stop contract, and the `[currentIdeaId]` readiness cleanup; the heal suite + bounded-fetch sibling stay green and a Babel transpile-check confirms the `text/babel` block still compiles. **Docs:** `currentIdeaIdRef` comment broadened; rationale comment added above `startReadinessPoll`. **Out of scope (callout):** the benign clear-to-default `.catch` handlers (`setPrdSectionDiff({})`, `setAnnotations([])`, the load-effect `/readiness` catch) are left unguarded — a stale reject only resets to empty and is self-corrected by the new idea's own load.

- **Ideas chat: heal-poll no longer flashes a stale idea's messages/PRD after an idea switch.** `IdeasScreen.startSessionHealPoll` runs a 5 s `setInterval` that fetches `GET /api/ideas/${ideaId}/session` with `ideaId` captured at call time. If the operator switched to a different idea while a poll was still in flight (up to `maxTicks: 192` ≈ 16 min on the abort/timeout recovery branch), `clearInterval` (via the `[currentIdeaId]` cleanup effect) could not cancel an *already in-flight* fetch's `.then`/`.catch` microtask, so the stale resolve ran `setMessages` / `setPrdContent` / `startReadinessPoll` / `onResolved` against the now-foreground idea — flashing the wrong idea's conversation and PRD into view. Latent before, but **widened by the signal-based-timeout change** (which now routes *every* chat timeout through this poll's ~16 min branch, not just an explicit 408). **Fix (`ui/index.html`):** mirror `currentIdeaId` into a new `currentIdeaIdRef` (kept in sync by a small `useEffect`, the established `isLoadingRef` ref-mirror pattern) and bail — `stopLateHealPoll()` + `return`, before any state write — at all three loop exits (interval tick, `.then` resolve, `.catch` reject) whenever the poll's captured `ideaId !== currentIdeaIdRef.current`. The guard lives only inside the helper, so the call-site `onResolved`/`onExhausted` callbacks are simply never invoked for a stale idea (no dual-source guard). The `[currentIdeaId]` cleanup effect is retained — it clears the interval immediately on switch; the guard covers the one thing it cannot (the in-flight fetch). **Tests (TDD, written first, 4 red→green):** new `tests/test_ui_ideas_heal_poll_idea_switch_guard.py` pins the ref declaration, the state→ref sync effect, the resolve-path guard ordered before `setMessages`, and ≥3 guards (tick + resolve + catch) with the catch path ahead of `fails += 1`; sibling `test_ui_ideas_bounded_fetch_contract.py` re-run unaffected (no new `setInterval`/`/session` fetch). **Docs:** block comment above `startSessionHealPoll`. **Out of scope (callout):** `startReadinessPoll`, `startRoadmapRecoverPoll`, and `startNotificationPoll`/`refreshMessages` share the same latent cross-idea staleness pattern (the latter two lack even a `[currentIdeaId]` cleanup effect) — deferred to keep this fix surgical.

- **Pipeline log rotation resolved against the wrong root → `orchestrator.log` never trimmed, grew unbounded.** `session_cleanup.rotate_pipeline_logs()` joined `heartbeat.log` and `orchestrator.log` against `OPENCLAW_ROOT` (`~/.openclaw`), but both pipeline logs are written under `AUTODEV_PIPELINE_ROOT` (the `.autodev` state dir — `heartbeat_cron.py`'s `LOG_FILE`, `ui/server._spawn_orchestrator`). The `os.path.getsize` check therefore ran against non-existent `~/.openclaw/*.log` paths and silently no-opped (guarded by `os.path.exists`), so the real logs were never rotated — an unbounded-growth / SD-card-exhaustion hazard on the Pi (`PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md` §1), and it matters more now that `.autodev/orchestrator.log` is the single canonical orchestrator audit log surfaced by the UI "Pipeline log" tab. **Fix (`autodev/pipeline/session_cleanup.py`):** import `resolve_pipeline_root`, derive `AUTODEV_PIPELINE_ROOT` via the same module-level pattern as `heartbeat_cron.py` (canonical env → `<repo>/.autodev`), and rotate both pipeline logs against it. `session_cleanup.log` deliberately stays under `OPENCLAW_ROOT` — it is this cron's own log, co-located with the OpenClaw session state it prunes. `heartbeat.log` stays in the rotation list: it has no in-repo writer (operator-provisioned cron stdout) but is a documented `missingok` rotation target (`PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md:20`, `PIPELINE-SPEC.md:1358`), and its natural home is beside `orchestrator.log`. **Tests (TDD, written first, all green):** new `autodev/tests/test_session_cleanup_log_rotation.py` (5) — both pipeline logs rotate when oversized under the pipeline root, a stray `orchestrator.log` under `OPENCLAW_ROOT` is left untouched (regression guard from the other direction), sub-5 MB logs are preserved (5 MB threshold unchanged), and the module exposes `AUTODEV_PIPELINE_ROOT` with `LOG_FILE` still under `OPENCLAW_ROOT`. Full pipeline suite 896 green. **Docs:** `rotate_pipeline_logs` docstring added (roots + rationale + `missingok`); `PIPELINE-CONSTRAINTS.md` §1 clarifies the in-process rotation root.

- **Ideas chat reply stranded on disk when `turns/{n}.done` never landed → "Working…" forever, even after a refresh.** Observed live (Balloon Popping Game, session-24): the agent wrote the reply (`turns/24.md`) but the `.done` sentinel never landed (interrupted run), and `turns/25.md` appeared too. The completion contract hinged entirely on `.done`, so the held POST waited the full 900 s backstop and `GET /session` left the placeholder `pending` indefinitely — the reply was invisible *even on refresh* (the reconciliation required `.done`). Fix (`ui/server.py`): new `_ideas_stranded_md_reply(idea_dir, turn, attempt_start, quiet_secs)` surfaces a fresh `turns/{n}.md` when `.done` is absent **and** the activity stamp has been silent for `quiet_secs` (the agent has demonstrably stopped, so the `.md` is final). The stamp-silence gate is essential — during a normal turn the agent writes the chat `.md` first, then runs a (possibly ~2 min silent) PRD-draft model call, then writes `.done` last; gating on stamp-silence means a stranded `.md` is only surfaced once the agent stops, so healthy long turns are never prematurely resolved. Wired into both `_poll_sentinel_with_idle_detect` (the stalled/timeout exits now return `succeeded` if a fresh reply `.md` is present) and `_reconcile_ideas_session_after_late_done` (now heals a `pending` placeholder, not just `error`, and accepts a stranded `.md` via the helper; `GET /session` passes `ideas_idle_threshold` as `quiet_secs`). Verified live: a `GET /session` on the stuck session-24 healed the placeholder from `pending` to the real "Number Blocker" reply. New tests: `tests/test_ideas_stranded_md_recovery.py` (9 — helper unit cases incl. the stamp-active negative, poll stalled→succeeded, reconcile pending→reply).

- **Ideas chat held one connection for the server's full 900 s patience → long replies hung / needed a refresh.** The chat send was a single blocking `fetch` with no client-side timeout, so after `poll_timeout` was raised to 900 s the browser held one HTTP connection for up to 15 minutes. The recovery poll that surfaces a reply without a refresh only started after an explicit 408/502/503 — which now doesn't fire until 900 s — so any long reply whose connection dropped or hung mid-wait left the spinner stuck until a manual refresh (the server had finished and written `session.json`, but nothing was watching). Fix (frontend, `ui/index.html`): the chat `fetch` is now bounded by an `AbortController` (`CLIENT_WAIT_MS = 120 s`); on abort **or** a network drop the handler keeps the "Working on your request…" bubble (no error, no draft-restore — the server is still working) and hands off to a recovery poll that watches `GET /api/ideas/{id}/session` until the turn's assistant message is non-pending (a real reply **or** the server-written reason-aware error), covering the full 900 s backstop (`maxTicks: 192`) and bailing fast (~30 s) if the server is actually unreachable. The reason-aware timeout messages still reach the user — the recovery poll reads the placeholder the server writes on its 408. **One shared `startSessionHealPoll` helper** now serves both the 408 late-heal (`resolveWhen` = no assistant error remains) and the abort/network recovery (`resolveWhen` = assistant no longer pending) — the previous inline late-heal `setInterval` was removed so there is a single session-poll loop (dual-source guard). Server `poll_timeout` stays 900 s (the server keeps working; the client just stops *holding* the line). New contract suite `tests/test_ui_ideas_bounded_fetch_contract.py` (5 tests) pins the AbortController, the abort→recovery-poll-without-error handoff, the non-pending resolve predicate, the ≥180-tick coverage, and the single-shared-loop invariant.

### Added

- **Reason-aware Ideas chat timeout messages.** The chat poll already computes *why* a turn failed (`PollResult.reason` ∈ `no_first_activity` / `stalled` / `timeout`), but every failure collapsed to one generic "the model may be slow" message. Now `POST /api/ideas/{id}/message`'s 408 surfaces the reason: **`no_first_activity`** → "The agent never started responding — OpenClaw may not have picked up the request. Check that the gateway is running, then retry." (points at infra, not the model); **`stalled`** → "The agent began working but went quiet partway through — the model likely stalled mid-response. Retrying usually clears it."; **`timeout`** → "The agent ran for ~N min without finishing — the request may be too large or the model very slow. …". New `ui/server.py` helper `_ideas_timeout_message(reason, poll_timeout)` is the **sole author** of the wording — the same string lands in the persisted session placeholder *and* the 408 body, and `ui/index.html` reads `detail.message` from the body and renders it verbatim (generic copy kept only as the body-unreadable fallback). This deliberate single-source design avoids duplicating per-reason wording across backend + frontend (the dual-source drift lesson). **Contract change:** the 408 `detail` is now a structured `{reason, message}` object instead of a flat string. Tests: new `tests/test_api_ideas_timeout_reason.py` (unit map + per-reason integration asserting 408 body and placeholder match), frontend contract assertions in `tests/test_ui_ideas_timeout_recovery_contract.py`, and `tests/test_api_ideas_message.py`'s deadline test updated to the structured-detail shape. **Scope note:** roadmap generation (`/convert`) keeps its separate 480 s wall-clock poller (no `PollResult`), so its 408 message is unchanged — bringing it to parity was explicitly deferred.

### Fixed

- **Ideas `poll_timeout` raise was a no-op in production + config-template drift.** The Round-3 entry below states `POLL_TIMEOUT` was raised 180 → 900 s, but that change was **ineffective in production**: `load_config()` does `DEFAULTS.copy()` then `.update(user_config)`, so `DEFAULTS["poll_timeout"]` (left at 180) always won over the `POLL_TIMEOUT` module-constant fallback. The effective hard backstop stayed 180 s — the live PRD turns that "validated" Round-3 all finished under 180 s, so the gap went unnoticed. Fixed by setting `DEFAULTS["poll_timeout"] = 900` (the value `load_config` actually serves); the constant is the fallback only for tests that patch it with a mock config omitting the key. Separately, **three config templates carried stale values** that would silently override the corrected defaults on a fresh clone: `ui/config.example.json` had `poll_timeout: 600` and `ideas_idle_threshold: 120` (→ 900 / 300 to match `DEFAULTS`), and the `.env.example` + `setup_helpers.py` (`ensure_dotenv_ideas_idle_hints`) comment said "Defaults if unset: idle 120" (→ 300). New guard `tests/test_config_defaults_consistency.py` (4 tests) asserts `POLL_TIMEOUT == DEFAULTS["poll_timeout"]`, that the effective `load_config()` backstop is ≥ 900, and that `config.example.json` tunables equal `DEFAULTS` — so the constant/DEFAULTS/template trio can never drift apart again. The UI server must be restarted to pick up the corrected default.

- **Ideas chat stall-signal cross-contamination — readiness now warms its own stamp.** Resolves the secondary finding flagged in the Round-3 timeout entry below. The background readiness assessment (`ideas:{id}:readiness`) is auto-fired fire-and-forget after **every** chat turn (`ui/server.py` `_trigger_readiness_assessment` → `asyncio.create_task` at the end of `post_ideas_message`), runs as the same `prd-creator` agent, and carries the same `ideaId` as the foreground chat turn. Because `extractIdeasIdFromSessionKey` yields the same id from both the chat-turn key (`ideas:{id}:session-{n}`) and the readiness key (`ideas:{id}:readiness`), both warmed the **same** `prd_creator_activity.stamp` — the single signal the chat-turn poller (`_poll_sentinel_with_idle_detect`) watches, and which is its SOLE consumer. Confirmed overlap path: nothing blocks the user from sending turn N+1 while readiness for turn N is still running, so an overlapping readiness run could keep the stamp fresh and mask a genuinely-stalled foreground turn — degrading detection from the 300 s stall threshold to the 900 s infra backstop. Severity is low (the backstop still fires; the window is bounded), so this is a scoped robustness fix, not an outage fix. **Fix (plugin-only):** new `isIdeasReadinessSession` + `ideasActivityStampFilename` selectors in `autodev/plugin/src/utils.ts` route the readiness session to a separate `prd_creator_readiness_activity.stamp`; `touchIdeasPrdCreatorActivityStamp` gained an optional `stampFilename` arg (defaults to the chat stamp), and both Ideas branches of `stall-detector.ts` (typed-hook `recordPipelineActivity` + agent-event `recordPipelineActivityFromAgentEvent`) pass the selected name. The chat stamp now means exactly "the foreground chat turn is alive". No Python/orchestrator change — the chat poll already watched the chat stamp; readiness simply stops touching it. **Out of scope (noted):** `clarity`/`convert`/`format-correction` sessions also touch the chat stamp, but they are user-initiated synchronous operations (not auto-fired after every turn), so their overlap probability is far lower; convert/format-correction additionally run as `roadmap-converter`, reaching the chat stamp only via the agent-event backstop. Revisit only if those bite. **Tests:** `stall-detector.test.ts` gains 4 (bare + production-prefixed readiness isolation, agent-event readiness isolation, chat-turn regression guard — 60 total, was 56); `test_ideas_stamp_detect.py` gains `test_overlapping_readiness_stamp_does_not_mask_chat_turn_stall` (consumer-side guard: a hot readiness stamp must not keep the chat poll alive). Plugin rebuilt via `npm run build`; the gitignored `dist/index.js` must be redeployed (`npm run deploy`, then restart the gateway) for the fix to take effect in production.

- **install.sh plugin deploy — fresh clones / re-runs now load current plugin code.** Three gaps surfaced during the Ideas-chat hotfix let a `git pull && ./install.sh` leave a STALE bundle loaded even though repo source was current: (1) `openclaw plugins install` lacked `--force`, so re-runs errored "plugin already exists" and kept the old bundle — now `--force`; (2) install.sh only *printed* "restart the gateway" but never did it, so the new bundle never loaded — now a guarded `systemctl --user restart openclaw-gateway` (degrades to a manual-restart hint on non-systemd hosts); (3) `openclaw plugins inspect` only proves hooks are *registered* (a stale bundle registers the same hooks), so install.sh now greps the **deployed** bundle (`$OPENCLAW_ROOT/extensions/autodev-pipeline-signals/dist/index.js`) for a current-source marker (the `agent:{role}:ideas:` matcher) and warns to re-run `npm run deploy` if missing. All in step 11; no behavior change to other steps. New static lints: `tests/test_install_sh_plugin_deploy.py` (5 tests) pin all three so the gaps can't silently return.

- **Ideas chat false-timeout — timeout thresholds too tight for thorough PRD drafts (Round 3).** After the stamp hotfix proved working (stamp advances reliably without `OPENCLAW_ROOT`), live MCP-browser validation still 408'd on PRD-draft turns. Root cause, proven by env-gated plugin instrumentation over a real turn: **OpenClaw delivers model calls opaquely** — `model_call_started` → *(silence)* → `model_call_ended`, with `lifecycle` firing only at session boot (not periodically) and token-streaming (`STREAM:assistant`) inconsistent. A *successful* PRD-draft turn contained a single model call with **118.3 s of total stamp silence**, against the old `ideas_idle_threshold` of 120 s — a 1.7 s margin from false-killing a healthy run. The old `poll_timeout` of 180 s likewise barely held a 146 s turn. **There is no finer progress signal available** for this provider path, so the only robust knob is the timeout itself. Fix (Ideas-only, no plugin/pipeline change): `POLL_TIMEOUT` 180 → **900 s** (full-turn infra backstop) and `DEFAULTS["ideas_idle_threshold"]` 120 → **300 s** (per-gap stall detector, matching the pipeline's stall philosophy); `ideas_startup_grace` stays 30 s (first `model_call_started` fires in <1 s). Tradeoff: a genuinely hung model now shows the spinner up to ~300 s before the 408 — acceptable given the 408 path preserves the draft and offers retry. Tests: `test_ideas_stamp_detect.py` gains `test_ideas_poll_defaults_accommodate_thorough_prd_drafts` (pins the values + the 118 s rationale) and `test_150s_silent_gap_stalls_under_old_default_but_survives_under_new` (proves a 150 s gap stalls at 120 s but survives at 300 s). **Secondary finding (now fixed — see the readiness-stamp entry above):** the `:readiness` background assessment session shared the idea's `prd_creator_activity.stamp`, so a readiness run could warm the same stamp as a foreground chat turn — a stall-signal cross-contamination edge. Readiness now writes its own `prd_creator_readiness_activity.stamp`.

- **Ideas chat false-timeout — env fallback + deploy gap (Round 2 of the session-key hotfix).** The prior session-key shape fix shipped to source but did not take effect in production, surfaced by the Cursor MCP-browser validation run on the Untitled Balloon Popping Game session. Two operational defects on top of the source-level fix:
